# Open Threads

**Volume 01, Batches 0001–0005, Chapters 1–50, are written, closed and audited.** Every promise below is marked **PAID** (a chapter settled it), **PART** (a chapter moved it and named it on the page), **CARRIED** (unchanged and still owed), **RULED** (an author-level decision the volume audit settled on 26 September 2026), or **OPEN BY DESIGN**. A thread is closed only when a written chapter pays it off. The Batch 0001 block is followed by the Batch 0002 block, then the Batch 0003 block, then the Batch 0004 block, then the Batch 0005 block and the audited volume-close accounting, then the standing volume-by-volume and character promises, which are unchanged.

**The *Author-level decisions deferred to a later outline phase* list below is RULED IN FULL. Every item is now a decision, not a deferral, and each carries its ruling and the date 26 September 2026.** The full reasoning is in `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md` Part 2 and the consolidated rulings are in `state/continuity.md` under *Rulings of the Volume 01 volume audit*. **Nothing in this file is waiting for a planning phase to decide.**

## Opening batch promises (Volume 01, Batch 0001, Chapters 1–10) — as paid

- **PAID — Ordinary work and cost (Chapters 1–3).** Chapter 1 opens on a paid pump repair that Calder refuses to patch, with a second crack he eats, a chandler's note eleven weeks into six, and a fouled chain that takes his shoulder. The mole haul, the anchor hook finding a real edge, and the fouled ground line give the ordinary work its whole weight before any glass is named.
- **PAID — Legitimate claim (Chapters 2–3).** The shard arises from a paid job; Calder cannot buy the claim and does not try to. He submits a labor log, an anchor position mark, and crew names, and Kest grants a **four-day disputed claim window** because Calder's work generated the recovery, not because Kest trusts him. **That window closed when the objects were surrendered.**
- **PAID — Jurisdiction on paper (Chapters 1–4).** Every dive in the batch is inside the harbor line under a named instrument: the claim window, and a separate **work permit** naming the inner edge of Gannet Shoal for **two dates** with a forty-mark bond. Kest says aloud in Chapter 3 that it does not reach the wreck's outer marks, the cutter's recorded mooring ground, or the keel line beneath the shoal. Chapter 4 pays that off: the marker line stops Calder a few fathoms short of the rib's outer end and he turns back. **The permit is spent.**
- **PAID — The first transparent hull (Chapters 3–4).** The pale run on the shoal bottom is established as a vessel-scale transparent hull with a continuous keel line and eleven keys, not a dropped panel. It is **not dated or attributed in Chapter 4**; its age and civic origin are Chapter 5's discovery and belong to the **plate** alone. The plate lifted in the same dive is transparent glass from that structure, not *Gannet* iron and not a *Gannet* artifact.
- **PAID — Two anchors, never fused (Chapters 1, 4, 8, 10).** The drifted mole anchor that held the shard is tagged and left bedded, marked with Kest's own float mark, and released back to the harbor authority for a proper lift. The freed shoal iron is a different piece, is ordinary, and stays on the Free Keel stack. Calder writes the distinction into the record in Chapter 4 (*anchor, shoal, port quarter of the Alder Reach — not the mole iron*) and both irons are named separately wherever either appears. **Neither is glass and neither was affected by the seizure.**
- **PAID — Two objects, two provenances (Chapters 5, 8, 9).** The **nine-line builder's mark is on the plate** (Chapter 5) and the **joinery match to a Crown yard case specification is on the shard's cut edges** (Chapter 9). The public provenance duplicate states both separately, and Chapter 9's second document is a separate, labelled, unverified account of the echo, kept physically apart from the physical findings. The shard is never declared pre-Dominion civic glass.
- **PAID — The two paid working days (Chapters 4–5).** One dive day and one bench day, the bench day being the permit's second date, spent building the cradle, the air limit, and the cutoff while two men were out slinging and the yard earned nothing. Both are on the record in Mara's book and in the crew's book.
- **PART — First echo cost (Chapters 6–10).** All six preconditions are spoken aloud in order, a named tender, an independent line, a fixed air limit, a seated physical anchor, a pullable cutoff, and a written reason. The costs are paid and are still active: rope-burn reenactment, tinnitus with a second bell a third of a second late, hearing confusion that survives into Chapter 10, and a line in the crew's book. **Still owed:** the two clean daylight looks before any further entry, and the twenty-four-hour minimum off the water.
- **PAID — Mara's independence (Chapters 2, 5, 8, 9).** She cannot enter an echo, certify truth, or become an assistant, and she does not. She produces the eastern rope district, refuses to certify a tidy provenance, refuses to read a builder's mark as consent, refuses to let the aperture be opened in a shed, holds the Registry's original custody log while Calder holds only a marked public copy, and writes her own stated reason for the entry. **Her refusal to certify has not yet cost her anything on the page; that cost is still owed and belongs to a later volume.**
- **PAID — Batch loss (Chapter 8).** Both glass objects leave the yard in one witnessed surrender and are entered separately in the Morrow circuit custody room. The crew keeps the freed shoal iron, the measurements, the repair-seam records, the scaled drawing, the anchor-bearing sheet, the names, the countersigned labor log, and the public provenance duplicate. **No glass stays in Calder's hands past Chapter 8, and the batch contains no loophole.**
- **CARRIED — The unaccounted plate note (Volume 4 pay-off).** The repairer's note about a plate cut from the *Gannet*'s own iron plating remains public in the copy of Alden's signed report, and Calder has now read it twice in front of his father. Alden gave one sentence in Chapter 9 — that he saw many things leave the pile in a storm in the dark and could not tell one plate from another — and no more. It is never fused with the transparent plate, and it still closes in Volume 4 with the removed file page.
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — First echo witness.** The *Vigil* rating who carried the case is still unnamed, unlocated, and uninterviewed, and the gloved hand with the caulking callus is still something the witness saw. Calder may believe it was Oren's and may not show it; the prose makes him choose not to write the heel-note down at all. See the deferred decisions list below.
- **PAID — No teacher for the entry (Volume 1 lock).** The six conditions were assembled in the yard out of Tobias Wren's secondhand memory of the Veyr cutters thirty-one years ago, Nessa's engineering, and Mara's paperwork, and the prose says so in Calder's and Nessa's own voices. No master arrived. The entry worked because the conditions were met.
- **PAID — The *Vigil* arrives as paperwork (Chapters 8–10).** A Registry seizure order, a filed naval salvage claim over the whole shoal, a cutter taking a berth at the naval pile, an officer's paper with a four hundred yard limit, and a tender setting floats on the bearing. There was no boarding, no gunfire, no physical seizure, no face-to-face meeting with Holt, and no vessel-to-vessel action of any kind.
- **CARRIED — The compartment measurement (Volume 1 midpoint pay-off).** Chapter 9 put the survey's ninth page, the public case specification, and a case length and breadth given as a **range** in the same room for the first time, laid two bearings over a public mooring line, and read the three-second gap out loud. The contradiction is now available to anyone holding four documents. **It is not proved, and no earlier chapter may treat it as proved.** The measurement at the site belongs to the Volume 1 midpoint, and the ground is under a naval claim.
- **PAID — Earned forward pull (Chapter 10).** The batch ends on **Calder's own spoken decision**, in front of Nessa, Bevin, Tobias, and Mara, after the cutter's order has arrived: he keeps investigating under naval scrutiny with no glass, no permit, no second entry, and a bell he cannot trust, gives Nessa the standing shutdown and Bevin the logbook job, and prefers to be slow and straight about it. The tender's line is the price in the scene; the last beat is his own pencil and his own sentence.
- **CARRIED — Route clue.** A broad west-southwest bearing only. Three independent bearings agreeing inside four degrees, the *Vigil*'s public mooring line, and Orison named once by Tobias at seventy fathoms down, which is the depth in `bible/world.md` and `bible/terminology.md`. **No map, no route, and no purpose.**

## Batch 0002 promises (Volume 01, Chapters 11–20) — as paid

- **PAID — Poor month economics (Ch11–13, 16, 18).** Ordinary jobs refuse a seizure crew; a technically good Free Keel strop-share encumbers the shoal iron; a Marr-name direction kills a week's scrubbing unless Calder attacks his father's report, which he refuses. One honest buoy job pays twenty-six in full and the crew eats, and the Saturday tide job on the 22nd pays twenty-four, which is what funds the ten printed copies and the bond. Air, rope, tallow, pump hire, wages, cradle, and the note stay on the page, and the mark-by-mark chain is locked in `state/continuity.md`.
- **PAID — Hearing room distinguished (Ch14, 19).** Claim window (spent recovery permission), work permit (named site and dates, spent), and one-season charter (wider seasonal permission, not held) are told apart without a glossary. The extension runs **from the 24th to the 14th of next month** — Kest's offer in the room was "twenty days further," and the term as entered is twenty-one — on twelve marks plus held gear and a harbor eighth, not ownership.
- **PAID — Shutdown exercised (Ch12).** Nessa calls "Enough" on a surge dive with the patch half seated; Calder obeys with air unspent. Hearing misplacement is operational and compensated by call-backs thereafter, including the number at the ladder whether or not it matches the diver's own count.
- **PAID — Refusals that stand (Ch13, 17).** Alden refuses work-witnessing for roof reasons (not the report), costing a job. Mara refuses provenance certification and a custody-room line and is right; both stand, and the hearing imposes the same limit on the bearing sheet without her asking. Kest in Ch14 bars the mole and gives pump packing in the same scene.
- **PAID — The family name in the hearing (Ch13, 19).** The harbor clerk uses it in Ch13 as a countersign refusal. The batch's hearing uses it as well: the naval officer asks whether the harbor authority will found an instrument on the figures of the yard that signed the *Aster Gannet* report, Calder refuses to defend his father's report, answers with Kest's own countersigned log, and the officer's note stands in the record as his question. **This is the volume's Movement 2 beat and it is spent. It may not be re-run.**
- **PAID — Mole anchor lifted by harbor (Ch14, 16).** Ordered Tuesday for Thursday's slack water; crane, chain, slip's crew, harbor cost, Kest's mark, watched from the fish dock rail because Calder was barred from the wall. Free Keel never touches it. Drifted iron is now municipal and up.
- **PAID — Public record priced (Ch18–19).** Ten crew-only copies printed for twelve marks out of Saturday's wage without Mara's name; the bond tin deliberately not opened for paper; bearing sheet and plate drawing withheld; duplicate stays public by ruling. Consent's first form stated: a record serves working hands.
- **CARRIED — Defense to the 14th, bond gear, eighth share, iron third, Venn forty, slip tides, spring cradle, ungloved arm, empty tin.** All survive into Ch21+ as debts.
- **CARRIED — Compartment, nine-line mark, Oren mark, delayed note, custody numbers.** None resolved; all restated under existing locks.

## Batch 0003 promises (Volume 01, Chapters 21–30) — as paid

- **PAID — Ordered chain, at a distance (Ch21, Ch23, Ch28).** The wreck chain is shown to be **ordered rather than scattered**, and it is shown **without touching it**: three points on one bearing, first to second a mile and two hundred yards and second to third a mile and two hundred and ten yards, a difference of ten yards, a foot and an inch of bottom in one figure out of thirty-six on two miles of a course the harbor authority itself sounded. Order shown; **purpose withheld — no chapter in the batch says the run was laid, and the word *placed* survives only inside the hearing record.** No map, no route, no line drawn between the points by the reeve's ruling. **This answers the volume's new-volume question in its first half and leaves the second half — who controls the missing links — untouched.** *(Batch 0003's review flagged one deliberate deviation from the card here: `outline/volume-01.md` Movement 3 says the vessels are **positioned**, not scattered. The chapters stop at order. Recorded in `state/continuity.md` under proposed canon changes for the volume auditor, not resolved in prose.)*
- **PAID — A fourth document and a second witness (Ch22).** The harbor authority's own 13-year-old wreck entry for the *Aster Gannet* records *number four, port side aft, grating in place, not cleared, contents not removed*, which is custody language in a harbor's hand and which explains the silence: the space was never emptied on any record in this harbor, so it was never measured. Kest is the second witness, from his own memory and his own search. **Adds a reason; proves nothing; the compartment is still unproved and the measurement remains the Volume 1 midpoint's.**
- **PAID — Training the tender (Ch23, Ch25).** Bevin Rook is the bottom man under Nessa; Calder tends with bad ears; the call-back is now a written rule that the tender's count goes in and the diver's own count goes in only if he says it aloud; and when Calder's head turns under load he is *told what the bell was* rather than left to find out. The line-diver, the pump, the fall and the board are reassigned in writing.
- **PAID — The bad descent, and its price (Ch24, Ch25).** A southerly, a borrowed boat, a shackle under the authority's own foundation stone, a chain that comes tight, and a left hand crushed in a bight. Un-recuperable on the page: two fingers, six weeks of a boy's wages, twenty-four marks the yard does not have, the Free Keel's inside-the-line rescue cover withdrawn for the winter by the crew that gave it, a stove in somebody else's boat, and a harbor that paid eighteen of twenty-six. **The crew is poorer and the tender is gone.**
- **PAID — Contagion used against its author (Ch26, Ch27).** The circuit office quotes Mara Venn's handling note of the 14th word for word, draws four clauses from it, and argues in its fourth clause that a line of joined construction is not a scatter. Her words are used **correctly and about a different subject** — the note is about a join, the notice applies it to a position — and the purpose it quotes has been stripped of the sentence in which the yard said **it had no measurement of the assertion it was making**, which is not the purpose's closing sentence but the one the whole of Chapter 22 turns on. She answers with a second note that is correct, narrow, refuses to say *safe*, refuses to say *unsafe*, and carries the **date of the 22nd, eight days after the defense lapses**. **The contagion argument is lodged and the public panic Volume 9 will later exploit has a real paper behind it.**
- **PAID — The deeper panel foregone (Ch29).** A plate with a Crown-shop bolted rim lies in eleven feet at the mole's fifth seam, bedded in the harbor's own foundation stone, **seen at the top of the tide** on the 3rd by the reeve and three witnesses and entered in the reeve's own book — **three named witnesses, four men who saw it** — as *seen, not measured, not touched*. The lawful instrument is written in six clauses and is **void on its face** because the only countersign available comes from the office that has just called the ground dangerous. The unlawful way is available that night and is refused in writing, in front of the crew, with three reasons, the second of which is that a figure taken alone in the dark makes every other figure in the record uncheckable. **The panel is not lost and does not return; it is there in March, and the yard has written down that it knows where it is and has not gone.**
- **PAID — Public evidence kept, private dive forgone (Ch29, Ch30).** Calder struck the word *placed* out of his own copy of a filed purpose and left the hearing record uncorrected, so that both dates are legible; he put three points and a sighting on a public wall with no line drawn between them; and he asked for his bottom back at a hearing **without asking for a date or a place**. **The character change is on the page and the foregone panel stays foregone.**
- **PAID — The delayed note, unresolved, heard five times and logged three (Ch21, Ch23, Ch24, Ch28, Ch30).** Physical sound, near legal water, ambiguous, logged with hour and water state by a boy who cannot hold the pencil steady, and **never resolved**. It was logged on the 26th, the 2nd and the 4th. It went unentered twice: the mole's chain alarm on the 28th, when both of Calder's hands were on the cleat and Nessa told him what the bell was, and the top of the make on the 29th, when he had no book at all. That second one is the batch's quietest cost.
- **CARRIED — Everything that costs.** The defense to the 14th, the hearing on the 7th, the bond gear in pawn, the harbor eighth, the note at eleven weeks into six with nothing paid this month, the air pot a third gone, five marks in the bag, the *Kittiwake* on the hard, the shoal iron's third to Sella Brigg, the forty marks owed to Mara, the 400-yard bar, the notice of the 31st, the reeve's chart claim, and the ten sealed terms.

## Batch 0004 promises (Volume 01, Chapters 31–40) — as paid

- **PAID — The same shoal, and the geography withdrawn to get there (Ch31, Ch33).** Movement 4 asks for the *Vigil's* claim and the seized material on the same ground by physical measurement and the provenance duplicate and not by the echo. **The yard's own attempt at that is withdrawn in public**: Term Three strikes the anchor-bearing sheet from every sitting of the harbor authority at the yard's own request, with a slip on it signed by both men, and the filed purpose of the twenty-seventh is left standing in the hearing record with *placed* in it and no correction asked for. What replaces it is ordinary municipal iron and a rope: the authority's own board tag on its own ground tackle, the reeve's rag float with the chalk seam number, the eight-year-old unclaimed-wreck list naming the *Alder Reach* with her ground tackle gone in a westerly, the fouled line's eastern make, and **a saw kerf in a crown shackle that was loaded again.** The plate is on claimed ground and the permit that lifted it is two days older than the claim. **The shard is not on the claimed ground and never was, and Calder says so in a room before anybody asks.** The compartment is still unproved and nothing in this batch touches it.
- **PAID — The Crown's claim, entered and answered (Ch32).** The naval salvage claim goes into the hearing record on the eighth, twenty-four days after it was lodged, in its own four sheets, with the captain's signature line read aloud. The yard objects to **three words** — *continuous*, *including*, *as it was worked* — and Kest enters the claim as filed and the objection over his own signature on the same page. The yard **concedes in the room that its third point came out of the authority's own book**, refuses the vessel's demand for its ten printed copies, and gets a new consent form in exchange: shown on a hearing-room table, one at a time, in daylight, with the clerk, and never carried off it. The *Vigil's* demand for the sheet the mooring line was laid down on in thirteen years ago is **requested** in the officer's notebook and unanswered.
- **PAID — Kest's position, and a price with somebody else's name on it (Ch34, Ch40).** The *Vigil* moves to strike the reeve's fifth-seam sighting as the fruit of his own breach of his own notice. **Kest refuses and enters the refusal with the breach in the same book**, because the only way to keep the sighting is to keep the notice standing whole and unnarrowed, and that keeps the harbor authority's own moorings, fenders and herring berths out of service with no end date. **Elias Voke of the herring boat *Anna Ray* is named on the page**: nineteen winters of moorage dues, two berths and a warp, the north wall, forty boats in on the first of March in a northeast blow, a hard at Ashtide applied for and not answered, and a disputed claim entered in a hearing room over the clerk's objection, on the reeve's own motion, with a promise to say in the same room in a fortnight whether the coast office has answered. **The cost of the fence lands on the herring fleet and not on the yard and not on the reeve.** It carries into Volume 2, where the coast is poor and busy and the first of March is real.
- **PAID — Mara's method, and the note in her file (Ch35, Ch40).** The circuit office asks, under **Magister Ilya Serr's seal line** and nothing else, for two certifications: of the ground and of the yard's record. **Calder asks her to sign before he has thought about it, she refuses him in front of him, and she refuses to let him withdraw the asking.** She refuses in writing on four grounds, of which the first is that she cannot certify a bedded seam as a position, and the fourth is that the office will not assist in reading its own notice as a measure about ground. **The refusal is a note in her file and does not close the query of the twenty-first of last month, and her remaining quarter of standing is spent.** She is not thanked and does not want to be. She is not the crew's witness, advocate or employee, and Calder may not ask her to be.
- **PAID — The plan, and the answer from the office that wrote the notice (Ch36).** A lawful six-clause plan is written with its reason first, lodged at the circuit office, and **returned uncountersigned the same afternoon on four grounds, all of them correct.** The first: the office will not countersign an instrument putting a person inside the closure its own notice created, and a countersignature on application makes a notice a first refusal. **The fifth-seam plate is unreachable by anybody and stays where it is.** Kest's own reason for not asking is entered in the hearing room on the seventh: a reeve's request is a pressure and a pressure may be refused without a reason. The bottom the yard won on the seventh is worth nothing and the yard knows it.
- **PAID — A hand's cost, and the crew reorganized around it (Ch37, Ch40).** **Bevin Rook asks for his wages, for the first time in five weeks, and asks for the claim rather than the money.** Sixteen marks for the reading of the book from the fourteenth of October and twenty-four for the hand from the twenty-ninth, **forty in all, and the yard has five.** The reading is ruled **a trade and not a wage until the tenth of December**, and it stops on that date whether or not there is work, and the reorganization is honestly conditional: if the yard cannot pay a reader the yard has no reader. **The mother is told in a hearing room, out loud, by a man in a coat, as she asked on the thirtieth of October.** His return to the water is fixed at the tenth of December and entered in a record. **April is not paid off and must not be.**
- **PAID — The family beat, once, and the mark still held (Ch38).** Calder writes the reason the eighth term requires, **two hundred and forty words with the brother left out of them**, and takes the slate to his father's bench and says nothing, which is what their household has done for thirty years. **Alden Marr warms and does not confess**: *that is well written*, *it is thin at the end*, one sentence about a signature in a report and a room with a door shut in four years, and then he works at the near end of the same bench for two hours without speaking. **No fragment, no report talk, no second story.** At half past ten Calder tells Mara Venn what the mark is, in about nine seconds, and she refuses to write one word of it anywhere ever, refuses to let him take the asking back, and tells him what the hiding has cost. **The concealment's cost is now a different kind of thing from the last time**: not one false line on a private slate, but **a fourth *not stated* in a municipal hearing record that goes to Veyr, and a delayed note in a naval file that the yard's own book contradicts and cannot explain.** The mark is still hidden from every office, from Kest, and from his father, at the end of the batch. *(Batch 0004's review flagged one deliberate deviation from the card here: `workspace/volume-01/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` hard rule 12 and its required character changes asked Calder to **hide the Oren-specific mark again**, and Chapter 38 has him tell Mara Venn instead, in about nine seconds. `outline/volume-01.md` asks for the concealment to hold at the **volume boundary**, and Chapter 38 is not the boundary, so the prose is defensible — but it reverses a card instruction outright, and the volume auditor must rule on it. Recorded in `state/continuity.md`'s proposed-canon list and in the deferred-decisions list below, item 9. Not resolved in prose.)*
- **PAID — The three points under attack, and a false precision withdrawn (Ch39, Ch40).** The *Vigil's* officer attacks the even interval with the correct argument: a difference equal to the tolerance of one of the two numbers is an agreement and not a measurement. **Nessa Pike then finds the half he did not know**: the first point is not a point at all but a bedded seam of stated length, and the yard has been using its south end, and forty feet of ambiguity is thirteen yards against a published tolerance of ten. **Bevin Rook gives his own count — seven and eleven against the tender's nine and ten and a half — the figure he declined to enter on the twenty-eighth because it could not be checked, and enters it now because it is the only check there is.** The yard withdraws **ten yards** in the room, in the book, and off the wall of figures, and says plainly that it does not know whether the three places are on a straight within anything better than the length of a seam, and the load-bearing fact is the office's own one-in-thirty-six. **The naval officer records that a party has produced the figure that damages itself, unasked. One version of this lock, and it is the only one: the withdrawal is on a wall — a fresh market-wall sheet in Calder's own hand, *the difference is withdrawn by the yard. See the sheet inside, which says why* — and what is on no record and on no wall is the difference stated as a measurement.** *(The Batch 0005 review found this stated three incompatible ways across the state files; the sentence above is the single version, and Chapter 41's purpose now carries it.)*
- **PAID — The fourteenth, and what the defense did (Ch40).** **The defense of the twenty-fourth of last month is discharged, not extended and not renewed**, on four grounds, and the bottom restoration of the seventh goes back to the reeve from the fifteenth. The yard gives up its claim to the shoal ground, its claim to any share of the first recovery, the ten yards, and the exclusive use of ten pieces of paper. It keeps the labor chain found and not disturbed, the third clause heard and not decided, the notice received as correct, the sighting received as seen and not measured, the ruling that no line may be drawn, and a reeve who raises **of his own motion the question of a one-season provisional working charter**, with a written purpose due **on the twenty-first**, read into the record with the door shut **on the twenty-second**, and a sitting on **the twenty-eighth** that anybody may object to. **Term Ten is answered in the room on the seventh, in the words of the term, in public, before anybody can ask.** **The charter is not granted and the defense is not renewed; Volume 1's resolution is Chapters 41–50.**

## Batch 0005 promises (Volume 01, Chapters 41–50) — as paid. **Volume 01 closes in Chapter 50.**

- **PAID — The purpose and the twenty-second (Ch41, Ch42).** A six-page purpose with **nine lines asking for nothing**, filed on the 21st, read into the record with the door shut on the 22nd with the clerk out of the barge, and **the conservator's second note dated the same day in the same week, and the two documents never learning about each other.** The purpose's real defect is found at the bench by the boy who reads the book: *it never says where the crew means to work.* Kest enters that in the margin of his own book, which the clerk does not copy, and tells Calder he has done it and is not proud of it. **The concealment's cost is now on the page in a third form: a purpose that is silent about its own ground, in a document going to Veyr, filed by the man who was told on the 13th that silence has a shape.**
- **PAID — The Navy's paper, and the book (Ch43).** The *Vigil* answers the charter question inside its month with three terms. One is **granted for free** because it is already true. Three is granted: a copy of the hearing record and a count of the ten printed crew copies with their places. **Two — the crew's book, produced on request and copied — is refused**, in four sentences on the market wall with the date and the hour on the back. **Nessa Pike's fifth overrule of the campaign, and it wins: a total refusal reads in a room as a crew with something, and four concessions make the fifth refusal believable to a man who has never met them. Her cost is the count**, signed in her own hand, of ten pieces of paper and where every one of them is, which is the thing she told a room in November a man with a notebook could bend. Bevin Rook adds the sixth line, *not included: any paper of any other crew*, and asks for it first.
- **PAID — How a man gets a steel on that ground (Ch44).** The answer to a question carried since the 27th of October is a **form from a man in a barge**: the mole was carried out fifty yards to the south in the year of the Cut and the authority's own surveyor's hand wrote *the foreshore at the south end being mixed, containing iron and timber*, and **the mole was built on the ferry.** The notice of the 31st closes the third to the eleventh seam and does not reach the twelfth, the dump, or water, and a claim window for one date with a hand lead costs nothing. **The batch's reversal, written as the discovery of a form and not as a climax.** The complication is that a *hand* on the plate needs a work permit, a bond, and the lifting of the reeve's own undertaking — and the yard's purpose asks for none of those. **Kest writes a Sunday entry putting the yard's own incompleteness into his own book before he opens it on the Monday, and Calder files a supplement at ten past ten on the morning of the sitting admitting, of his own motion, that a purpose filed on a Monday and a request for the bottom on the Saturday six days later is a thing a room ought to ask about. The rehearsal for the volume's climax is a man correcting himself in public before anybody asks.**
- **PAID — The charter, the share, and the price (Ch45).** **A one-season provisional working charter is granted in the harbor authority's hearing room on Monday the 28th of November**, on a purpose filed on the 21st and a supplement filed at ten past ten on the morning, and it is a **wider seasonal permission to the last day of March next**, inside the harbor line and bounded by the outer marks, and **it is expressly not the Second Bell license and does not lead to one.** **The harbor's eighth and the harbor's share of any raising follow the yard in and were not argued; the crew's share is retained at the figure the crews had already agreed; the bond is the freed shoal iron at a clerk's thirteen to the crew with a third entered to the *Low Lantern*.** The accounts are read out in the room and every figure in them is unpaid. **Kest pays for it in other people's berths and says so himself:** his own chart claim narrows from the third-to-eleventh seams to three seams about the fifth, which returns the timber fenders, three of the four sets of moorings and one herring berth on the first of December, and leaves the ninth seam's mooring out because a fouled shackle bedded in stone is not a seam and a notice he did not write holds the man who would work it, and leaves the north berth out, and pays **nothing** of the nineteen winters. Neale is in the room and is answered in a reeve's own voice. **Kest's fortnight's promise to Elias Voke is paid on the day he promised**, with the coast office's word *capacity*, and Voke offers to write the authority's capacity statement himself and put the north berth, the ninth seam and the boy's name in it. **The reeve's undertaking of the 25th of October is lifted for the season on named dates** with a stated reason, a tender who is not the diver, a cutoff and a log, and **the tenth of December is put in the schedule with Bevin Rook as the diver on it**, and the *Vigil*'s tender moves to a cable's length and a second cutter's boat takes a berth at the pile.
- **PAID — The compartment, as a reversal and not a climax (Ch44, Ch47).** The **plate of the *Aster Gannet's* own iron in nine and a quarter feet off the south end**, a coaming four foot by three foot outside and **three foot nine by two foot four clear inside, measured with a rule in the hand**, a grating out of its seat and in its seat with the rebate worn bright inboard only, and a **dressed aperture of a hand's breadth and a half with fourteen holes at a template's spacing, fouled continuously inside and out, giving no hour, no man and no direction.** The finding is the honest mechanical form of the volume's midpoint, and **it now runs in three steps, because the Batch 0005 review found the two-step version was a non-sequitur**: the space lies under a grating in a deck with no other opening, so the hatch is the only way anything has ever gone into it or come out of it; nothing of the case's seven-to-eight-foot described size could pass three foot nine by two foot four either way; and this office's own thirteen-year-old wreck file gives the space as *grating in place, not cleared, contents not removed*. **So the case cannot have gone in that way, nothing of that size can be taken out that way, a space nothing can be taken out of has not been cleared, and a harbor has been saying so in writing for thirteen years. So a paper saying the hold stood empty at that moment is not describing the space at that moment.** The yard wanted to prove the room was occupied and has proved the room cannot have been cleared, which is worth more because it is a figure. **What was in the space is still unknown and is not asked for, and the compartment is not proved occupied by anybody, and no Volume 2 chapter may say it was.** *(A deliberate reading of `outline/volume-01.md`'s midpoint, flagged in `state/continuity.md`'s proposed-canon list, item 12.)*
- **PAID — The bell-buoy core, as a working thing (Ch46, Ch50).** **A usable bellwright's core is recovered in forty-one minutes on the 6th of December**, iron with a clapper and a tongue half worn through and four pieces of worked glass leaded in a band in the waist, and **it answers this harbor's own tide bell through the ground a hand's width of a second late, in Calder's hand, in front of four people, and Bevin Rook writes a line with two things in it for the first time in four months.** The delayed note of Chapters 1–40 now has a cause and the cause is a bell: every logged occasion in the volume was an iron in the harbor being struck, and nobody had ever struck anything. It is a **supply problem, a maintenance problem and a person going out in a boat** — fouling is not the enemy, a wet gudgeon writes the bell off, a new tongue is a day's work every four or five years, and a bell that is not looked after stops being a signal and starts being a rock. **It cannot be worked in a shed, because the second clause of the notice of the 31st keeps recovered transparent mineral out of every shed in this harbor, so it is hung or it rusts.** It is hung on the 25th on harbor gear with a chart notation in the reeve's own hand that names the cost. **It is not an instrument, it reads nothing, and it says no word.** *(The craft of it is flagged in `state/continuity.md`'s proposed-canon list, item 10.)*
- **PAID — The fragment, and the public route (Ch47, Ch48, Ch49).** **A fragment holding Oren's first minutes of the seizure is recovered on the 8th of December** from the plate's outboard edge, in two keyed portions, with a ground band along its lower cut face that the yard enters as a form and refuses to compare with the object in the custody room. It is entered under the charter, on the Free Keel platform, with all six conditions read aloud, a **stated reason in the harbor authority's book at eight in the morning unedited**, a forty-minute limit, and the cutoff. **The anchor is the *Gannet's* own stern board and not Oren's brass bell, because the bell is his brother's and will not go into a public record, and because the ship was in the room and is not a person.** The scene is one witness's limited frame: a case on the shoulder, **the man with the paper behind him and the paper upside down**, a phrase read twice with the word *empty* in it, four men and the count right, a tag with the third figure lost, a hand on the collar, and **the boy looks at the man and not at the case** — a choice he dislikes and cannot argue with. **It does not speak a command, apologize, forgive, or explain anything, and the evacuation bell and the second rescue line are not in it.** It is entered in the circuit custody room on the 13th as the **third** item under a third number, under the same seal line, with a **municipal claim entered for the purpose of a record and not of possession, which cannot be withdrawn without a request, and the yard will not make one.** **The *Vigil*'s attention lands: the tender at a cable's length, a second cutter's boat at the pile, a request for the harbor authority's own chart refused in eleven seconds by a method the reeve has now used on himself, and a paper that re-enters three items to *contested* and gives no reason.**
- **PAID — The cost of the public route (Ch47, Ch48).** **Calder chooses the public route for the fragment and pays for it in a specific childhood memory of Oren's voice, in the body and not in a speech.** On the morning of the 9th of December he can remember the call, its length, its three marks, its flick, and the place in the middle where a boy's breath went, **and the lift at the end of it is not there and there is nothing to remember.** It is not muffled and not forgotten; it is absent, and no person in Morrow Quay can restore it and none will be asked. **Nessa Pike names the price in one sentence — a boy on a deck in a public record with no name on him is the only way anybody will ever know he was there and also the only reason nobody will ever know which boy — and Calder takes it knowingly**, because the private alternative was refused in his own hand on the 3rd of November with three reasons. **The concealment survives the volume:** the mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation; Mara Venn wrote none of it on the 13th of November and writes none of it on the 26th of December; Bevin Rook heard something come out of a man's mouth and wrote *not written as a voice* and said aloud in the yard that he will not write it; **and the one false line on the unverified note is left false, because two words would make it true and a true slate with a boy's name on it is a document in a yard any man may read.**
- **PAID — The record (Ch49).** A public sheet in three parts in four hands goes on the market wall under the cutter's claim **with the limits written inside the rules**, showing the cutter's claim and the plate on the same ground and the manifest unreliable as a description of that space — **as records, with their limits, and with no office having ruled on any part of it.** There is no money to print it and six copies go out to the herring skippers, the net loft and the *Low Lantern* instead, which is the consent question's second form: a record serves the people who work the ground. **Mara Venn certifies the method for nothing and has the yard write *nothing* on the back of it in Calder's own hand.** Kest reads the custody entry of the 20th out in the hearing room and says the fourth of his four things as a fact about a file: **the condition in the order of the 14th of October was satisfied on the 13th of December, the order has not been lifted, no paper anywhere states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material, and the *Vigil* is the second office in this harbor that has never been told.**
- **PAID — Bevin Rook in the water on the tenth of December (Ch48).** **He is in the water at nineteen minutes with his hand in his pocket on a cradle in eight feet, tendered by Calder Marr, with Nessa Pike on the fall, and she says *Enough* and he comes up angry and she does not apologize and does not look away.** The reading of the book stops on the tenth and the wages begin at four a week and are not paid; the whole of the forty marks stands. **She takes the word *wages* out of the yard's book on Wednesday the fourteenth of December and puts *claim* in its place, in her own hand, because he asked and not because anybody told her to.** The crew's work is rebuilt around a boy who is in the water and is owed money, and the reeve put the boy's own date into the charter's schedule because it was in a hearing record.
- **PAID — Alden (Ch41).** **He falls silent.** He was warm on the 13th of November and was not in the yard from the Saturday the 19th, and sent word that it is the cough and that he is not to be disturbed and not to be sent for, and is entered in the crew's book as *not chased, no reason given and none asked for*. **Calder does not go**, and that not-going is the change. He is at the far bench in the same shed, has not spoken to anybody since the 13th of November, and is not asked for anything. **No confession, no fragment, no report talk, nothing about the ferry, and the plate note untouched.** The Movement 2 family beat stays paid and is not re-run.

## Volume 01 close accounting

**AUDITED 26 September 2026. This supersedes the Batch 0005 account of itself, which appears above it in this file and in `state/current.md`. The audit is `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md`; the consolidated rulings are in `state/continuity.md` under *Rulings of the Volume 01 volume audit*.**

**What Volume 01 answered, on the page.** The provenance of the first recovery is a chain of labor no office has disturbed. The *Vigil*'s claim and the recovered plate rest on the same ground, the plate was lifted two days before the claim was lodged, and **no forum has ruled on either**. The public manifest is **unreliable as a description of standing space number four, in three steps and in that order**: the clear opening is three foot nine by two foot four, the space is eight foot four by six foot nine clear and eleven feet two deep under the grating, the case is seven to eight feet in its longest face, **the hatch is the only opening**, so the space cannot have been cleared; **and this office's own thirteen-year-old wreck file, in a harbor's hand, says *grating in place, not cleared, contents not removed*.** A **working bell-buoy core** is recovered, understood as a signal and not a relic, and hung on harbor ground, and it answers the harbor's own tide bell through the ground **a hand's width of a second late** — the same second as the sixth of December, said aloud by the boy with the book, and the same signal the Volume 15 finale pays as a working route signal. A **fragment holding Oren's first minutes of the seizure** is surfaced **through a public route** at the cost of a specific childhood memory of his voice, and is in Registry custody under a claim the yard cannot withdraw. The **one-season provisional working charter** is granted, the **salvage share is kept**, the harbor's eighth and share follow the yard in, the reeve's undertaking is lifted on named dates, and **it is not the Second Bell license and does not lead to one**. Bevin Rook is in the water on the tenth of December under a tender who is not him. **Ilya Serr's name and senior Registry examiner's seal stand on a seal line and never in a room, and the reason for the seizure of the 14th of October is still undisclosed.** **Calder still hides the Oren mark at the volume boundary**, and the concealment is a false line he has declined to correct, a boy with a book who will not write what came out of a man's mouth and was not asked, and a woman who would rather be the only person who knows what is in that anchor and has never been asked.

**What Volume 01 opened.** Why the transparent vessels lie in a deliberate route toward Orison and **who holds the missing links** — and the second half of that question, which the first half of the volume did not reach, is opened by Sera Mott at a mole head on the 21st of December: the vessels are in a route, this one answers from the westward and always late, the ones that answered from the westward were kept by men who went out in boats, and **the ones she knows about stopped because the men who kept them stopped and the men stopped because there was nobody on the far banks.** That is a road with nobody on it and not a mystery, and the seaward end of the run is under a naval claim with a tender at a cable's length, and nobody has ever asked the cutter what the seaward end is for. Also open: why a presumption of Crown material expired in December and nothing arrived; who cut a hole in a ferry's shell from the outside and when; what the nine-line mark means and no office in Veyr can say; and whether a route whose maintenance stopped can be restarted by people who have never agreed to keep it.

**Owed to Volume 02, by name, and now planned.** `outline/volume-02.md` carries all of it as locks and `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` places it across Chapters 51–60. **The nineteenth of January**, a Thursday, at the mole's toe, with a lead and a bell and no diver unless a reason is in the reeve's book at eight in the morning — because the run does not stop at the mole or the shoal, it is under the town, and the harbor authority has no chart of what is under the town because the town was built on it. **Elias Voke's statement of capacity**, the hard at Ashtide, and **the first of March with one herring berth short and the ninth seam's mooring out of service** — a Wednesday, nine weeks and two days off at the volume's close. **The circuit office's next request, worded so that a refusal is a delay**, which is the instrument of Mara Venn's Volume 4 break and which is owed to Volumes 2–4. **The forty marks, twice**: Bevin Rook's claim and Mara Venn's certificate, both unpaid, **on a bag that has held five marks since the first of December and has had nothing put into it since the eighteenth of November.** **Nineteen winters of dues**, disputed and unpaid. **A charter that can be suspended after an inspection**, with a reeve who says out loud that he has no cause and will not suspend it. **A notice of the 31st that cannot be lifted in Morrow Quay**, whose third clause catches three named people from earning a living inside their own harbor line. **Two proper plans for the fifth seam**, owed to Volumes 2 and 3, and the mechanism is a change of politics in an office and not a better argument from a shed. **A coast that is poor and busy and short of a hard.** And a bell with a fouled waist and a tongue half worn through, hung on the town's own ground, that somebody has to go out and look after.

**What the volume audit ruled, and what it repaired.** Eight deferred author-level decisions are now **RULED**, dated 26 September 2026, in the list below and in `state/continuity.md`. **Two mechanical repairs were made in place in the chapters** — Chapter 39's recalled words at *ten feet* are now **nine feet**, and Chapter 48's interval to the first of March at *eleven weeks and a day* is now **eleven weeks and four days**. **Three structural findings are recorded rather than repaired**, each with the cheapest honest repair named: two chapters that are two rooms, five sub-floor chapters in Batch 0002, and one state-file figure with no chapter behind it. **The "worse on length than Batch 0004" claim is withdrawn.** **The six bible files are unchanged**, because the bible already permitted the bellwright's core and no amendment is required. **Nothing in this file is waiting for a planning phase.**

## Threads opened by Batch 0005 and now owed to Volume 02

**Audited 26 September 2026. Each of the nine is marked still owed, carried or closed here, and each is now placed in `outline/volume-02.md` and, where it falls in Movement 1, in `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md`.**

1. **The nineteenth of January at the mole's toe. — STILL OWED. CARRIED, and now the batch's closing chapter.** Named by the reeve after Calder asks, not before (Ch 50). A lead, a slate, a bell, and no diver unless a reason is entered in the harbor authority's book at eight in the morning with Pike on the cleat. The purpose is to trace the near end of the run **inland, under the town**, and there is no instrument in Morrow Quay or Veyr that will tell Calder what a route is and no forum that will rule on one. **Closes in Volume 2, Movement 1 — Chapter 60, Thursday the nineteenth of January, at the top of the flood** — and it is the first thing the leash is used for.
2. **The custody-log entry of the 20th of December. — STILL OWED, and it is the Crown's next move.** Three items re-entered to *municipal claim entered, contested*, with the seal line and **no reason**, on a presumption whose condition expired on the thirteenth. **The office has told the harbor authority in writing, in a hearing record, that no paper exists stating what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material.** That sentence is a live instrument and Volume 2 must not let it be forgotten **and may not let it be answered by a clerk.** **Closes in Volumes 2–4.** The Crown's next instrument is **a request from the circuit office worded so that a refusal is a delay**, and it arrives in **Chapter 52**.
3. **The *Vigil*'s paper of the 23rd, answered and unanswered in the same four sentences. — STILL OWED, CARRIED.** One and three granted, two refused, and the refusal is the crew's book. The cutter holds a paper on the file, the claim is in the record, the tender is at a cable's length, and a second cutter's boat is at the pile. **The officer has said in a road that an office with no opinion is an office that is going to want one.** **Closes across Volumes 2–3.** In Volume 2 he may be named, may sign, may send a paper, and is described from a distance only.
4. **The six lines of the count, and the line about other crews' paper. — STILL OWED, CARRIED, and turned once in Movement 1.** A Registry conservator's certification of *method* for nothing, with *nothing* written on the back in Calder's hand, and a public sheet with six copies carried to the herring skippers, the net loft and the *Low Lantern*. **Nobody consented to any of it except in person.** **Carried to Volumes 2–5, where publication is the argument.** **Chapter 54: a man on a market stall who is owed nothing asks for a copy to keep, and the yard gives him one — the first time in the volume that giving something away is the useful thing to do, and the first time the consent question costs the yard a record it cannot get back.**
5. **The strung line: a compartment that cannot have been cleared, and a case that went along a deck. — STILL OWED, and its binding is now a ruling rather than a flag.** Two witnesses now, one from a vessel and one from the deck, agreeing that four men carried a case past a paper read twice, and disagreeing about everything else. **The fragment is in custody and cannot be opened by anybody in Morrow Quay. Closes in Volume 4.** **The compartment is not re-used as a reveal in Volume 2, and no Volume 2 chapter may assert the case was in the space** — `outline/volume-02.md` lock L6, which carries the ratified three-step form.
6. **The aperture, dressed from outside, with fourteen holes at a template's spacing, fouling continuous, giving no hour. — STILL OWED, OPEN ACROSS VOLUMES 2–6.** Nobody can say who cut it or when or from where. **The fouling is the honest limit and may not be improved on in any later volume by a convenient document.** Not touched in Movement 1, and not to be touched in Chapter 52 or Chapter 60.
7. **Sera Mott's half. — STILL OWED, CARRIED, and locked.** *The ones she knows about stopped.* A route whose maintenance stopped is a fact about people and not about magic, and **who is left to go out is Volume 11's argument in embryo.** She has been asked by archivists and has said no and believed them, and she will say no again in about two years. **Closes across the series; the first half of the answer is Volume 5 and the argument is Volume 11.** **She may be asked once in Volume 2 and may say no again, and nobody may enter her name in a record without her asking for it** — `outline/volume-02.md` lock L20.
8. **The core's maintenance. — STILL OWED, and the volume that pays its craft is Volume 5.** A tongue half worn through, fouling that is not the enemy, four pieces of glass in a waist that cannot be got wet, a rope a year, and a bell that stops being a signal and starts being a rock. **This is the Bellwright stage's whole cost, introduced in Volume 1 by a working man and not by a stage list, and it is owed a craft in Volume 5.** `bible/power-system.md` places Bellwright in Volume 5 and **the volume audit ruled on 26 September 2026 that this is not in conflict: Volume 1 has a man, not a stage, and the six bible files are unchanged.** **Movement 1 begins the maintenance** — Chapter 55, the going out, a rope and a man's day every four or five years — and takes no stage.
9. **The bellwright himself. — RULED, AND THE NAME IS FIXED: CORVIN SARN.** Named nowhere in Volume 01, forty years old or thereabouts, with a blacksmith's forearms, who has been the harbor's own bellwright since the twentieth of October and who was asked nothing by anybody until a woman sent for him. He is the first person in the series to state the craft's cost in its own terms. **The volume audit ruled on 26 September 2026 that he is given a name in Volume 02, that the name is Corvin Sarn, the harbor's own bellwright of Morrow Quay, and that it lands in Movement 1 in the chapter that carries the first of the going out — Chapter 55 — spoken in a person's mouth and not written in a narrator's list. He teaches nobody, and he is not a Stage 5 mentor.** **The card deviation that caused this thread is closed, not deferred.**

## Threads opened by Batch 0004 and now owed

1. **The written purpose due on the twenty-first.** A blank slate on the bench with a heading and nothing under it. Kest reads it into the record with the door shut on the twenty-second, **the same day the conservator's second note is dated**, and the sitting is on the twenty-eighth. **This is the volume's last movement and the charter cannot be granted without it.**
2. **The one-season provisional working charter, the harbor's eighth, and the harbor's share.** Raised on the fourteenth, not granted. It is wider than anything the yard has held, it must be renewed, it is not the Second Bell and does not lead to it, and the eighth and the share both follow the crew into it. **Volume 1 payoff, Chapters 41–50.**
3. **The *Vigil's* month.** From the fourteenth of November the cutter has a month to send a paper on a charter question, and the officer has already entered the yard's three-word objection and the *Vigil's* claim in the same record. **A naval paper on a charter sitting is the Crown's attention arriving, and it is the volume's climax's ground.**
4. **The sheet the mooring line was laid down on.** Requested in the officer's notebook on the eighth and unanswered. If the *Vigil* produces it, only the *Vigil* has seen it; if Calder presses for it, it enters the record. **Owed through Volumes 1–3.**
5. **Elias Voke's claim and the first of March.** Nineteen winters of dues, disputed, entered, with Kest's promise to report in a fortnight in the same room. The harbor authority's fence on the third to the eleventh seams is what makes it a real loss, and **Volume 2 is where it is felt.** Carried from Batch 0003's thread 2 and now embodied in a person.
6. **The circuit office's second request.** Mara Venn's refusal was answered by a clause saying the office does not treat it as a comment on a person, and the ground description is still uncertified. A second request, worded so that a refusal is a delay, is the office's obvious next move and it will come. **Carries to Volumes 2–4 and is the instrument of her Volume 4 break.**
7. **Mara Venn's fourth *not stated*, now public.** Three in her own book and one in a hearing record that goes to Veyr, entered at the yard's request. She told Calder on the fourteenth of last month that one of the *not stated* entries would eventually be the reason his evidence was set aside. **That promise is now half-kept and the volume auditor should know which half.**
8. **The delayed note in a naval file.** Unattributed, with an hour and a state of the water, beside a yard's book with four matching lines. It is the concealment's own evidence and it is already in two offices. **It is the motif the Volume 15 finale pays as a working route signal, and it must not be resolved in Volume 1.**
9. **Bevin Rook's forty marks and the tenth of December.** A claim and not a wage, a reading that stops on a date, and a boy who has been put in the middle of a yard's record for nothing. **Owed into Volume 2, and April is not the date for the water — the tenth of December is.**

## Threads opened by Batch 0003 and now owed

1. **The notice of the 31st and its clause three.** Clause three stops Calder, Nessa and Bevin from entering the water inside the harbor on the authority's work. **PAID in Chapter 31 and Chapter 40: the yard was heard saying what it is, in its own words, in a hearing room, with a date, and the reeve entered it as `heard, not decided` and added his own words — that he has no paper saying the notice is wrong and none saying it is right, because a reeve's seal on a notice is a finding that a man served it correctly and not a finding that the notice is right.** The clause was not withdrawn and cannot be, and a lawful plan to work around it was refused on four correct grounds in Chapter 36. **The plain fact that a clause which stops three named people from earning a living has been read in Veyr as a measure about glass is now in a municipal hearing record with the yard's own sentence against it, and it is the seed of the Volume 4 inquiry's public argument, and it must not be resolved in Volume 1.**
2. **Kest's chart claim and its municipal cost.** The harbor authority has claimed the mole's inner face between the third and eleventh seams pending examination, and has paid for it with the timber fenders, four sets of moorings and two herring berths. **PAID in part in Chapter 34: the cost is now a person.** Elias Voke of the herring boat *Anna Ray* has nineteen winters of moorage dues entered as a disputed claim against the harbor authority in a hearing record, on the reeve's own motion, with a promise to report in a fortnight. **The herring fleet still has nowhere to lie on the first of March**, and this is a real cost owed to ordinary people by a protective act. It closes in **Volume 2** when the coast is poor and busy.
3. **Kest's breach of his own notice on the 3rd.** He went onto ground he had served closed, entered the sighting in his own book, and named the three witnesses who were standing there. **He has now put himself in a room a naval officer can use, and he knows it, and he had drafted the instrument that would have let a crew at the ground on the 31st and kept it back because showing it would have sent them out at night.** This is the first of the steps toward his Volume 9 testimony, and it may not be dramatized again before then.
4. **The conservator's second note, dated the 22nd.** **PART: the date is now said aloud three times in a hearing room, at a bench and in a road (Ch31, Ch35), the refusal of the ground is written under it (Ch35), and the date is entered in the hearing record of the seventh as a date the room is asked to note and not wait for.** The defense it was dated against has been discharged on the fourteenth, so the note now arrives eight days after nothing at all, and it will be dated the same day the yard's purpose for the twenty-eighth is read into the record with the door shut. **It has not yet arrived and no chapter has pretended it has. Movement 5 or an early Chapter 41 must let it land, with the date honoured.**
5. **The circuit officer's countersign.** **PAID once and hard in Chapter 36: a lawful six-clause plan was written, lodged and returned uncountersigned the same afternoon on four grounds, the first of which is that the office will not countersign an instrument putting a person inside the closure its own notice created.** Kest's reason for not asking is on the record, and Calder asked anyway, on his own paper, and was refused. **The panel at the fifth seam is therefore not reached in Volume 1 and cannot be, and the mechanism by which it is ever reached is a change of politics in an office, not a better argument from a shed.** A later plan is owed and is the strongest single card in Volumes 2 and 3.
6. **The ten terms for the seventh, sealed against addition after the sixth.** **PAID: all ten are read, ruled on, and answered in Chapters 31 and 40.** One proved the labor chain, two recorded the bar, three **withdrew the yard's own bearing sheet with a signed slip on it**, four received the three points, five received the fifth-seam thing as seen and not measured with the reeve's own entry on the record on his own motion, six noted the date of the twenty-second and did not wait for it, seven was heard and entered as **not decided**, eight restored the bottom **for the term and seven days only** and gave it back on the fourteenth, nine produced the reeve's own statement of what he needs, which is that **he cannot ask**, and ten was answered in the room on the seventh in the words of the term. **No term was added after the sixth and none was argued back on the fifteenth.**
7. **The crew with no means of work.** No boat, no pump, no bottom man, no tender, no permit, and a notice that closes eight seams of the town's own wall. **Volume 1 cannot resolve this; it is the condition Volume 2's charter and storm-exposed work exist to relieve, and it must be visible on the page when the charter is sought.**
8. **Bevin Rook's hand and his six weeks.** Wages nil, twenty-four marks owed by the yard, a bonesetter's binding and a surgeon's writing, and a mother holding the original in her apron. **His return to the water is deferred to April and must not be paid off quickly.**

## Threads opened by Batch 0002 and now owed

1. **The held gear bond.** Pump, clamp stock, cordage, and the drawing chest in pawn to eighteen until the 14th; the pump's new housing cannot be bought while it is pledged, so any job that needs a pump hires the harbor's at three a tide. **It comes back on the 14th, which is the day the defense lapses, and both events must land on the page.** Carries to Volume 1 Movement 5 and into Volume 2.
2. **The harbor eighth.** An eighth off any future raising follows the crew to any charter. Owed through Volume 1.
3. **Mara's Registry query.** Copying expense, duplicate witnessing, three *not stated* entries underlined; while it stands every entry she writes needs a circuit officer's countersign and her certificates take three weeks. **Batch 0003 spends roughly a quarter of the remaining standing and dates the exhaustion to Christmas.** Deniable friction, not her Volume 4 break. Carries to Volumes 3–4.
4. **The naval officer's noted connection.** The sitting record carries the Crown's question about the yard's relation to the 13-year-old salvage report, adopted by no one and answered by nobody. **It was read into the record in Chapter 19, Calder refused to defend his father's report, and it may not be re-dramatized in any later volume.** Carries as paper to Volumes 1–4.
5. **The *Low Lantern*'s stove and the withdrawn cover.** Sella Brigg paid for her own stove in and withdrew her crew's rescue cover for the winter on her own statement. **Harl Dace's cover stands inside the line and is the only cover the yard now has.** Owed through the winter and closed in Volume 2.

## Threads opened by Batch 0001 and still owed

These are new. Each names the volume that must close it, per thread hygiene.

1. **The two custody numbers.** The courier wrote them on the back of the second sheet and they are deliberately not quoted in the prose. They may be given digits when a later volume needs them on the page, and must not be invented retroactively. Carries until Volume 4.
2. **The nine-line mark's meaning.** The batch establishes that it describes a thing held together from more than one place and that no office in Veyr can interpret it. The **Nine Harbors Compact as a compact about custody closes in Volume 5**, and until then the mark may not be named as theirs.
3. **The aperture under the Crown flange.** A chamber with something settled in it, darker and older glass, lid never lifted, held under a written handling plan. Volume 1's later dives should return to it; the general question of how a forced or fused record is examined belongs to Volume 3's faultreader work and Volume 6's descent. **Batch 0003 found a second bolted rim, on a plate at the mole's fifth seam that has never been measured, and deliberately did not say the two are the same work. They are not to be fused.** The aperture's own volume is unchanged.
4. **The saw kerf in the shoal anchor's crown shackle.** Somebody sawed it nearly through and it was loaded again. Ordinary iron, nobody's claim, no consequence yet, and in Chapter 19 it is priced: twenty marks at a fair sale for the whole stack and thirteen to the crew after Sella's third, because a kerf nobody can account for makes a buyer pay scrap. Open until the chain is examined as evidence rather than as tackle; the natural home is the Volume 1–3 wreck-field work.
5. **The repairer's mark on the *Alder Reach*'s inside framing.** It is a shop mark of **eleven years back** — the middle of the Gannet decade, in plain reckoning — struck inside the sixth frame where a caulker's iron would land. Calder has told the crew it is a shop hand of that decade and has told nobody that the notation is written the way Oren's own pressure logs were written. This is a private suspicion with no evidence, it is **not** a *Gannet* fitting, it is not a mark on the glass, and it must not be resolved by a convenient document. Open across Volume 1 and into Volume 3, where the chain's order becomes legible.
6. **Nessa's condition and the cost of Calder's silence to her.** Mara has three *not stated* entries and has told him in writing that one of them will eventually be the reason his evidence is set aside. This pays in **Volume 4**, when Calder hands her the evidence and the original anchor, and it is the professional spine of the slow burn.
7. **The taper of the hearing.** Two clean daylight looks, then a slow recovery, then Volume 14's temporary loss and Volume 15's permanent damage. The Volume 1–13 stages must not let the tinnitus vanish silently between books; every volume should name it once.

## Central mystery, staged by volume

| Volume | Thread to answer | Question left open |
|---|---|---|
| 1–3 | Why does the Gannet evidence contradict the physical cargo handling, and why do the transparent wrecks form a route rather than a scatter? | Who controls the missing links in the chain? |
| 4–6 | Who changed civic custody into Crown ownership, and what evidence proves the Quietening was a forced-consensus cascade followed by a deliberate civic scuttling? | Who first used a seal to turn emergency custody into permanent ownership? |
| 7–10 | How did the Dominion edit charts and rebuild the Crown Seal, and how many communities must Ilya's network connect before activation? | What will the coast do when a single voice claims to be the only safe way to remember? |
| 11–14 | Can independently governed routes resist synchronization without filling every gap, surrendering restricted testimony, or recreating centralized control? | When the last connection cannot be cut safely, can Calder remain connected on his own terms? |
| 15 | The historical answer is already planned, but Oren's context changes at the midpoint: the evacuation bell had sounded and a second rescue line was active when he cut. His visible hand movement cannot prove what he understood, so the uncertainty defeats any attempt to make one witness a clean symbol; the final authority choice remains for the climax. | None in the form of a new enemy. The aftermath asks what a public memory system must do when it fails in an ordinary human way. |

## Character promises

- Alden's original repair log and partial confession remain unrevealed until the middle volumes: the partial confession begins in Volume 9 and the original repair log is handed over publicly in Volume 15. When he confesses, the reader must be able to distinguish his signed harbor salvage report from the custody copy Kest later alters in Volume 9, and the unaccounted plate note must have been paid off by the removed file page in Volume 4 rather than by his confession.
- Oren's stored choices must remain incomplete and occasionally resistant to Calder's preferred interpretation; no resurrection, no obedient final message, and no echo that speaks a command.
- Calder's full Second Bell license is not a Volume 1 payoff. Volume 1 settles the first recovery claim; the Volume 4 inquiry removes his eligibility for the full examination.
- The Mara relationship progresses through professional equality and public accountability. The Volume 12 marriage is a chosen life, not a plot reward.
- Tobias Wren is injured during the Orison investigation and later trains crews from shore. He is not killed to validate the protagonist.
- Ilya Serr's fear must stay credible: he is not defeated by being shown irrational, but by a coast that shares risk through many custodians instead of surrendering authorship.

## Institutional and physical promises

- The bell-buoy core recovered at the end of Volume 1 answers with the same delayed note Calder hears in the continuing keel line at the end of Chapter 10. That single signal is the motif the finale pays: the bellwright's answer becomes a working route signal rather than a relic.
- The three Gannet documents (harbor salvage report, Navy seizure report, Registry custody log) must stay distinct in the reader's mind from the first mention onward.
- A recovered echo can be stable and still be wrong. At least one later volume must show a sincere witness who was deceived, not only a liar.
- Consent marks (private, family, route use, public witness, sealed) are honored as written. Emergency access requires two independent custodians, a recorded reason, limited use, and a restored seal.

## Author-level decisions deferred to a later outline phase — RULED IN FULL, 26 September 2026

**These are now decisions and not deferrals. Each was ruled by the Volume 01 volume audit and the full reasoning is in `reviews/volume-01/volume-01-close.md` Part 2, mirrored in `state/continuity.md`, and carried into `outline/volume-02.md` as a lock where Volume 02 can be contradicted by it.**

1. **Captain Holt's position after Volume 3 — RULED: Volume 03's decision, and not before.** She loses her ship and command in Volume 3 and is the officer whose pressure makes Kest alter the harbor authority's custody copy of the Gannet salvage report in Volume 9. The posting and the channel by which a disgraced officer reaches a Morrow Quay reeve have to be chosen by the volume that makes her loss real, because the point of her defeat is that it costs her something. **Volume 02 does not need it and must not invent it:** Holt may be named, may sign, may send a paper, and is described from a distance only. **This is not a Volume 01 defect and no series question has been decided here.**
2. **The first echo's witness — RULED: OPEN BY DESIGN, and not the volume that needs him.** He is deliberately unlocated: the prose gives him a body — the smallest of four men, near end of the case, a lead tag with the first two figures and not the third — and gives Calder the sound of his own voice saying *I'll take the near end, I'm smallest*, and no name, no face, no rank, and no way to trace him. **The volume that first needs him decides whether he is findable and what finding him would cost, and that volume is not Volume 2**, whose business is a forged seal and a coast road. **He may not be resolved by a convenient anonymous letter or a surviving crew list**, and that instruction stands for whichever volume takes it.
3. **The abandoned Oren lead in Volume 5 — RULED: Volume 05's decision, and the lead's content cannot be pre-decided,** because the choice only means something once there is a lead on the page to give up. What is fixed now is the shape of the obligation: Calder saves a living Lowfen bell archive instead of pursuing a lead on Oren, the archive is saved, **the Oren route closes, and a Crown patrol seizes the crew's legal charter.** **Volume 02 must not plant an Oren lead in order to have something to abandon in Volume 5.**
4. **Lira Venn's volume — RULED: NOT ASSIGNABLE FROM A VOLUME AUDIT, and this is a standing instruction rather than a deferral.** She is canon in the bible — Mara's younger cousin, a Registry copyist, never confused with Mara, her relationship stated the first time they share a scene — and she must be used before her later work duplicating public records matters, and `bible/characters.md` says plainly that she may not be invented into a plot after the fact. **The allocation is a series-level decision owed by a series phase. Until it is made, no chapter in any volume may use her as a device.** This is recorded as a constraint on drafting, not as a question this file is waiting on.
5. **The teaching question for Bellwright, Keelwright and Chorus Diver — RULED: each is ruled by the volume that owns the stage — Volume 5, Volume 9, Volume 11.** What is fixed now is the rule binding all three, and it is already in force in Volume 1: **no convenient master, no teacher who arrives and explains the craft, and a team craft rather than a gift.** Volume 1's entry procedure was assembled in the yard out of Tobias Wren's secondhand memory of the Veyr cutters thirty-one years ago, Nessa Pike's engineering and Mara Venn's paperwork, and the prose says so in two characters' own mouths. **The Volume 2 instance is the bellwright, and he teaches nobody** — see item 13.

6. **The clerk's slate wall, the tidal day, and the closure's edge — RULED, all three, 26 September 2026.**
   **(a) The harbor authority's clerk wipes the hearing room's slate wall every Monday at eight o'clock whether there is a sitting or not — CONFIRMED as an institutional fact.** The wall was blank at the sittings of the seventh, the tenth, the fourteenth of November, the twenty-eighth and the twenty-sixth of December, and Chapter 50 says the next wipe is the fourth of January. **A figure on that wall survives one week; the ledger is the record and the wall is a convenience.**
   **(b) The *Vigil* tender's days on station are carried forward from the page and are not computed from the calendar — CONFIRMED, and the base is now stated: interpolate from the fourth of November at twenty days, which reproduces every figure on the page exactly** (twenty-four on the eighth, thirty-nine on the twenty-third, forty-four on the twenty-eighth, seventy-two on the twenty-sixth of December). **Never state a day-count no chapter gave as though a chapter had given it.**
   **(c) Ordinary harbor work landward of the third seam is not inside the closure of the notice of the thirty-first, and a crew the notice bars from the authority's work may still take private work on harbor ground inside the line — CONFIRMED.** The notice closes the mole's toe and inner face from the third to the eleventh seams, does not reach the twelfth, the dump, or water, and **no notice has ever closed water.** Clause three catches only the authority's work. **This is the only reason the yard has any money in Volume 1 at all and it stays true in Volume 2.**

7. **Chapters 31 and 40 are the longest in the manuscript — RULED: recorded, not defended, and the remedy is a shape instruction in the next outline, not an edit.** 4,963 and 5,822 words against `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s 2,200–3,200, both hearing rooms, each carrying a full agenda. **Splitting either would mean inventing a scene, and a volume of fifty chapters can afford two long rooms.** The volume is not harmed and the chapters were not rewritten. **The fix is now written down where a writer will meet it: `outline/volume-02.md` lock L22 and the shape rules at the head of `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` — no chapter is two rooms, and a chapter that wants to be a hearing room and a market is split across two days with a body or a market or a boy in one of them.**
8. **The first entry's hand cost — RULED: the prose stands and the deviation is accepted, not applied.** `bible/power-system.md` lists *Calder's hands shake for two days* among the costs of a first echo entry; Chapters 6 and 7 invert it, and the Batch 0001 review judged the inversion the stronger beat, because the whole point of the cost is that it is not in his hands. **A prose phase cannot edit the bible and this audit did not, so the item is recorded as a decided reading of the bible rather than as an open question.** **Binding for the whole series: no later volume may pay the shake as a debt that was owed. If Calder's hands shake, they shake from cold, air, a rope, a held sheet of glass, or plain fear, and it reads as that.**
