9. **Chapter 38 tells Mara Venn the Oren mark, where the Batch 0004 card asked for the hiding to continue — RULED: THE CHAPTER STANDS AND THE CARD IS AMENDED PROSPECTIVELY, 26 September 2026.** The card and the chapter cannot both stand and the chapter is the better of the two, for three reasons that are on the page: **`outline/volume-01.md` asks for the concealment at the *volume boundary* and Chapter 38 is not the boundary — a shed at half past ten at night, a fortnight before a hearing — and Volume 01 in fact closed with him still hiding it from every office, from Kest, from his father, from the crew and from the custody room**; **what it costs him is not grief but a false record assembling itself in a naval file, in three documents that do not know laid side by side**; and **two of the concealment's three surviving parts exist only because Chapter 38 happened**, being a boy with a book who said out loud in the yard that he will not write what came out of a man's mouth, and a woman who has said out loud that she would rather be the only person who knows what is in that anchor and has never been asked. **The deviation is accepted, not tolerated, and is no longer carried as a proposed canon change.** **Binding instruction, now in `outline/volume-02.md` lock L4 and in the Batch 0001 card file: a concealment may be told once, to one person, in a private scene, at a price — a second telling buys nothing, and the volume must close with it still hidden from every office and from the crew.**
10. **The bellwright's core and the craft of it — RULED: THE BIBLE ALREADY PERMITS IT. ITEM CLOSED. NO AMENDMENT PROPOSED, 26 September 2026.** `bible/terminology.md` defines a *bell buoy* as a physical signal buoy that can *listen for a stable echo*, and the core hung on the twenty-fifth of December **listened and answered**, which is the definition's own verb; and it defines a *listening bell* as one used by a trained operator to *receive route and witness signals*, which is also what the core did. `bible/power-system.md`'s Bellwright entry is a **craft, not a rank** — *a craftsperson who can work echo-glass into panels, cases, bells, and buoys and can maintain a small local listening route, and whose formal advancement requires a crew and ongoing maintenance* — and the clause that governs is that **a bell buoy cannot transmit the entire experience; it gives a trained operator a signal, timing, and sometimes a practice trace.** Chapter 46 obeys that clause to the word: the core gives a signal and a timing, a hand's width of a second, across a local route, and transmits no experience, no witness, no scene and no practice trace. **The Bellwright stage at Volume 5 is not in conflict and needs no amendment: Volume 1 has a man, not a stage.** **The six bible files are unchanged and no proposed canon change arises from this item.** **One honest note for the record, since this file asserted it more flatly than the prose does: the words *it reads nothing, it settles nothing and it says no word* are the volume's lock and are not a sentence in any chapter.** The negative is carried by the craft's economics and by Kest's chart notation — *not a mark and not a light and not a danger, a signal, kept by hand, at the cost stated in this office's hearing record* — and that is sufficient. A later volume must keep the object on that footing rather than hunt for a line to quote.
11. **The *Aster Gannet* lies in pieces under and beside the south end of the mole, and the mole was built on her in the year she went down — RULED: RATIFIED as canon, 26 September 2026.** Chapter 44 establishes it twice over, from two independent hands: Tobias Wren unasked, and the harbor authority's own account of its own works read in the barge and not copied, in a surveyor's hand dated the tenth of October, four days before a cutter came into the harbor — *the foreshore at the south end being mixed, containing iron and timber*. **It is load-bearing beyond the measurement: it is the only lawful ground on which the compartment could be measured inside the harbor line at all**, because the notice closes the third to the eleventh seams and does not reach the twelfth, the dump, or water, and no notice has ever closed water. Without it there is no plate, no coaming, no grating, no aperture, no fragment and no midpoint. **Binding and verified to be honored:** no custody object returns, nothing on claimed ground is touched, the *Gannet's* ferry iron and the transparent keel are two different objects and are never fused, and the run of transparent material is continuous from the mole's toe outward.
12. **The midpoint's exact finding is *the compartment could not have been cleared*, not *the compartment was occupied*, and the argument needs all three of its steps — RULED: THE THREE-STEP FORM IN CHAPTER 47 IS RATIFIED, 26 September 2026, and carried into `outline/volume-02.md` as lock L6.** `outline/volume-01.md` states the midpoint as *the* compartment *the manifest calls empty is too large to have been empty*, which is a statement about the fit of a measurement and a thing, and **the chapter's three steps are the mechanical content of that sentence**: (1) the clear opening is three foot nine by two foot four, the space is eight foot four by six foot nine clear and eleven feet two deep under the grating, the case is seven to eight feet in its longest face, and the space lies under a grating in a deck **so the hatch is the only way anything has ever gone into it or came out of it**; (2) **a room nothing can be taken out of is a room that has not been cleared** — the step the Batch 0005 review found missing, since excluding *this case* through *this opening* does not establish that the space was never cleared of anything else; (3) **this office's own thirteen-year-old wreck file, in a harbor's hand, says number four, port side aft, grating in place, not cleared, contents not removed.** All three are on the page in the entry of the eighth of December, in the reason entered in the reeve's book at eight that morning, and in Bevin Rook's read-back. **This proves the manifest unreliable and proves nothing whatever about what was in the space, and it is the stronger and more durable finding because it is a figure and not a sentence.** **Binding for the remainder of the series:** no chapter may state or imply that the sealed case was in standing space number four; the three Gannet documents stay distinct; and **any volume that restates the finding restates all three steps, never the two-step form** — `outline/ending.md` promise 4 pays this in Volume 15, and a volume that compresses the chain between here and there makes the finale's payoff smaller than its setup.
13. **The bellwright is unnamed — RULED: HE IS NAMED IN VOLUME 02 AND THE NAME IS CORVIN SARN, 26 September 2026.** The harbor's own bellwright of Morrow Quay, about fifty, with a blacksmith's forearms, in that yard since the twentieth of October, present in Chapter 16 as *the bellwright* who fitted a new tongue to the harbor buoy, and given the whole of the trade's argument in Chapter 46 and a share of the closing chapter in Chapter 50. **The name lands in Volume 02, Movement 1, in the chapter that carries the first of the going out — Chapter 55 — in the chapter in which somebody goes out in a boat in the weather to look after a hung bell, and it is spoken in a person's mouth and not written in a narrator's list.** The reasoning, so no later batch re-derives it: **a name spoken at a first introduction is a label, and a name spoken by a crew that needs him next month is a fact about a working town.** **He teaches nobody, he is the harbor's bellwright and not the Free Keel's, and he is not a Stage 5 mentor** — the formal Bellwright stage is Volume 5 and requires a crew, and Volume 1's lock against a convenient master applies to every later volume. Carried in `outline/volume-02.md` lock L17 and in the Chapter 55 card.

14. **The batch's shape fault and its length comparison — RULED: the "worse on length than Batch 0004" claim is WITHDRAWN and is not re-entered. The shape judgement stands. The length instruction is moved out of this file and into `outline/volume-02.md`. 26 September 2026.** The correction, verified by measurement in this phase: the **40,859** first recorded for Batch 0005 was a `chapter-004*.md` glob that swept Chapter 40 in from Batch 0004, and **the true total is 35,514** (Ch41 3,096 · Ch42 2,822 · Ch43 2,983 · Ch44 3,571 · Ch45 4,324 · Ch46 3,921 · Ch47 3,791 · Ch48 3,637 · Ch49 3,248 · Ch50 4,121), which is the only figure in the repository. **On the corrected measurement Batch 0005 is 4,022 words shorter than Batch 0004's 39,536 and past the 3,200 guideline in seven chapters against Batch 0004's nine**, so the claim that it was "six chapters past the 3,200-word guideline where Batch 0004 managed two" and worse on length than Batch 0004 was an artefact of the same bad glob, **is withdrawn, and is not defended.** The shape judgement stands and is the part that matters: **the pressure types alternate as Batch 0004's review asked, and no chapter in Batch 0005 is two rooms, which was Batch 0004's specific fault and what cost it its two longest chapters.** The remaining excess is a structural property of a movement containing a hearing, a recovery and a custody surrender, not a fault of any scene. **The length instruction is now a volume-level rule in `outline/volume-02.md` lock L22 and in the shape rules at the head of `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md`, where a writer will meet it, and not only in a state file.**

## Flagged for the controller, not fixable from a writer phase

These are defects in controller-owned files. A writer or reviewer phase must not edit `.opencode/agent/`, `AGENTS.md`, `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`, `scripts/`, or `.github/workflows/`, so they are recorded here for the controller to repair.

- **`AGENTS.md` §Systems and In-World Interfaces, "The System" line.** The writer agent definition at `.opencode/agent/novel-writer.md` instructs the writer to "Use the System naturally. Keep panels rare, readable, and connected to the fictional world." That contradicts `AGENTS.md`, `bible/power-system.md`, `bible/premise.md`, `bible/terminology.md`, `outline/series.md`, and batch hard rule 1, all of which forbid any game-style interface in this world. The writer agent is the process that actually produces the chapters, so it is the highest-leverage place a panel could enter the novel. **Mitigation in force meanwhile:** `workspace/volume-01/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, `batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, `batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, `batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, and the queued `batch-0005/PROMPT.md` each state the ban unconditionally and enumerate the permitted world-native substitutes, so no batch can introduce one even while the agent file stands. **Five batches have now been written under that mitigation and all five came out clean, and the claim is stated at the width it is true at:** **Chapters 31–50 contain no occurrence of the word *panel* and none of the standing banned terms, and no chapter in those two batches contains any of the listed interface furniture.** The earlier chapters do use *panel*, always for a sheet of glass — Chapters 5, 6, 7, 26, 27 and 29, including the Chapter 29 title *The Panel at the Fifth Seam* — and **no occurrence anywhere in the manuscript is a panel that is not a piece of glass**, which is the thing the ban is actually about. A mechanical scan of the whole volume for the word therefore does return hits, and the correct claim is the narrow one, not a clean scan. **A fifth batch has now been written under that mitigation and it came out clean:** Chapters 41–50 contain no occurrence of *panel*, of *the System*, of *stats*, of *skills* or *skill*, of *mastery*, of *status panel*, of *notification*, of *quest*, of *level up*, of *true memory*, or of any software vocabulary, and the only message-like objects used in the batch are the yard's purpose and supplement, the reeve's chart notation and his Sunday entry, the reeve's suspended-instrument warning, the *Vigil*'s three terms and the yard's four-sentence answer, the count, the tender's written reason, the crew's book, the six-line public sheet with the market-wall nail, the custody-log entry with its seal line, the chart notation for the hung core, and a bell. The only message-like objects in Chapters 21–30 are the shoal book's correction column, the reeve's four lists, the wreck entry read in room, the four-clause notice with a seal over the nail, the *Vigil* captain's one-page request, the reeve's six-clause instrument, the reeve's notation on the harbor chart, the crew's book, the yard's board copy of the notice, the three sheets and the fifth-seam sheet on the market wall, and the ten written terms — none of which tells Calder what to do next. **The agent line should be rewritten as a conditional keyed to the approved fiction bible**, and the same check is worth making for every novel in the fleet, since the line is shared. **In Batch 0004 the single occurrence of the word *panel* in ten chapters was a glass panel at the mole's fifth seam, and it was changed to *the thing at the fifth seam* so that a mechanical scan of the batch returns nothing at all; the batch's only permitted message-like objects are claim seals, a seal line, the reeve's chalk, the hearing record, a crew's book, a tag and a rag float with a chalk number on it, market-wall sheets, a slate wall, ten printed copies, two reeve's entries, a board tag, a fouled-line entry, a custody-room request, a plan and its refusal, a withdrawal, and a blank headed slate — and none of them tells Calder what to do next.**
- **`state/phase-ledger.json` has not been advanced past bootstrap, and the gap is the ledger and not the markers. Restated after the Volume 01 close, 26 September 2026, and one earlier claim in that record is now withdrawn.** The file still shows `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap` at `running` and `volume-01-batch-0001` at `planned` with `attempts: 0` and `resultCommit: null`, while fifty finished chapters, five complete batch summaries, an audited volume, a Volume 02 outline, a Volume 02 batch plan, four updated state files and a queued writer phase exist. **This phase was handed the record that `workspace/volume-01/batch-0005/` has no `.done` marker. That is wrong and is withdrawn: `workspace/volume-01/batch-0001/` through `batch-0005/` all carry `.done`, `workspace/phase-000-bootstrap/` carries `.done`, and `phase-001-outline` and `phase-002-batch-plan` carry `.retired`. The gap is the ledger, not the markers, which is a narrower defect than three consecutive batches recorded.** A planning or prose phase may not edit the ledger or write markers, so this phase wrote no `.done` and no `.retired` and did not touch the file. **Consequence for the next writer: do not read the ledger as evidence that any batch is unwritten, and do not mark anything done; the controller owns that transition.**
- **`workspace/volume-01/batch-0006/PROMPT.md` existed, carried no marker, and sorted ahead of `workspace/volume-02/` in the runner's ordered scan. The controller has since written its `.retired` marker, and this is closed.** It was a **withdrawn no-op**: as queued it contradicted the chapter it claimed to inherit (it said the batch should run *from about Thursday the 7th of January* when 1 January is a Sunday and the 7th is a Saturday, and when its own first card is the fourth of January) and it asserted a starting state of *nothing in the bag, nothing in the tin* against Chapter 50's own *five marks in a bag*. **It would also have handed four unratified author-level decisions to a writer as canon, and those four are now ruled.** Everything Batch 0006 would have needed is in `outline/volume-02.md`, in `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md` and in the queued prompt, so nothing was lost with the withdrawal. **As of 26 September 2026 the file is marked `.retired` and the runner will skip it; no writer phase may treat it as live, and the controller, not a writer, wrote the marker.**
- **The marker gap has moved, and it is still the ledger's and not the markers'.** `workspace/volume-01/batch-0001/` through `batch-0005/` carry `.done`, `workspace/volume-01/batch-0006/` now carries `.retired`, `workspace/phase-000-bootstrap/` and `phase-003-volume-01-close/` carry `.done`, and `phase-001-outline` and `phase-002-batch-plan` carry `.retired`. **`workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/` carries `.done` — a claim made in an earlier draft of this record that it had no marker at all was wrong and is withdrawn — and `workspace/volume-02/batch-0002/` carries none yet, which is the controller's transition and not this file's to report either way, while `state/phase-ledger.json` still shows `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap` at `running` with `volume-01-batch-0001` at `planned`, `attempts: 0` and `resultCommit: null`, against sixty finished chapters, six complete batch summaries, an audited volume, a corrected Volume 02 outline, five updated state files and one queued writer phase.** A prose or planning phase may not write a marker or edit the ledger, so this phase wrote no `.done` and did not touch the file. **Consequence for the next writer: do not read the ledger or the marker set as evidence that any batch is unwritten, and do not mark anything done; the controller owns that transition.**
- **Review artifacts are inconsistent across batches, no reviewer log is kept for any batch, and the reviewer may be read-only by design.** `reviews/volume-01/` holds `batch-0001.md`, `batch-0003.md` and now **`volume-01-close.md`**, the volume audit. **There is no stored review for Batch 0002**, whose only record of its review is the writer's own repair sections in `state/current.md` and `state/continuity.md`. **No batch in either volume has a reviewer log kept in this repository: `logs/` holds only `close.log` and `models.log`, and `reviews/` holds the Volume 01 Batch 0001 and Batch 0003 reviews, the two volume audits, and nothing for Volume 01 Batch 0002 or for any of Volume 02's five batches. Every Volume 02 review finding is disposed of in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 000N review repairs*, and that section is the authority.** If `.opencode/agent/novel-reviewer.md` is instructed to be read-only and to report only to `logs/`, then the ledger and the reviewer definition should be made to persist a review file per batch, or `AGENTS.md`'s Quality Gate line "a reviewer has checked the result" needs a home for the verdict. A prose or planning phase may not edit either file, so it is recorded here. **`reviews/volume-01/batch-0001.md` and `batch-0003.md` were left exactly as they were by the Volume 01 close, and no attempt was made to reconstruct the missing reviews.**

- **`state/phase-ledger.json` is still not advanced past bootstrap after a hundred finished chapters, and this is now a live dispatch blocker rather than a bookkeeping gap. Restated after the Volume 02 Batch 0005 review, 26 September 2026.** The file still shows `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap` at `running` and `volume-01-batch-0001` at `planned` with `attempts: 0` and `resultCommit: null`, while **one hundred finished chapters, ten batch summaries, a closed and audited Volume 01, a closed Volume 02, a Volume 02 outline, ten Volume 03-phase prompt files under `workspace/` and one queued volume-close prompt exist.** `PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s selector *reads the phase ledger and claims the lowest incomplete batch*, so **a controller that trusts the ledger will select bootstrap and re-run a phase that has already been superseded.** `state/current.md` and the queued close prompt both reserve the ledger to the controller, so **no writer, reviewer or audit phase may repair it and none was attempted.** **Consequence for the next writer: do not read the ledger as evidence that any batch is unwritten, do not mark anything done, and do not queue a phase; the controller owns the transition and this record is the hand-off.**
## Intentionally open at the ending

- The precise origin of tideglass remains uncertain.
- Some merged echoes may develop behavior beyond their witnesses; the ending does not prove the sea has a conscious will.
- The Dominion survives as a diminished political power, so the new public network can fail and be repaired in ordinary human ways.
- Oren's restricted remainder is never opened. The final answer about his last hour is deliberately incomplete.

## Thread hygiene

- New threads may be opened inside a batch, but each one must name the volume that will close it, or be flagged here as intentionally open.
- A thread that contradicts the bible, the series outline, or the ending outline is not a new canon fact. It is an error to correct before the next batch runs.
- Any change to the power rules, the consent rules, the mystery chain, or the ending is recorded here as an author-level decision and mirrored in `state/continuity.md`.

## Volume 02, Batch 0001 (Chapters 51–60) — as written, Movement 1

**Volume 02 is in progress. Chapters 51–60 are written. This block marks every thread the batch moved, every thread it opened, and every author-level decision it made. No thread below is closed by a chapter unless a chapter says so on the page.**

### Threads carried from Volume 01 and moved here

1. **The nineteenth of January at the mole's toe. — PAID, in Chapter 60.** A Thursday, the top of the flood, the mole's toe, a claim window of the seventeenth naming one date and a look only, a hand lead and a slate and a bell, **a reason of two hundred and sixty-nine words with no diver in it, entered in the harbor authority's book at eight in the morning in Calder's own left hand with the clerk present**, Pike on the cleat, the harbor's clerk and the tide gang on the wall, and the harbor's crane and barge on the authority's account. The figures are **nine at the south end, ten and a half at the north end, seven and three-eighths at the mole's root**, and then **five and a quarter sounding landward under a stone quay**; the run was traced **inland, under the mole's root and under a quay** and the day was stopped at twenty past eleven because a route that no paper has ever been written for cannot be asked about in a hearing room. **All of it was sounded on ground the notice of the thirty-first closes, under a claim window this office had no power to write, and Kest says so in the record twice — see the new lock L24.** **What is under the town is not explained and is not named.** Closes in Volume 02 Movement 1; the run itself is carried to Movement 2.
2. **The custody-log entry of the twentieth of December and the Crown's next move. — PART, in Chapter 52.** **The second request has arrived and is on the page in its own words**: deliver the public sheet and all ten copies into the custody room so the record of a contested material may be single; a party declining must state its ground in writing within fourteen days or is treated as not having declined; a request returned without a ground comes back with a second seal, **and the second seal is a seal of delay**. The yard has paid three marks to have it entered and dated, has not refused, and has not answered. **Mara Venn is named in the copy as a person who has already declined a related request, so the yard cannot spend her twice, and she has no standing to refuse anything.** **She does not break with the Registry in this chapter, in this batch, or in this volume, and the instrument of her break is now on the page in its first form.** The fourteen-day clock expires on the thirteenth of January, and **the clerk's *fifteenth day* and *twenty-ninth day* are day-counts from the thirtieth of December and not dates: the second seal is owed on the twenty-ninth day, which is the twenty-seventh of January.** **Closes across Volumes 2–4; the volume that answers it is Volume 4.**
3. **The *Vigil's* paper, answered and unanswered. — CARRIED, and pressed once.** In the road on the fourth of January a man off a cutter's boat asks the yard, in front of eleven people, whether the crew means to be working inside the line this season, and gets a one-sentence answer about a charter, and the boy enters the question, the words, the hour, the answer, and the fact that it was not asked twice. **Captain Holt is not named, not in a room, and not on paper in this batch**, and the tender lies at a cable's length and the second cutter's boat stays at the pile. Closes across Volumes 2–3.
4. **The six lines of the count and the line about other crews' paper. — PART, and it has begun to cost.** A man on a market stall who is owed nothing asks for a copy to keep and is given one; **the count of the printed crew copies goes from ten to eleven and both numbers are true of their own dates**; the naval rating's slate now holds a number a week out of date; and Harl Dace of the *Grey Widow* asks in a net loft **who the record is for**, after establishing that it binds nobody and consents to nobody, and the yard has no answer. **This is the consent question's first cost and it is not free.** Closes across Volumes 2–5, where publication is the argument.
5. **The strung line: a compartment that cannot have been cleared, and a case that went along a deck. — CARRIED, untouched.** The compartment is not referred to in this batch, and **no chapter states or implies that the sealed case was in standing space number four.** The three Gannet documents stay three documents. Closes in Volume 4.
6. **The dressed aperture, fourteen holes, fouling giving no hour. — CARRIED, untouched.** Not referred to. The fouling may not be improved on by a convenient document. Open across Volumes 2–6.
7. **Sera Mott's half. — CARRIED, untouched.** She is not asked in this batch. She may be asked once in Volume 2 and may say no again, and nobody may enter her name in a record without her asking. Closes across the series; the first half of the answer is Volume 5 and the argument is Volume 11.
8. **The core's maintenance. — PART, in Chapter 55, and the mechanism is now in the world.** Corvin Sarn is named, states the cost of a signal in his own terms, refuses the crew's share in one sentence, and gives the mechanism: **the core answers because the flood scours its seaward face and the fouling is what does the scouring, the plate under the mole's root never answered because nothing has moved over it, and a bell only speaks while somebody keeps the weather working on it.** A rope a year, a man's day every four or five years, and a person in a boat in the weather. **Eight marks and a day of a boat is the recurring price and it recurs in the chapter's last line.** The Bellwright stage is still Volume 5 and is not taken. Closes in Volume 5 as a craft.
9. **The bellwright himself. — PAID, in Chapter 55.** **CORVIN SARN, the harbor's own bellwright of Morrow Quay, named in Nessa Pike's mouth and again by Calder Marr, in the chapter that carries the first of the going out.** He teaches nobody and he is not the Free Keel's. The audit's ruling is discharged and is not carried as an open item.

### Threads opened by Batch 0001

10. **The copy that left the yard. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 5, with the consent argument.** One of the printed crew copies is in the hands of a coaster's master who is not a Free Keel man and is owed nothing, and it is to be kept and not returned. The count is published and now wrong by one. **Volume 2–5 must ask what a record in a man's keeping is, and Volume 3 and 4 must use the fact that the record can be used against the yard as well as for it.**
11. **Who the record is for. — NEW, open. Closes in Volumes 2–5, and the argument peaks in Volume 4 or 5.** Harl Dace asks it in a net loft and the yard cannot answer it: the sheet is a record of an object, it binds nobody, it consents for nobody, and it is on a wall and in five lofts and a chest. **The answer is the Open Keel Assembly's charter in Volume 9 and its public procedure in Volume 15, and no Volume 2 chapter may answer it.**
12. **The run under the town. — NEW, open. Carries to Volume 02 Movement 2 and beyond.** Four hundred feet of ordinary figures — **nine at the south end, ten and a half at the north end, seven and three-eighths at the mole's root, five and a quarter sounding landward over a stone quay** — the bottom coming up at about a foot in thirty, and no chart and no forum. **Nine and a quarter is the ferry plate beyond the eleventh seam and is not one of these figures, and the two objects are never fused.** **The leash is named as a leash at its end: a route that no paper has ever been written for cannot be asked about in a hearing room.** Closes nowhere in this volume; it is the volume's opened pressure and the material of the Volume 2 midpoint's coast road and the Volume 7 chart question.
13. **The reason with a hole in it. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 4, with the record.** The first stated reason written for a municipal book that carries no purpose, entered in the crew's book in the boy's own right hand as an omission with a date, because four men in a boat agreed not to ask the question. **A document that says what was done and not why is a document a man in an office can fill in with whatever he likes.** The volume may not resolve it, and may not let anybody in the yard ask the boy about it.
14. **Eighteen marks on the first of February, and a wage on the first of February. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 02 Movement 5.** The authority's day rate for the nineteenth is entered and payable on the first of February, the same day the boy's first four marks a week falls due, and Calder has told him nothing. **This is the first money that has ever been coming to this yard out of somebody else's pocket, and Volume 2's resolution is a boy whose wages begin to be paid in part out of somebody else's pocket — and the yard's own debt to Mara Venn is NOT paid and may not be paid out of it.**
15. **The second seal, owed on the twenty-ninth day from the thirtieth of December, which is the twenty-seventh of January. — NEW, open. Closes in Volumes 2–4.** The yard has neither refused nor answered. The second request comes back with a seal of delay, and the seal of delay is not a refusal. **Volume 2 Movement 3 is where the request is answered and re-asked, and the second seal is the instrument of it.**
16. **Three custody items under three numbers, digits still not quoted. — CARRIED, untouched.** None of them returns in this batch and none is asked for. The yard does not ask for the custody room. The municipal claim on the third item stands and is not withdrawn.

### Author-level decisions taken in this batch

- **The power rules are unchanged and no stage is taken.** Calder does not dive in this batch and cannot, the notice's third clause naming him; the one body in the water is a hired diver on a herring skipper's account. **What changes is method and not rank**: a schedule, a tender who is not the diver, a fixed limit on the slate, a cutoff that can be pulled without asking anybody, a stated reason entered before the diver goes in in the diver's own left hand with the clerk present and not edited, a named ground in the paper, and **other people in the room who know why** — the tide gang on the wall, the harbor's clerk, the reeve's man, and a boy with a book who writes down what he heard and what he did not. **His growth in this batch is that he asks for a specific thing on a specific date and takes a smaller answer without arguing, and that is method.**
- **The consent rules are unchanged and are now under load for the first time.** A copy went out of the yard to a man it did not name and cannot recall, the count changed, and a record of an object is established in the yard's own mouth as binding nobody and consenting nobody. **Nothing in this batch overrides a seal, a limit or a refusal, and no emergency access was used.**
- **The mystery chain is unchanged and not advanced.** No chapter explains the Quietening, identifies the Crown Seal, names the Nine Harbors, explains the nine-line mark, proves who altered the manifest, reveals what the sealed case held, names the Office of Returning Memory, or puts a senior examiner in a room. **The run under the town is a physical discovery of a bearing and a bottom and nothing else.** **Ilya Serr, Halven Rook and Captain Holt do not appear anywhere in the batch, on paper or otherwise.**
- **The ending is untouched and no new antagonist, institution or final enemy appears.** The only new named people are ordinary working people: Corvin Sarn, Tam Slee, Amos Pryde, the copying clerk, the chandler, the market stallholder, the bonesetter, the mother. **The copying clerk is the first institutional figure in the book who is drawn as a man who fills in a box and says what a box does, and he is not an antagonist and may not become one.**
- **A correction to a planning file, recorded on the batch and APPLIED on the review, 26 September 2026.** The line in `outline/volume-02.md` lock L1 and in `workspace/volume-02/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` that reads *the fourth, eleventh, eighteenth and twenty-fifth of January are Mondays* is incompatible with the same lock's *the fourth of January is a Wednesday*, and the interval *twenty-six December to the first of March is nine weeks and two days, eighty-one days* gives a figure that is wrong for its own weeks. **The correct Mondays in January are the 2nd, 9th, 16th, 23rd and 30th, nine weeks and two days is sixty-five days, and 1 February is a Wednesday as 1 March is.** The chapters ran on the corrected grid from the first draft. **On the Batch 0001 review the planning files were corrected in place** — lock L1, Movement 1's *eight weeks off*, and `outline/batches/volume-02-batch-0001.md`'s two intervals — **each with the correction and its date entered in the file, because a correction that lives only in a state file is a correction the next outline phase overwrites.** The batch had found two of the three planning-file errors and missed Movement 1's; all three are now closed. **The queued Batch 0002 prompt's own calendar list was corrupt as well and has been replaced with the measured grid.**
- **A new author-level decision, taken on the Batch 0001 review and now `outline/volume-02.md` lock L24: the harbor authority works inside the notice's own closure, knowingly, and says so in the record.** The first clause of the notice of the thirty-first closes the mole's toe and inner face from the third to the eleventh to all work and all approach by all persons "which includes this office, this yard, and any other crew in this harbor," and the harbor authority has no power to lift it. The batch's permit of the eighth of January, its reason of the twelfth, its claim window of the seventeenth and its day of the nineteenth all sat inside that closure, and the batch recorded none of it. **The decision is that the reeve enters the breach in his own hand every time, in the yard's reason, in his own barge, and under the reason in the authority's book; that Calder Marr puts the reeve's own November ground to him in the room and is answered; and that the cost is Kest's alone.** **Binding on every later chapter of the volume: an instrument of this office that puts a person, a boat or a lead inside that closure carries the reeve's acknowledgement on the page, and the closure is never treated as lifted, narrowed or irrelevant.** It is consistent with the standing ruling that no notice has ever closed water and with ordinary harbor work landward of the third seam being outside the closure. **No antagonist, institution, stage or enemy is added by it; `outline/ending.md` is untouched.**
- **One reconciliation that changes a stated position, flagged.** The card for Chapter 58 requires a fourth set of moorings and the herring fleet's winter warp on the harbor authority's own account, and the fourth set in Kest's own words of the twenty-eighth of November is the ninth seam's, so **the ninth seam's mooring is bought back into service in Chapter 58 and is not worked.** The north berth stays out, one berth stays short on the first of March, and the nineteen winters stay disputed and unpaid, so the volume's clock is unchanged. Recorded here and in `state/continuity.md` rather than smoothed.

### Threads opened or re-priced by the Batch 0001 review, 26 September 2026

17. **The harbor authority inside its own closure. — NEW, open, and now `outline/volume-02.md` lock L24. Closes in Volume 02, Movement 5.** The permit of the eighth of January, the reason of the twelfth, the claim window of the seventeenth and the day of the nineteenth all put the Free Keel yard, a hired diver or a boat with a hand lead inside the first clause of a notice the harbor authority served and cannot lift, and the batch recorded the contradiction nowhere. **Kest now enters it three times in his own hand and has said aloud that he wants the yard holding it as well as he is.** **Movement 2 and every later movement of this volume must carry the acknowledgement whenever that ground is worked, and the ninth seam's mooring — bought back out of the office's own money and still not worked — cannot be worked without it, which is the price of a mooring nobody in this harbor can legally touch.** It is a cost on the reeve and it is not free.
18. **The note of the thirty-first of December, and its second seal. — PART, date corrected.** The second seal is owed on the **twenty-ninth day from the thirtieth of December, which is the twenty-seventh of January**; the *twenty-ninth* is a day-count and three state files and a prompt had hardened it into the twenty-ninth of January. Movement 3 is where the request is answered and re-asked. Closes across Volumes 2–4.
19. **Two empty answers in the delayed note. — NEW, open, and the count is fixed at two.** The eleventh and the nineteenth of January were logged with the note and no call in it. **Every earlier logged occasion had a call in it, and no chapter may raise the count beyond two or treat two empty answers as an answer.** The answer inside the note remains reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint. Closes in Volume 15.
20. **The run's own figures, and the two objects that are not one. — CARRIED, and the figures are now checkable.** **Nine at the south end and ten and a half at the north end of the bedded seam, on the tender's count, with Bevin Rook's own seven and eleven given aloud on the rule that a diver's own number only goes in if a man on a mole can check it; seven and three-eighths at the mole's root; five and a quarter sounding landward under a stone quay.** **Nine and a quarter feet is the *Aster Gannet's* ferry plate in its coaming beyond the eleventh seam, sounded on the twenty-seventh of November, and the plate and the bedded seam may never be fused.** No chart, no forum and no form for a run under a town. Closes nowhere in this volume; it is the volume's opened pressure and the material of the Volume 2 midpoint's coast road and the Volume 7 chart question.

**Review artifact.** The Batch 0001 review returned **FIX**; **the reviewer's own log is not kept in this repository, and the old citation is withdrawn rather than satisfied by an invented file.** **Its disposition is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0001 review repairs* and the short form in `state/current.md` § *Batch 0001 review repairs*.** Twelve required changes, eight prose and state improvements and three planning-file residue items were applied in place; three calendar errors and one wrong word count the review did not flag were found while verifying its grid and fixed on the same pass; and **the review's own corrected length figure is recorded as wrong, because it dropped Chapter 51 at 3,645 and so reported three chapters past the guideline when four were.** No stored review artifact exists for this batch in `reviews/`, which is the same gap already recorded for Volume 01 Batch 0002 and Batch 0004.

## Volume 02, Batch 0002 (Chapters 61–70) — as written, Movement 2

**Volume 02 is in progress. Chapters 61–70 are written. This block marks every thread the batch moved, every thread it opened, and every author-level decision it made. No thread below is closed by a chapter unless a chapter says so on the page.**

### Threads carried from Volume 01 and Batch 0001 and moved here

1. **The supply problem. — PAID as a batch, and paid for in Chapter 65.** **Understood in two chapters and paid for in the third**, as the volume asked: the pump with a web cracked in its housing that cannot be worked *and cannot be hired out*, the air pot a third gone, the *Kittiwake* on the hard with no cradle, hired gear at a mark a tide, and the harbor's crane, barge, pump and hand-crane all on the authority's own account and not the yard's. **The paying is in somebody else's pocket: fourteen marks out of the harbor authority's own account on the first of February, and two days of a tide gang at this office's cost on the seventeenth of February.** It is not waived: the pump is not fitted before the eighth of March, the pot is a quarter of a mark worse than in January, and a crew of three men and a book's worth of hands is what a notice naming all three of them is worth here for hiring a crew. **Closes across Volumes 2 and 3 as an income, not as a repair.**
2. **The rival crew. — NEW and CARRIED. Closes across Volumes 2–3.** **Noll Tarner of the *Sea Wren*, with Wend Crale and Piet Marrow, fifteen, who cannot read.** A Free Keel crew off the slate since November for taking a job at Ashtide without telling his own crew first, which he says in two sentences and does not apologise for, broke, and with no cover. He is not a villain and does not become one. **He asks the question nobody in the Free Keel asks — whose book is the day in, and can a man come and read my dive in your book, and what is it for** — and he says out loud that a yard that puts its own errors on a public sheet and asks a fleet to check them is a thing no fleet has ever done and no fleet is going to start with. **The Free Keel yard's book is now the book of another crew's work, entered in the yard's own book as an unasked entry by the boy on the twenty-seventh of January.** Closes in Volumes 2–4, and the answer is the Assembly's charter in Volume 9 and not this batch's.
3. **The three storm-exposed recoveries. — PAID, all three, and each funded before it was attempted.** The ninth seam's mooring, worked on Friday the seventeenth of February and **in service from four o'clock**; the herring fleet's ninety fathoms of winter warp on the north wall on Monday the twenty-seventh, of which **forty feet are in the sea** on a shackle of the harbor authority's own moorings gear; and a herring skipper's ring and warp off the same wall on Tuesday the twenty-eighth. **Every one spent a day the crew cannot replace, and the yard was paid nothing for the first and a bad rate for the other two.** Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5 with the writ.
4. **The second seal and the second request. — PART, and the instrument of Volume 2 Movement 3.** **The second seal came on Friday the twenty-seventh of January, which is the twenty-ninth day from the thirtieth of December, and the yard entered the arrival and did not answer and did not refuse.** The nineteen words are on the page in the office's own type. Mara Venn priced it in the order Calder asked, as a figure and not a decision, and said *do not make me useful*. **The office's paper still says ten copies and the count is eleven, and nobody in the shed knows whether the office has not been told.** **Kest read the whole thing into the hearing record of the first of March on his own motion.** **Closes across Volumes 2–4; Movement 3 is where the request is answered and re-asked.**
5. **The coast office's word *capacity*. — PAID, in Chapter 70, and it is the batch's turn.** The answer is **Elias Voke's own statement**, written at his table on the Saturday night before the sitting, **the night of Saturday the twenty-fifth of February**, for nothing, in his own hand, read out by him in a hearing room and sent to a coast office, **and it is a document the yard did not write and cannot fix, and neither can the harbor authority.** It names the north berth, the ninth seam and Bevin Rook, and the hard at Ashtide, unanswered since the ninth of November, and the *Kittiwake* on the hard with no cradle. **The harbor authority's own statement of what it has spent since the twenty-eighth of November is read into the same record on the same morning, and the two will be read by the same man in Veyr.** **Closes in Volume 2, Movement 5, where the answer reaches Veyr with a reporting clause on it.**
6. **Elias Voke's statement of capacity. — PAID, in Chapter 70.** He wrote it himself, for nothing, in his own hand, and it is the answer to the coast office, and it costs the yard the boy's name in a public record and costs Voke nothing he can afford. **The name is entered in a hearing record at the boy's own asking and with the boy's own answer in the ledger's margin, unsummarized.** Carried to Movement 5.
7. **The ninth seam's mooring. — PAID, in Chapter 66, and the change is flagged.** **Worked on the seventeenth of February and in service from four o'clock**, at the cost of a day the yard could not sell, a diver going down twice on the same day, and a fouled shackle cast in a sleeve of the mole's own foundation stone. **The fouling on the ground line between the third and the ninth seams is untouched and is now entered in a reason in an office's own book as a fact of the office's account and not of the office's work.** See *Deliberate reconciliations* below.
8. **The hard at Ashtide. — PART, in Chapter 70.** Applied for by Voke on the ninth of November, unanswered in eleven weeks, up twice and told it is in, and named in a fisher's document going to a coast office. **Closes in Volumes 2–3, and the answer is a coast office's.**
9. **The first of March. — PAID as a date, in Chapter 70.** A Wednesday. **Forty-one boats in the basin** where the clerk said forty on the Thursday, one berth short, the north berth out of service and the one that takes a north-easter, a mooring back and not a berth, three tide-gang men off, nineteen winters unpaid, fifty fathoms of warp on a wall that wants ninety, and a coast office with its word answered by a fisher. **It is the volume's clock and it is now on the page.**
10. **The charter as a leash. — PART, and lengthened.** Kest in the hearing record of the first of March: **what comes back from Veyr decides whether there is a second season, there may not be one, and the yard's part in that is nothing.** A one-season provisional working charter is not a permission to work when the holder feels like it. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5 with the writ and the reporting clause.
11. **The delayed note. — PART.** **The eleventh logged occasion is Monday the twenty-seventh of February, and there is a call in it**, longer than the one of the twenty-sixth of December and further off, a grown man's, not written as a voice, no source. **The count of occasions logged with no call in them remains two, the eleventh and the nineteenth of January, and the count is entered as a fact and is not a question.** **Corvin Sarn enters that he heard it and cannot place it.** Two occasions were heard and not entered and neither is an occasion: the mole steps on the evening of Thursday the twenty-third of February, and the hearing room on the morning of the first of March, which is the twelfth occasion since the twenty-sixth of October and is entered nowhere. **The answer is not approached, not named and not resolved; Closes in Volume 15.**
12. **The concealment. — CARRIED, and extended on the second part only.** The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book, and not in Kest's chart notation; the false line on the unverified slate is still false; **the boy entered on the twenty-third of February that neither he nor Calder Marr has asked the other, and that two of them keeping a thing out of a book is twice as safe and half as honest, and that he would rather it was half as honest than twice as safe**; and the woman with no standing left knows what is in the anchor and has still not been asked, and in Chapter 70 she said in a room, in advance of being asked, that she is a door that has been tried and will not answer for it. **Nobody in the crew may ask the boy about it and nobody did.** Closes in Volume 9 and Volume 15.
13. **The core's maintenance. — PART, entered as owing out of the yard's own pocket for the first time.** **Eight marks and a day of a boat on Monday the twenty-seventh of February, entered in the yard's own column under a rule and NOT paid**, because it is the same figure as the eleventh of January and the whole of the point of a signal is that it is the only line in a ledger that is the same on every page, and because the eleventh of January was the harbor's money and this was not, and because a yard that cannot pay a woman's eight shillings and tenpence is not going to take a bellwright's eight out of a column a boy is owed from. **The eight stands owing with the date on it and is the first figure this yard has entered this winter knowing before it went in that it would not be paid.** Corvin Sarn came out because the ground and the gear are the harbor's, and said the line is good and the waist is the same and the scour is clean, and **that he has heard the answer nine times in five months and has never heard a voice in it.** The Bellwright stage is still Volume 5 and is not taken. Closes in Volume 5 as a craft, and the eight marks closes in Volume 2 Movement 5 in a scene that pays it.
14. **Sera Mott's half. — CARRIED, untouched.** She is not asked in this batch. She may be asked once in this volume, in Movement 3 or 4, and may say no again, and nobody may enter her name in a record without her asking. Closes across the series; the first half of the answer is Volume 5.
15. **The compartment, the dressed aperture, the custody numbers, the ten yards, the run under the town, the first echo's witness, Alden Marr. — CARRIED, all untouched.** No chapter of this batch refers to any of them, and **no line is drawn between the three points and the ruling stands.** The run's four hundred feet of figures stand as they stood on the nineteenth of January. **Alden Marr is at the far bench, is not asked for anything, and Calder Marr does not go; no fragment, no report talk, no second story.** Closes in Volumes 4, 5, 6, 9, 15 and Volume 15 respectively.

### Threads opened by Batch 0002

16. **A fisher's document answering a coast office. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.** Voke's statement of the capacity of this harbor to moor the fleet is in a hearing record, in his own hand, for nothing, and it is on its way to a coast office, **and the answer it gives is that this harbor cannot moor the fleet, and the yard did not write it and cannot fix it.** The harbor authority's own statement of what it has spent goes beside it. **Two documents, one man reading both, in a city none of them has been inside.**
17. **A ring in the sea and a stone post with a shackle in it. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5, and the echo of it runs to Volume 7.** The harbor authority supplied its own old iron because it would not buy iron in the last week of February, and the yard accepted that and worked on it, and a herring skipper lost a ring and forty-one feet of warp. **The cause is in the yard's own book in the first column, the loss in the second, with the name of the man who lost it, and the skipper asked for the cause to be said by the yard before she said it.**
18. **Fifty fathoms of warp where ninety belonged. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.** The north wall is half bare on the first of March, in a north-easter, with forty-one boats in the basin. **The money that would replace it is in an account that bought four sets of moorings and a season of warp in the fortnight before a reeve promised them in a room.**
19. **Three tide-gang men off, and one of them a Slee. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.** Rowlandike, Petch and Wil Slee, off from the twenty-fourth of February to the fourth of March, in the week before the fleet is in, because the money went on moorings. **Wil Slee's brother Tam went down twice on the seventeenth under Neale's eye and has said nothing about it.**
20. **A clerk who keeps a page instead of a box. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 3 and 4.** The harbor authority's clerk has now had four things entered in that office's book this quarter in something other than a box: a reason with no purpose on the nineteenth of January, a dive record in a boy's own hand on the seventeenth of February, a boy's answer in the margin of the ledger on the first of March, and a nineteen-word second seal read in on a reeve's own motion. **He is a man who fills in a box and says what a box does and is not an antagonist and may not become one**, and he is now the office's memory of things that were not in a box.
21. **The twenty-third of February and the first of March. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 15.** **Two** occasions the crew did not enter: the mole steps in the wind on the evening of the twenty-third, and the hearing room's ledger on the morning of the first. **There is no third and there never was an occasion on the eleventh of February; an earlier draft of this entry named three and glossed the third with an object — a collar — that appears nowhere in Chapters 61–70, and it is gone.** **A record that is not entered is not a record of nothing, and the crew now has a habit of not entering things that a bellwright is standing next to.**
22. **The two hundred and sixty-nine words and the four hundred feet. — CARRIED from Batch 0001, and untouched.** The run under the town is not referred to in this batch. Closes nowhere in this volume; it is the volume's opened pressure and the material of the Volume 2 midpoint's coast road and the Volume 7 chart question.
23. **Neave Tarrant, in the third lay, nine winters, out in the blow. — NEW, open. Closes in Volume 2 Movement 5.** He is not back until Wednesday the first of March and a boy who cannot read said his name in a shed and nobody said anything.

### Author-level decisions taken in this batch

- **The power rules are unchanged and no stage is taken.** Calder does not dive in this batch, and the reason he gives for it on the mole on the seventeenth of February is that a notice stopped him. **What changes is method, not rank, and the batch's clearest instance of it is the two-column convention** — what a man on a mole can check, and what he cannot, with a reason against the second — **which is now a written practice in the yard's book, used on a rival crew's dive, on a sounding, on a ring seen in an eddy, and on forty feet of marline in the sea.** A stated reason is entered before anybody is hired and unedited; a limit set by the diver at the boat is entered as such; a tender is named who is not the diver; the cutoff is the tender's; and **the man who called a day in January is on a mole in February writing down somebody else's dive.**
- **The consent rules are unchanged and are under load for a second time.** Another crew's day is in the yard's book, a copy of it went out to a man to keep, the count is published and now out of date in two offices at once, and **the record of this harbor's own ground is described in a fisher's document going to a coast office, with the name of a boy who is owed forty marks in it.** No seal, limit or refusal is overridden anywhere in the batch and no emergency access is used.
- **The mystery chain is unchanged and not advanced.** No chapter explains the Quietening, identifies the Crown Seal, names the Nine Harbors, explains the nine-line mark, proves who altered the manifest, reveals what the sealed case held, names the Office of Returning Memory, or puts a senior examiner in a room. **The run under the town is not touched at all in these ten chapters.** **Ilya Serr, Halven Rook and Captain Veyra Holt do not appear anywhere in the batch, on paper or otherwise, and Captain Holt is not named and has not sent a paper.**
- **The ending is untouched and no new antagonist, institution or final enemy appears.** The only new named people are ordinary working people: Noll Tarner, Wend Crale, Piet Marrow, Edric Pringle, Neave Tarrant, and a man off the *Anna Beth*. **The Volume 2 midpoint is not in this batch and Movement 2 does not speak of a coast road.** No conflict with a naval cutter occurs and no confiscation of a working boat occurs; both are Movement 3.
- **A shape and length record, measured and not defended. The total is corrected by the Volume 02 audit.** **31,300 words across ten chapters, re-measured after the Batch 0002 review repair; the 30,995 that stood here was measured before the repair and was never recomputed, and the per-chapter figures for Chapters 61, 63, 65 and 68 are corrected to 3,024 / 3,195 / 3,498 / 2,790.** **Six inside the 2,200–3,200 guideline and four past it**, the largest Chapter 70 at about eleven per cent over, and **no chapter in the batch is two rooms.** The hearing room is used once, in Chapter 70, and the batch's pressures alternate: supply, a rival crew, ordinary work, an office, money and a boy, a closure and a record, aftermath, labour in weather, a recovery that pays, and a sitting. **This is the same ratio as Batch 0001's five inside and five past with the largest at fourteen per cent over, and the largest chapter here is smaller.**
- **A correction to a state figure, made on the page rather than in a file.** The Free Keel yard's copy count was **eleven** from the eighteenth of January, and the office's paper of the thirtieth of December and the naval rating's slate of December both say **ten**, and **the batch does not resolve which of the three states of knowledge is the true one — not told, told and not corrected, told and left standing — and enters the not-knowing as the third of three facts with no opinion in it.** That is a discipline, not a canon change, and it is now binding.

### Deliberate reconciliations, flagged rather than hidden

1. **The ninth seam's mooring is worked and in service from four o'clock on Friday the seventeenth of February**, which changes the position it stood in at the volume's opening and in Chapters 51, 53, 57, 58 and 60. **A mooring is not a berth: the north berth is still out of service, one berth is still short on the first of March, and the fouling on the ground line between the third and the ninth seams is untouched and is now on the page in a reason in the harbor authority's own book.** The volume's clock is unchanged. Recorded here and in `state/continuity.md` rather than smoothed.
2. **Two occasions heard and not entered** (the mole steps on the evening of Thursday the twenty-third of February, the hearing room on the morning of the first of March) and **two more that are not occasions at all**, the eleventh and the nineteenth of January, which were logged with the note only and no call. **The count of logged occasions is eleven and the count of occasions logged with no call in them is two, and neither count is raised in this batch.**

**Review artifact.** Batch 0002 has now been reviewed and it returned **FIX**; **the reviewer's own log is not kept in this repository, and the old citation is withdrawn rather than satisfied by an invented file.** Its disposition is the "Batch 0002 review repairs" section of `state/continuity.md` and the short form in `state/current.md`. Twelve findings, six of them required before Batch 0003 runs, were applied in place: the money figures in Chapters 65 and 68, four impossible weekday/date pairings in Chapters 61, 67 and 70, the February calendar paragraph here and in `state/continuity.md`, the queued `batch-0003/PROMPT.md` calendar from 18 March on, the three unentered bell occasions (two of which had never been written), and a false claim about a missing marker. No review artifact is stored in `reviews/`, which is the same gap already recorded for Volume 01 Batch 0002 and Batch 0004.
