## Volume 02, Batch 0005 (Chapters 91–100) — as written, Movement 5, the rescue and the writ. **VOLUME 02 IS CLOSED.**

**Volume 02, Chapters 51–100, is written and closed by this batch. This block marks every thread the batch moved, every thread it opened, and every author-level decision it made. No thread below is closed by a chapter unless a chapter says so on the page, and the volume's own resolution is now on the page in full.**

### Threads carried from Volume 01 and Batches 0001–0004 and moved here

1. **The circuit office's fourth request of the sixteenth of March. — CARRIED, and it is now shadowed by a writ.** It was still unanswered and unrefused at the end of Movement 4 and nothing in this batch answers or refuses it. **What changes is that a party with no instrument can no longer be asked for its book, because a party with an instrument has an instrument, and the writ of the first of May has a reporting clause on it that asks for findings and not for a book.** The yard's objection to the clause is entered unsummarized and goes up the coast. **The request itself is not answered and is not refused, and the yard's position has changed underneath it.** Closes across Volumes 2–4; the volume that answers it is Volume 4.
2. **The public record as a thing other people hold. — PAID as a method, and it is now six hands and a mouth.** The tenth thing on the market wall carries the count of the copies of the public sheet — eleven, unmoved, with the four unreached holders named — and a slip went up under it and a shilling changed hands for it. **Six hand-copied copies went out in six hands of six boats, none of them the yard's, and the yard's own wall sheet says so, and the boy who reads the book is not one of the six writers and is a mouth and not a hand.** Noll Tarner's answer to his own month-old question has been made into a practice: a record of a dock is for the people who come after you. Closes nowhere; it runs to Volume 15.
3. **The count of the copies, out of date in two offices. — CARRIED, static, and stated on a wall for the fourth time.** Eleven on the yard's wall and in the circuit office's type, ten on a naval slate, both standing, and nothing on a market wall changes either. Closes in Volume 03 and the echo of it runs to Volume 7's chart question.
4. **The supply problem. — PAID as its opposite. The pump is at the second lay and then on the mole and then in a fire, and the yard has no air, no gland and no hose.** The pump went out on the twenty-eighth at four marks to the harbor authority at the yard's own rate, was the yard's reason for standing on ground the notice closes, and was paid. **The pot of air bought on the seventh of April is opened on the morning of the twenty-eighth and is the whole of the air in this harbor, and it is empty on the Friday night.** The yard makes a hand lead's line at night and does it wrong once. Closes as a repair in Volume 03 and the money for the gland and the hose is Volume 03's first job.
5. **The rival crew, Noll Tarner. — PAID, and turned for the last time in this volume, and he is still not a villain.** He reads the ninth thing and again the tenth, **says the word *network* out loud on a market wall on the third of May and is refused it in front of about forty people**, and then supplies the better form of the volume's new question in his own mouth — *the question is who edits the charts, and it is a question about a man with a pen* — and it goes in the first column because a man in a market can be asked again tomorrow. **He is never thanked and does not ask to be.** He and his crew remain at the pile. Closes in Volumes 3–4.
6. **Elias Voke's statement of capacity. — PAID as a person.** He names the price of a night's work at a day's rate in his own words, twelve marks, **refuses to let it be called a payment for a bell**, and asks a clerk to check that the February warp and the Friday warp are not the same fault, and he is in the hearing room on the first of May and says out loud that the writ is a longer leash and that he would have given a good deal to be wrong about the second season. **The statement of capacity itself is still on its way to a coast office and has not been answered.** Closes in Volume 03.
7. **The charter as a leash. — PAID. The provisional writ of the first of May is taken in its place and is a longer leash with a name on it.** It runs to the first of March next, it is inside the harbor line, the four-hundred-yard bar off the outer marks stands, the ground is named, **and its last line says the harbor authority is not the office that can renew it, that the second season is not its to give, and that nothing in the instrument says there is a second season.** The four-day reporting clock has started. Closes as the leash in Volume 03, where the reporting clause's first returns fall due.
8. **The hard at Ashtide. — CARRIED.** Unanswered since the ninth of November and named in the position on the third of May. Closes in Volume 03, and the answer is a coast office's.
9. **The ninth seam's mooring, the north berth, the warp, the third lay, the mole-head light. — PAID, MOVED, and one of them is paid for out of the yard's own action.** The ninth seam's mooring **is out of service from the first of May with no end of it entered, and a man of thirty-one nearly drowned on it on the twenty-eighth of April and is alive, and the yard enters that the notice did not save him and will not say that it did and will not say the notice was not worth the paper it is on.** The north berth took a sea on the night of the twenty-eighth with fifty fathoms of warp on it. **The mole-head light is not on and will not be, and Corvin Sarn names the consequence in a shed in front of four men: a bell in the dark is a bell that is not there.** Closes in Volume 03.
10. **A wage at four marks a week. — PAID IN PART, and this is the resolution's second payment.** **Sixteen marks go out of four hands in a market on Saturday the twenty-ninth of April, and not one mark of the sixteen out of the yard's own work, and the boy takes the ninth, tenth, eleventh and twelfth weeks of April — four lines — and leaves the fifth of March, the oldest line in the column, because he is not going to take a man backwards in his own book.** No wage money moved out of this yard on the twenty-second of April or on any other day in the batch. The column closes **four lines deep at sixteen marks**, the same figure and the same depth as on the thirty-first of March, and nobody in the crew asks him one word about it. **The arrears are not waived: the fifth, sixth, seventh and eighth weeks of March are still in that column and the forty marks of claim stand whole.** Closes in Volume 03 and the fifth of March is carried as a named line.
11. **The forty marks twice. — CARRIED. Both stand whole and both are unpaid and the market money did not touch either.** **The writ's reporting clause cannot reach Mara Venn, and she says so in a hearing room before anybody asks her, and says she has a paper of her own going up the coast on her own account with three clauses about a false seal and a boat and three questions about a chart, and that she is not an assistant and is not going to be one.** She holds to the twenty-second of March. Closes in Volume 4 at her break, and the debt's last payment is later.
12. **The bellwright's eight marks and the going out. — PAID. This is the resolution's first payment and it closes the thread.** Eight marks and a day of a boat go out of four hands in a market on Saturday the twenty-ninth of April, **and not one mark of it out of the yard's own work, and the yard entered that fact because in three years a man in a city is going to find the bellwright's name in a wage column.** Corvin Sarn refuses to have it in a wage column, refuses to be thanked, refuses to make a repair or a finding out of the frame cut through the casting, refuses to have the number in a lead plug written on a slate, and refuses to take the plug out of the room on purpose. **The going out is done on Tuesday the second of May in a flat calm, and the going out recurs in about four years at eight marks and a day of a boat, and the yard has told the four of them now so that there is no argument about it in four years.** Closes as a figure in Volume 02; the craft closes in Volume 5.
13. **Mara Venn. — CARRIED, and she is expensive and right and does not break.** She says four times in a room, before anybody asks, that the writ cannot reach her and that she is not of it; she holds the forty marks; she sends a paper of her own up the coast with her own name on it and does not tell the yard what it says; and **she is not the yard's, not an assistant, and is not going to be one this afternoon.** **Her break is Volume 4 and it is not near.**
14. **The concealment. — CARRIED, hidden, and the volume closes with it hidden.** The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. **The false line on the unverified slate is still false and the slate was not moved.** The boy has not asked and has not been asked, and on the twenty-ninth of April **he entered that he has been to the fishery twice since the twenty-first of March and had his hand open the whole of both times and had not put that down, and called it a second omission, and entered both omissions in the same breath so that nobody can come in four years and say he hid one and not the other** — and nobody in that crew asked him one word about it, then or afterwards. And the holder came within four inches of asking the conservator again and did not, and a shipwright said from the door that he has asked four people nothing this month and has been told so once. Closes in Volume 9 and Volume 15.
15. **The delayed note. — CARRIED, and the count is protected explicitly for the last time.** **Eleven logged occasions, two empty answers, neither raised, and no occasion is entered in this batch.** The harbor's tide bell goes on the morning of the twenty-eighth of April and on the afternoon of the second of May, and **the boy takes the figure for the delay himself at about two o'clock on Tuesday the second of May, in the same hour as the bell and on the same flat calm, and reads it back twice, and the third column of the book says on the page that this is a figure and not an occasion, that a figure taken on purpose with a slate and a stopwatch is not a note heard out of the weather, and that a boy who has counted eleven of them is not going to make it twelve by writing a number.** The delay **did not move** in three months and a half and nobody in this harbor can say why. The answer inside the note is not approached, not named and not resolved. Closes in Volume 15.
16. **Alden Marr. — CARRIED, untouched, and the volume closes on the not-going.** He is at the far bench in six of the ten chapters, is not asked for anything, **the bench moves under the drip on the twenty-sixth of April and the two of them are in one room for nine hours in a gale and do not speak, and Calder Marr does not go over to him, and that is the relationship and it is not a plot point and it is not paid off in this volume.** No fragment, no report talk, no second story. Closes in Volume 9 and Volume 15.
17. **Sera Mott. — CARRIED and NOT ASKED AGAIN.** She was asked once in Volume 02, on Sunday the ninth of April, and refused, and her name is not in the yard's book at her asking, and **she is not asked in this batch and the volume may not ask her again.** Closes across the series; the first half of the answer is Volume 5 and the argument is Volume 11.
18. **The compartment, the dressed aperture, the custody numbers, the ten yards, the run under the town, the first echo's witness, the plate dug on the mole's toe in February. — CARRIED, all untouched.** **No chapter of this batch refers to any of them.** The run's four hundred feet of figures stand as they stood on the nineteenth of January. **No line is drawn between the three points and the ruling stands. No custody object is asked for and none returns, the municipal claim on the third item stands, and the yard will not make a request. Ilya Serr, Halven Rook and Captain Veyra Holt do not appear in a room, and the first two appear only on the reporting clause as a seal line and a requesting magistrate, and Holt appears only as a signature.** Closes in Volumes 4, 5, 6, 9, 10 and 15.

### Threads opened by Batch 0005

19. **A provisional writ with a reporting clause, and a four-day clock. — NEW, and it is the volume's leash. Closes across Volumes 3–5 and its reporting returns are Volume 03's first job.** Granted by the harbor authority on Monday the first of May, in the harbor authority's seal and not a naval one, running to the first of March next, naming four persons of whom the fourth is a hired diver of the *Grey Widow* who was asked in person and said yes, naming its ground, carrying the eighth and the share, and **expressly not the Second Bell license, not a charter, and not a renewal of anything.** The Crown attaches a reporting clause: every finding of worked glass and every raising of claimed material within four days, with place, hour, keeper and limits, the office's power to seat one of two disputants and take the finding as given, and production on demand. **The yard takes it, and enters its objection unsummarized, and says out loud that both facts are true and neither cancels the other.** **A Volume 03 chapter inherits: the first four-day return, the office's power to seat one of two men and take what a person says, and the question of whether a party can hold a paper and be honest at the same time.**
20. **A man found alive in five fathoms off the inner shoals, and the notice that did not save him. — NEW, and the volume's moral centre. Closes nowhere; it runs to Volume 4 and to the volume that answers the notice.** Ivo Trethewey, thirty-one, of the second lay, at the top of the make on the twenty-ninth of March, found on Friday the twenty-eighth of April, alive, with a shackle of the ninth seam's own mooring in a hand that would not open, having gone down to that mooring without an instrument and without a permit. **The yard entered three lines under the two columns: that he was not lawful there, that the notice did not save him and the yard will not say it did, and that the yard will not say the notice was not worth the paper it is on — and both of those are in one page and neither is a reason to touch the closure.** **Volume 03 inherits a notice that is not lifted, is not to be argued, and has just been shown, on a body, to be worth nothing and worth everything.**
21. **A lead plug with a number in it, in a chest in a shed, in no book. — NEW, and it is the volume's opened physical object. Closes across Volumes 3–6 and its echo runs to the plate dug on the mole's toe in February.** Two parts of a chain in a lead plug, a clean half-inch drilled hole, a number of which only a two and a bit of another one and a fourth that is not a two are visible, cut by a man with a breast drill and a steady arm, about eleven inches under a bell at eight and a half feet in a stone mole in a working harbor, and carried in Corvin Sarn's coat since the twenty-seventh of February. **He will not say what the number is and will not let it be written on a slate, and then he refuses to take the plug out of the room on purpose, and four men describe it and the four descriptions do not agree, and the reason against the whole of it is the shortest line in the yard's book: a thing in this yard's book that this yard has not got is worth more to it than the same thing in a drawer.** **The Oren mark and the eleven copies are three custody items in the circuit office and a plug in a chest in Morrow Quay, and the two are not connected and must not be connected.**
22. **A glass storm, written in the second column, and nobody may name a cause for it. — NEW, and it is the volume's standing hazard. Closes across Volumes 3–6.** On Wednesday the twenty-sixth of April, in nineteen hours of north-north-east, sound, light and perspective reordered around overloaded glass: a boy on a roof heard his own voice behind him in a yard where nobody had said anything; a man of fifty-one with a bad wheel of his own was told twice, flat and out loud, to come his wheel round, while he had it in both hands; and a diver in five fathoms of water heard a call out of the port quarter of his own head, three fathoms above the water, unhurried and not out loud. **It came through the water and not off it, it is in all of it, and the delay on the call is not given and the yard does not intend to invent one.** **Volume 03 inherits a physical and psychological hazard and not a monster, and it may be used again, and it may never become a voice that tells anybody anything.**
23. **Six hand-copied copies in six hands, and a wall that names no holder. — NEW, and it is the volume's answer to Harl Dace's question. Closes nowhere; it runs to Volume 5 and Volume 9.** The coast road is published in six boats, in six hands, none of them the yard's, **and the wall says the party asks no person to do anything with it and will not be asked.** One copy carries a line the wall has not got, in the objector's own hand: *and I have not been to it since the fifteenth of April and I am not going and that is mine and not yours.* **Volume 03 inherits six men with a piece of paper each and no obligation, and a refusal of the word *network* on a public wall, and a question about charts that is a question about a man with a pen.**
24. **A man of fifty-one with a bad wheel of his own, at the moment there is a bell in the water. — NEW, small, and it carries into Volume 03. Closes in Volume 03.** He is of the *Grey Widow*, he cannot place a sound, he is better on a lead than the boy and is not put on a lead, does not know that is why, has not been told, and has asked not to be told. **His bad wheel is his own and it is not Coll Ferrow's: the *Kestrel's Sister*'s bad wheel is that boat's and is the reason her man of thirty-one has not been out of this harbor in nine days, and the two wheels are two wheels in two boats and no chapter may fuse them.** He is in the barge on the wrong side of the mole during the rescue and had the wrong idea about where to look, and Nessa Pike walks him down a hill and not to talk. **Corvin Sarn's line — a bell in the dark is a bell that is not there — is Sarn's own, said in a shed doorway in Chapter 96 as he takes the eight marks and again in Chapter 100 from the ladder, and Dace's line for the second column in Chapter 98 is about the water and the going out and is his own.**
25. **Ewan Tristram's wharfinger's office and the cistern on a rota. — NEW, small, and it is a town. Closes in Volume 03.** The second lay's dues and keys, a shed with shavings in it, a cistern charged on a Monday and a Thursday by a man who walks out to a sluice, **and the man did not walk out on Monday because the harbor authority cannot pay a man to walk out to a sluice**, and a handbell rung from the mole head by a man whose tide gang is four men short. **A Volume 03 chapter may use the harbor authority's municipal poverty as a pressure and may not have it fixed for nothing.**
26. **A yard's third column, for what the boy said when he was not in the boat. — NEW, and it is a method. Closes nowhere; it runs to Volume 15.** Created on Tuesday the second of May out of the writ's four-day clause and a boy's hand that is going: the four men in the boat cannot check him, the column carries the time it was read on the steps and the time it was written in the book, and the difference between the two is the whole of what is being fixed. **It may not be applied retroactively to any date before the second of May 2026, and it is of the yard's book and not of any office's.**

### Author-level decisions taken in this batch

- **The power rules are unchanged and no stage is taken.** Calder does not enter anything and no power stage of any kind is taken and none may be. **What the volume pays is one rescue and one going out, and the rescue is done entirely by the method Volume 01 and Volume 02 built — a reason entered before anybody is wet, two divers named as not of the party, each of them fixing his own limit at the boat, a named tender who is not a diver, a cutoff he can pull without asking anybody, a two-column record afterward with a reason against every line of the second, and other people in the room who know why, four of whom read it back and a fifth who was not asked.** The condition is **not** named in this batch and the name is not used anywhere in Chapters 91–100.
- **The consent rules are under load for the fifth time and hold, and the batch adds two forms.** A claim seal with a boat's name on it is published unsigned and uncertified and no office in this harbor will sign it. A lead plug in a chest is described four times by four men and the number in it is written nowhere. A hat at a fish market is put in the second column because no office in this coast can be asked about the man who carried it. **No seal, limit or refusal is overridden anywhere in the batch and no emergency access is used, and the writ's reporting clause is accepted with a written objection rather than by being ignored.**
- **The mystery chain is advanced by exactly one step and no more.** A road that no current chart includes is published in six hands in six boats and is doubted and half-believed, **and it is not explained, not named as a network, not connected to anything under the town, and not resolved, and the word *network* is used once by a rival and refused in public.** **The compartment is not referred to and the three-step finding is not restated. The dressed aperture and the fouling that gives no hour are not referred to. The nine-line mark is not explained and the Nine Harbors, the Quietening and the Crown Seal are not named. What was in the sealed case is not revealed, the manifest is not proved altered, the Office of Returning Memory is not named, and no senior examiner is in a room.** The lead plug is opened as a physical object with a number in it and is **not** identified and **not** connected to the plate dug on the mole's toe in February, and the two must never be fused.
- **The ending is untouched and no new antagonist, institution or final enemy appears.** The only new named people are ordinary working people: **Ewan Tristram, wharfinger of the second lay; Ivo Trethewey, a second-lay man of thirty-one found alive; Ambrose Sell, a clerk of the circuit office and the mouth of a clause, not an antagonist and not to become one.** **Ilya Serr appears once, on a seal line on a sheet nobody signs by hand; Halven Rook is named once as the magistrate whose office requested the clause; Captain Veyra Holt is a signature.** No face is in a room.
- **A shape and length record, measured and not defended, and corrected on review.** **33,327 words across ten chapters after the Batch 0005 review repair; four inside the 2,200–3,200 guideline and six past it**, the largest Chapter 97 at about twenty-four per cent over and Chapter 95 at about twenty-three, and the four others past are Chapters 100, 98, 96 and 94. **Every chapter past the guideline is one complete room or one complete day, and three of them carry more than one room on that day — Chapter 92 is the shed and the market boards, Chapter 98 is the net loft, the mole's steps and the shed, and Chapter 100 is the boards and the head of the dock.** The claim that *no chapter is two rooms* was made in three state files and is **withdrawn**: it was never true of Chapter 98, which the continuity file itself conceded in one breath, and no cost is paid twice in any of the three. The prompt's instruction to let a scene run rather than drop a cost the crew pays was taken for the climax, the sitting, the bell and the wall. **The hearing room is used twice — for the office's own ledger on Thursday the twenty-seventh of April and for the one sitting on Monday the first of May — and the market wall is used once, on Wednesday the third of May, and no two of the three are the same day.** The batch's pressures alternate: a fire, a hand and a market, weather, an office and a paper, the water, money and four causes, a sitting, a bell, a going out, and a wall.
- **A correction to an inherited pronoun, made on the page and not in an earlier chapter. The record of it was itself wrong on review and is corrected here.** **Edric Pringle is a woman** — Chapters 91–100 use *she* and *her* throughout and give her the *Anna Beth*, the second lay, fifty-four years of age, a woman who has been difficult about money and who says the cause out loud first. **The wrong pronouns are seven occurrences and not two: Chapter 62 line 71, *a skipper called Pringle who has not paid me for it since November and knows exactly what he is doing*; and Chapter 69 line 29, *from Edric Pringle, on his own ring and his own warp and his own account*, and Chapter 69 line 71, *for one day's work on his own ring and his own warp and his own account*, each of those two carrying the phrase three times.** **Chapter 63 has no wrong pronoun at all — it says *a herring skipper called Edric Pringle* — and the earlier state files, which named Chapters 63 and 69 and said *twice*, were wrong and are corrected.** **No writer phase may edit Chapters 62 or 69; the discrepancy is recorded here and in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0005 review repairs* for the volume audit, which may edit a chapter mechanically, to rule on the canon and on the repair.**
- **Two clauses of `outline/volume-02.md` are discharged in a different form than the outline describes, and the volume audit must rule on both. This is a divergence and not a canon change, and it is recorded here rather than left under a claim that the batch makes no canon change and flags only a pronoun.** The **climax**: the outline asks for a rescue of a trapped fishing crew during a glass storm that destroys the equipment needed to claim a legal salvage award; the chapters gave **one man and not a crew**, a glass storm on the twenty-sixth and a rescue on the twenty-eighth **in an easing north-northeast three days later**, the destruction is of the yard's own gear, the paid official route was **interrupted rather than abandoned** because a claim window is an office instrument and the yard was never permitted there as a workman at all, and **nothing was raised, so no salvage award was ever claimable and the volume does not pretend otherwise.** The **resolution**: the outline asks that the coast road become evidence of a concealed network on a public wall; the chapters **refuse the word in public, use it once from a rival and refuse it, and put a question about a man with a pen on the wall instead.** The road is not resolved, not explained and not connected to anything under the town, and both forms are defensible. The full record is in `state/continuity.md` § 9 of the Batch 0005 section.
- **The volume closes hidden, as lock L4 requires.** The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation; the one false line on the unverified slate is still false; the boy has not asked and has not been asked and has now entered a second omission of his own; and the woman with no standing who knows what is in the anchor has still never been asked. **It was told once, in Chapter 38, and it is not told again here.**

### What Volume 02 answered, and what it opened, in one place

**Answered, on the page.** The crew has a paper and it is a longer leash with a name on it. The bellwright's eight marks are paid. Sixteen marks of a boy's wage are paid out of four men's pockets and the yard's own debt to the conservator is not paid and cannot be reached by the writ. A fire party takes four hundred feet of a woman's warp off her own wharf and the cause is said first by her and entered by the reeve. A man in five fathoms of water is alive, and the notice that closed his ground is not lifted, and the yard has entered that it did not save him and will not argue about it either way. A coast road no current chart includes is on a market wall under a naval claim in six hands and a mouth, and the yard has refused to put a name on it. The chandler's note is not re-written and that is said out loud. **A seal is false and a witness is real and both are in the same book, and the bell is a bell and it needs a boat.**

**Opened, and owed to Volume 03 by name.** *Who edits the sea charts, and why do all the missing roads converge on a place seventy fathoms down?* Also open: the first four-day return under the writ and the office's power to seat one of two men and take what a person says; a lead plug with a number in it in a chest in Morrow Quay and the plate dug on the mole's toe in February and the fact that neither is connected to anything and both are on the same ground; a man of fifty-one with a bad wheel who is not put on a lead and does not know why; Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found; the second season, which is not the harbor authority's to give and will come out of a city from a man who has never been inside this harbor; the hard at Ashtide, unanswered since the ninth of November; nineteen winters of dues; a compressor gland, ten feet of hose and a hand lead's line to buy; and a boy who is to be off the stone on the fifteenth of May whatever his own arithmetic says.

---

## Volume 02 close accounting — as AUDITED, and the Volume 03 handoff

**AUDITED 26 September 2026. This supersedes the Batch 0005 account of itself above and every other batch account, wherever they differ. The audit is `reviews/volume-02/volume-02-close.md`; the consolidated rulings are in `state/continuity.md` under *Rulings of the Volume 02 volume audit*.**

**Nothing is owed to Volume 02 and nothing is owed to a chapter that has not been written.** Ten author-level decisions are **ruled**, not deferred again, and each is dated.

### What Volume 02 answered, on the page

A provisional writ of salvage working, granted Monday the first of May, with **four names on it and a Crown reporting clause attached**, and a four-day clock that has started. **The bellwright's eight marks paid** out of four hands in a market and not one mark out of the yard's own work. **Sixteen marks of a boy's wage arrears paid out of somebody else's pocket**, four lines, with the fifth of March left in the column on purpose. **The yard's own debt to Mara Venn not paid, not paid out of the market money, and not reachable by the writ.** **A Kest payment that cost somebody who is not the yard**: four hundred feet of a woman's own warp, a mooring out of service with no end of it entered, a tide-gang man stood off to mind a cart. **The chandler's note not re-written, said out loud on a market wall on the third of May.** **Six hand-copied copies in six boats and a refusal of the word *network* in public.** **A man found alive in five fathoms off the inner shoals, and the notice that closed his ground not lifted and not to be argued.** **A seal proved forged and a witness proved real, both on a public wall, and neither a reason to believe the other.**

### The three things the audit ruled that changed a position

1. **The volume's central pressure is RESOLVED, and in the opposite direction to the opening.** The leash did not shorten; it lengthened, acquired a name and four persons, and now carries a reporting clock and a Crown signature. **The volume's own sentence is the better one and the outline is not amended.**
2. **The climax clause is DISCHARGED IN ANOTHER FORM, and this is stated plainly: there was no charter to abandon, the man was one and not a crew, the glass storm is the twenty-sixth of April and the rescue the twenty-eighth in an easing north-northeast, the official route was interrupted rather than abandoned, and NO SALVAGE AWARD WAS EVER CLAIMABLE, because nothing was raised. A clerk's box cannot hold the difference between a window that was worked and a window that was missed.** The outline's clause is not satisfied in its facts and is satisfied in its argument, **and neither is an unspent debt.**
3. **The resolution clause is DISCHARGED IN ANOTHER FORM.** The word *network* is used once, by a rival, and refused in public; **the volume's new question is about charts and about a man with a pen and not about a system**; and the convergence on Orison is refused by the yard in its own mouth. **The road is not resolved and no later volume may explain it, name it a network, or connect it to anything under the town.**

### Of the twenty-four locks: none breached

One is **superseded by the page** (L3: the count of logged occasions is eleven, not eight, and is now frozen), one is **ruled superseded by the chapters** (L21: the charter **expired** on the thirty-first of March and was not renewed, and the writ is a new instrument and not a renewal), one has a **corrected element** (L8: **the tongue is not on the frame**), and one is **withdrawn and replaced** (L22: no chapter is two rooms). **The plan for the fifth seam is a PART and is assigned to Volume 03.**

### Ten author-level decisions, RULED

1. **The Edric Pringle pronoun — SHE IS A WOMAN AND CHAPTERS 62 AND 69 ARE REPAIRED.** The state files recorded seven wrong pronouns in three places; **the true count is ten, in four places, and every one is corrected on the page.** **With it, ten British spellings — *harbour* four times in Chapter 91 and five times in Chapter 93, *favour* once in Chapter 97 — are repaired in place, in a book that writes *harbor* 512 times and *favor* 8. Twenty repairs in five chapters, all mechanical, and no word count moved: Batch 0005 is 33,327 and Volume 02 is 148,437.**
2. **The lead plug — RULED, with an owner and a closing volume.** Not a custody object, not one of the three items, not connected to the ferry plate dug in February or to the Oren mark or to the panel or to the road. **Owner: Corvin Sarn. The number is written nowhere. Thread across Volumes 3–6.**
3. **The glass storm — RULED, and no cause may be named for it in any later volume.** A physical and psychological hazard, not a monster, in the second column with a reason, and it may never become a voice.
4. **The third column — RULED a method and not a rule, and it may not be applied retroactively to any date before the second of May 2026.** It is of the yard's book and of no office's.
5. **The writ's four-day clock — RULED in the terms a chapter may and may not use it**, and the only office that may renew the writ is the harbor authority.
6. **The man of fifty-one with a bad wheel — RULED A THREAD AND ASSIGNED TO VOLUME 03.** His wheel is his own and is not Coll Ferrow's; he is not to be interviewed by Calder Marr; the storm's two flat instructions are in a yard's book and the yard has not asked him about them.
7. **The deliberate soft points — each ruled or assigned to a named volume.** **Captain Holt's posting and the channel to a Morrow Quay reeve are Volume 03's decision and may not be invented elsewhere.** **The first echo's witness stays where he is and Volume 03 is not the volume that needs him.** **The abandoned Oren lead is Volume 05's and Volume 02 planted none.** **Lira Venn's volume is a series-level allocation and no chapter in any volume may use her as a device until it is made.** **The teaching question is ruled to Volumes 5, 9 and 11.**
8. **The three bare items — RULED, and the sentence about a municipal claim and a presumed Crown material is a live instrument and was not answered by a clerk.**
9. **The plate dug on the mole's toe in February and the loose iron — RULED, and the answer is that they are not in this volume.** New appearances in Volume 03, on the same ground as the plug, and **not connected to it.**
10. **What a batch flagged and did not repair — ruled on each and inherited forward**, including the withdrawal of the "no chapter is two rooms" rule and the inheritance of the Batch 0005 repair whole.

### Every thread Volume 02 opened, marked at the volume boundary

- **The writ and the four-day clock — PAID as an instrument, CARRIED as a clock.** First return falls due in Volume 03 and has never been tested. **Closes across Volumes 3–5.**
- **A lead plug with a number in it — CARRIED.** Thread across Volumes 3–6, and its echo runs to the plate dug in February. **The two may never be fused.**
- **A glass storm — CARRIED, and no cause may be named for it.**
- **Six hand-copied copies in six boats, and a wall that names no holder — CARRIED.** Six men with a piece of paper each and no obligation. Runs to Volumes 5 and 9.
- **A man of fifty-one with a bad wheel — CARRIED, assigned to Volume 03.**
- **A yard's third column — CARRIED.** A method, not a rule, and not retroactive.
- **Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found — CARRIED, untouched.** There is one line in the yard's book saying there is nothing in it, and the yard has not gone and has asked nobody anything.
- **The eighth-clause plan for the fifth seam — CARRIED and OUTSTANDING. Volume 03 must answer it and the refusal has to cost somebody who is not the yard.**
- **The fourth request of the circuit office of the sixteenth of March — CARRIED, unanswered and unrefused.** The volume that answers it is Volume 4.
- **The hard at Ashtide — CARRIED, unanswered since the ninth of November.** The answer is a coast office's.
- **The second season — CARRIED as a named absence.** A Volume 03 chapter may name the absence and may not manufacture a renewal.
- **The boy off the stone on Monday the fifteenth of May, and the bonesetter's June — CARRIED.**
- **The gland, ten feet of hose and no air — CARRIED, and the money for the first two is Volume 03's first problem.**
- **The coast road and the question about charts — CARRIED, and not resolved.** The word *network* is not to be put in the yard's hand again.
- **The notice of the thirty-first and the man in five fathoms — CARRIED, not lifted and not to be argued.**
- **A compressor gland, a hand lead's line and a delivery hose to buy — CARRIED.**
- **The court of claims, the reason for the seizure of the fourteenth of October, the custody items, the aperture, the nine-line mark, the first echo's witness, Alden Marr's silence, the ferry plate, and the run under the town — ALL CARRIED, all untouched by Volume 02, all owed to the volumes named in their own locks.**

### The audit's own first pass, recorded so that no later phase re-derives a wrong figure

**Three of the Volume 02 audit's own figures were wrong and are corrected, and the most consequential had already been copied into the three Volume 03 files the audit had then created. The ruling in all three is the volume's own authority rule: the chapters decide a date, and the planning files are corrected to them.**

1. **Two slate-wall wipes that no chapter gives.** `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0005 as written* placed a wipe on Monday the twenty-fourth of April and put the chalk figure on that day; **there is none, and the only wipe named in Batch 0005 is Monday the first of May.** And the audit's own first pass placed a wipe on Monday the first of March 2026, because the first of March is a Wednesday and was the sitting day. **Chapter 70 says the wall had been blank since the cloth came off it at eight on Thursday the twenty-third of February**, and that is the one wipe in Volume 02 that is not on a Monday. **The wipes named in the volume are the 2nd, 9th, 23rd and 30th of January, the 23rd of February, the 13th of March, the 17th of April and the 1st of May.** There is **no** wipe on the twenty-fourth of April, and the chalk figure went up on Monday the first of May, not the twenty-fourth. **The next wipe after the first of May is Monday the eighth, which follows from the rule on the page — a figure on that board is a figure for a week, a figure in the yard's book is a figure for good — and is not a quotation.** `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0005 as written* carried the wrong version and has been corrected there.
2. **Three Mondays in May 2026 that are Thursdays.** The first pass of `outline/volume-03.md` lock L1, of `outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0001.md` hard rule 2 and of `workspace/volume-03/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` each gave the Mondays in May as the 4th, 11th, 15th, 18th, 22nd and 29th. **The fourth, eleventh and eighteenth of May 2026 are Thursdays. The Mondays in May are the 1st, 8th, 15th, 22nd and 29th, and the first of May is the first Monday of May and not the fifth.** All three files are corrected. A Volume 03 card had also put a wipe on Thursday the fourth of May; removed.
3. **Two chapters each entering "the seventh" of the reeve's acknowledgements**, in a ledger that holds six. **Chapter 102 is the seventh, at the yard's asking; Chapter 107 is the eighth, on the reeve's own motion and not asked for by anybody; there is no ninth**, and Chapter 109's reference is corrected to the true interval, three days from the seventeenth to the twentieth of May.

### Flagged for the controller, not fixable from a writer or audit phase
- **`state/phase-ledger.json` is still not advanced past bootstrap after a hundred finished chapters, and this is a live dispatch blocker.** The file still shows `currentPhase: phase-000-bootstrap` at `running` with `volume-01-batch-0001` at `planned`, `attempts: 0` and `resultCommit: null`, against a hundred finished chapters, ten batch summaries, two closed and audited volumes, three volume outlines, **seventeen phase prompt files under `workspace/` of which exactly one belongs to Volume 03**, and one queued writer phase. **`PHASE_SYSTEM.md`'s selector reads the phase ledger and claims the lowest incomplete batch, so a controller that trusts the ledger will select bootstrap and re-run a phase that has already been superseded.** This phase may not edit the ledger and did not, wrote no `.done` and no `.retired`, and did not treat the ledger as evidence that anything is unwritten. **Consequence for the next writer: do not read the ledger or the marker set as evidence that any batch is unwritten, do not mark anything done, and do not queue a phase; the controller owns that transition.**
- **No batch in either volume has a reviewer log stored in this repository, and fifteen state-file citations of one are now withdrawn.** The prompt this phase was handed named `logs/batch-0005.review.log`; the audit looked for the rest and **found that `logs/` has only ever held `close.log` and `models.log`**, that no `logs/batch-000N.review.log` was ever committed for any batch, and that `reviews/` holds only the Volume 01 Batch 0001 and Batch 0003 reviews and the two volume audits. **The repairs are not in question and were not touched** — `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 000N review repairs* is the authority for every Volume 02 review finding. **The false citations are withdrawn and no file was invented to satisfy one.** The Batch 0003 ruling that fixed the first citation is extended, because it described the directory wrongly. **The remedy belongs in `.opencode/agent/novel-reviewer.md` and `AGENTS.md`, neither of which this phase may edit:** if the reviewer reports only to `logs/`, nothing persists the log, and the Quality Gate line "a reviewer has checked the result" has no file to point at.
- **The one phase queued is `workspace/volume-04/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` and nothing else.** No Volume 04 batch-0002 prompt, no Volume 05 outline prompt, no later Volume 03 or Volume 04 prompt, no `.done` marker and no `.retired` marker. **Phase status is the controller's alone.** The Volume 03 close queued this one on 27 September 2026 and the restated form of this flag is at § *Volume 03 close — the six open threads, and the hand-over to Volume 04, 27 September 2026*, which is the single standing statement of the flag; **it is not restated again here and it needs no new restatement, and the ledger is a hundred and fifty chapters out of date.**

## Volume 03, Batch 0001 (Chapters 101–110) — planning repairs, and what Movement 2 inherits

**A reviewer pass over the Volume 03 outline, the Batch 0001 card file, the queued writer prompt, the Volume 02 audit that rules all three, and the state-file handoff returned FIX. The full disposition is `state/continuity.md` § *Volume 03 planning-phase review repairs*. No chapter was restarted, no card was cut, and no planned plot was altered. These are the thread-level consequences, and they are binding on the Batch 0001 writer.**

### Threads whose state the repairs changed

1. **The going out. — PAID, DONE, SPENT, and it may not be done again inside four years.** Eight marks and a day of a boat were bought in a market on Saturday the twenty-ninth of April for the second of May, **the going out was done at about ten past three on the second of May in a flat calm** (Ch 99:27), and the two-column and third-column entries for it are in Chapters 98 and 99. The standing sentence said in a street on the third of May — next one in about four years, eight marks and a day of a boat then as it is now, so that there is no argument about it in four years — **stands unreversed and unbreakable.** Chapter 110 was re-cast rather than re-funded: **a boat hired for the day out of the pump's eight marks, the difference between a bought going out and a boat put on the page before anybody gets wet, the bellwright in it for nothing, an eight marks and a day of a boat entered as owing him a second time, and no second eight marks paid.** Carried into Movement 2 as a standing owing and as a craft that recurs.
2. **The bellwright's one question. — CARRIED, and now unasked by a decision rather than by accident.** He may be asked one question in a room and not in a shed, and he said he will say it in a room if somebody asks him in a room and not before. **Batch 0001 does not ask him at all**, and says why out loud: the boy who read a position back twice went off the stone on the fifteenth, and a number said in a room is a number with no hand to enter it and no second column to put a reason against. **Movement 2 may ask it once, in a room, at a chapter's cost, and then the answer is his own words and no digit is printed and the third column is not where it goes. The number is still written nowhere.** The thread is otherwise untouched: the plug is in a chest in a shed, four descriptions do not agree, and it is not a custody object and not connected to anything.
3. **The reporting clause's trigger. — SETTLED, twice, and binding.** **The second limb is a claim, not a closure**: a ground the notice of the thirty-first closes is not a ground any other party claims, the *Vigil*'s claim lies beyond the outer marks, and the yard's own position is that the first limb caught the lump because the lump was itself a finding of worked glass on the twenty-eighth. **The counting rule is the day of the finding not counted, return due on the fourth day after it.** **Batch 0001 makes two returns — the fourth of May, two days late, and the tenth of May, on the day — and files a dated line on the thirteenth saying no third goes up.** The clause thread stays open across the volume: **a third return falls due on Saturday the seventeenth of June, out of a shard of worked glass raised on the tenth.**
4. **The pump. — PART, and the inherited figure is corrected.** Not out of the shed since Wednesday the fifth of April, when Iven Toft hired it for the day at four marks; **and a hire asked for on Saturday the twenty-second of April by a man with money and missed, entered against the yard with no reason against it.** Chapter 104 gives that man the pump: two days at four marks a day, eight marks in, two days lost. The eight marks are the whole of the money Chapter 110 has for a boat's day and the chapter says where it came from.
5. **The wage column. — PART, and a Volume 02 omission is closed forward.** The thirteenth week, four marks, fell due on Tuesday the second of May and was in no column. **Chapter 101 has the boy enter it on the fourth of May, in his own hand, with the reason that it went in on the fourth.** A wage week is paid or entered unpaid on its Tuesday, and a Tuesday is not a special day to the reporting clause and may not be written as one.
6. **The notice of the thirty-first. — STANDING, and its age is now six months.** Chapter 100's "eight months" is an error on that page, is not altered, and is not propagated. **The seventh and eighth acknowledgements are both spent in Batch 0001 and there is no ninth, so MOVEMENT 2 ENTERS NO ACKNOWLEDGEMENT AND PUTS NO PERSON, BOAT OR LEAD OF THIS YARD INSIDE THE FIRST CLAUSE.** The batch's own ground is landward of the third seam or a herring boat's own account.
7. **The four hundred and forty pounds of old iron and the bell-metal gun-port. — PART, and placed.** Raised on the twenty-eighth of April, unsold, on the yard's own ground behind the shed, in a heap the yard may not sell because it has declared a thing about its own gear in a book. **Open, and open deliberately: nobody has been asked what a gun-port of bell metal is, and the only man in this harbor who would know it at a glance is the bellwright, who is not asked in Batch 0001. A chapter in Movement 2 may put the two of them in the same room and may not make a finding of it.**
8. **The coast road and the city. — CARRIED, with Movement 3's beat now explicitly reserved.** The question written back out of a city about a bar of gravel is Movement 3's and **may not be produced in a market, a shed, a room or a boat in Movements 1 or 2**; Chapter 108 keeps a local man with a slate who wants to buy a copy of the sheet and is refused, and the refusal is recorded in his own hand in this harbor's market. The road is not resolved, not explained, not named a network, and is not connected to anything under the town. **Lowfen is named in Movement 2, in a working man's mouth, as a place that keeps its channels by hand; the nine final routes are not named in the prose; Orison is a place seventy fathoms down.**
9. **The two glass-storm lines. — CARRIED and deepened.** The storm of the twenty-sixth of April is in the second column with a reason and **no cause named in any volume**, and a second storm in Movement 2 gives the yard a second line it cannot check, written by a different man. **The man of fifty-one with a bad wheel is Dace's man off the *Grey Widow*; Coll Ferrow of the *Kestrel's Sister* is thirty-one and has a bad wheel too, and the two bad wheels in this harbor are two men and may never be merged.** The man of fifty-one has asked not to be told and is not on any lead in this yard and is not asked in a shed, a boat, a market or a room.
10. **The thirteen-week column, the third column, and the boy. — CARRIED, all three.** The third column is not renamed, not scratched out and not quietly stopped; it is filled late and the lateness is entered. **Nobody in that crew may ask the boy one word about the omissions he has entered, and Movement 2's Chapter 117 may show him being offered a June and may not resolve it, may not have the yard go to see him, and may not have the yard write to him.** The forty marks of claim stand whole and are not reduced and are not asked to be.

### The new cards

11. **`outline/batches/volume-03-batch-0002.md` now exists** — ten cards for Chapters 111–120, Movement 2, with a midpoint at Chapter 115 and a climax at Chapter 120 — **and `outline/volume-03.md` carries a "Handoff to Chapter 121" for them to land on.** Until now the queued Batch 0001 prompt required a next-phase prompt built from cards that did not exist, which the same prompt forbade inventing. **The Batch 0001 writer reads that card file, does not plan from it, and does not reconstruct it.**
12. **The one prompt queued is still `workspace/volume-03/batch-0001/PROMPT.md` and nothing else**, and this phase wrote no `.done` and no `.retired` and did not touch `state/phase-ledger.json`. **Phase status is the controller's alone, and the ledger is still a live dispatch blocker, as recorded above.**

## Volume 03, Batch 0001 (Chapters 101–110) — as written, Movement 1, the first return and the first bill

**Volume 03 Movement 1 is written. This block marks every thread the batch moved, every thread it opened, and every author-level decision it took. No thread below is closed by a chapter unless a chapter says so on the page. The full accounting is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0001 as written (Chapters 101–110), Volume 03*, and the chapters are the authority over both.**

### Threads carried from Volume 02 and moved here

1. **The writ and its four-day clock. — PAID AS AN INSTRUMENT, PAID TWICE AS A CLOCK.** Two returns are on the page and there is no third. **Return 1, Thursday the fourth of May, two days late**, about the raising of Tuesday the twenty-eighth, carrying four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in three pieces, a bell-metal gun-port, and a lump of green glass the yard had not looked at — **and, in the keeper's line, unasked, the disclosure that the object was in a shed for six days.** **Return 2, Wednesday the tenth of May, on the day it was due**, about the look on the sixth, saying the lump is worth nothing. **And a dated line in the yard's own book on Saturday the thirteenth of May that no return goes up and why, and that is in the yard's book and in no office's.** The counting rule is now spoken aloud in the shed: the day of the finding is not counted, the fourth day after it is the day it falls due, **and a return entered late is a return entered late and is not a different instrument and is not a smaller one.** Closes across Volumes 3–5. **A third return of the volume falls due Saturday the seventeenth of June and is Movement 3's.**
2. **The notice of the thirty-first. — STANDING, AND NOW WITH EIGHT ENTRIES IN THE LEDGER.** **The seventh was entered on the sixth of May at the yard's asking, for a person with a bucket standing on a crown in the open air.** **The eighth was entered on the seventeenth of May on the reeve's own motion and unasked by anybody, and it is the first in this harbor entered that way.** **Both are spent. THERE IS NO NINTH AND MOVEMENT 2 ENTERS NONE AND PUTS NO PERSON, BOAT OR LEAD OF THIS YARD INSIDE THE FIRST CLAUSE.** The notice is six months and sixteen days old at the end of the batch and is not lifted, not narrowed, not argued, and clause two is on the page three times as a reason and is argued by nobody.
3. **The eight-clause plan for the fifth seam. — PAID, AND THE MECHANISM IS NOW A CHANGE OF POLITICS IN AN OFFICE.** Refused on four grounds, every one right, better written than the first, served on the sixteenth and entered on the seventeenth. **What would move it is a change of politics in an office on a schedule nobody can name, and the first act of that change is a claim window for Monday the twelfth of June, spent by the harbor authority on its own account and not for this yard, to go out and enter in its own book what the notice has cost it — and the cost of it lands on nineteen winters of a herring fleet's dues that are going up a coast in the same file as Elias Voke's document and are going to be harder to dispute and not easier.** **The plate is still in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched.** Closes across Movement 4 and beyond. **Movement 2 does not have to answer it and may not reach the plate.**
4. **The supply problem. — PAID IN PART AND THE GAP IS NOW A NAMED GAP.** A compressor's gland is bought at the creditor's own figure of five marks in the creditor's own hand on a page standing at twenty-three, the carting not on the page; **ten feet of delivery hose that this harbor has not got, still not bought and not procurable, and the yard is without air for nine days and says so out loud twice.** The harbor authority has a compressor and a pot and about four fathoms of hose of its own, and the yard bought a pot of the office's air at its own two-mark rate on the eighth and spent the whole of it in a day. **The hand lead's line made badly in the net loft on the second of May is right, and the woman who said it would be right in a fortnight or not at all says so on the twenty-fourth in about nine words, and no knot in it has been taken out.** Closes the thread's beat and carries the rest.
5. **The boy off the stone. — PAID, ON THE DATE, AND NOT REPLACED.** **Monday the fifteenth of May, at the rate, four marks for the week of the ninth to the fifteenth, paid on the Monday and not the Tuesday, and nobody thanked him and nobody said what he was worth and he did not ask.** **The third column has a head on it and nothing under it, and a line under the head in Calder Marr's own hand saying that no man in this party is filling it and that a column in that shape is the honest shape of this yard and a man in a city is owed that shape.** **The wage week of Tuesday the sixteenth is not entered and the twenty-third is not entered, and the reason is on the page.** The forty marks of claim stand whole, unpaid, unasked to be reduced. **Closes in Volume 03 and the June berth is owed to Movement 2's own hands, not to this batch's.**
6. **The rival crew, Noll Tarner. — PAID FOR THIS MOVEMENT, and he is still not a villain.** He asks for a day and **is refused in a form he can check**, is given a free copy of the tenth thing in his own hand with his name on it, does not argue, does not come back, and does not ask twice. He supplies the movement's turn — **a road that no chart carries is a thing a man can sell, and the day a man comes up this hill with a map the first question is not what is under it but who pays for the map** — and he names **Lowfen**, about nine miles off, where the channels are kept by hand by men who do not want a diver, which goes in the second column with a reason because he has not been there. **The word *network* is not on any wall in this batch and is not in the yard's hand.** CARRIED into Volumes 3–4.
7. **Elias Voke's statement of capacity. — PAID AS A PERSON, AT A PRICE.** **He is in the room on the sixteenth because he carried the paper, and the price of a correct refusal lands on him, and he does not shout, and he asks for his name and his boat and the nineteen winters in his own hand in the account the harbor authority is sending up the coast.** The statement itself is still on its way to a coast office. **The hard at Ashtide is still unanswered since the ninth of November and is named in the position on the fifteenth.** Closes in Volume 03.
8. **The harbor authority's municipal poverty. — PART, AND THE THREAD CLOSES INSIDE VOLUME 03 AS THE VOLUME REQUIRES.** It is the reason the mole steps are four feet higher than they were in April, the reason the eighth of May is at the second seam, the reason the air of the eighth is the office's, the reason the mole-head light is not on, the reason the cistern is charged on a rota a man does not walk, the reason the light went out on the nineteenth of March, **and the reason the eighth of May's order came out of the harbor's own day work and not out of the repair account, which has been empty since the middle of February.** **None of it is fixed for nothing and no chapter of this batch had it fixed.**
9. **The forty marks twice. — CARRIED, BOTH WHOLE, BOTH UNPAID, AND THE MARKET MONEY DID NOT TOUCH EITHER.** **Mara Venn is in a market on the eighteenth of May, not asked, prices six marks and two days out loud, and says the forty is not one mark smaller for the four marks a shop at Ashtide refused her in writing.** The writ cannot reach her and she is not of it. She is not an assistant and was not asked to be useful. Closes in Volume 4 at her break and the debt's last payment is later.
10. **The bellwright's eight marks and the going out. — PAID AND SPENT AND NOT TO BE REPEATED, AND A NEW OWING IS STANDING.** The going out of the second of May is done, is spent, and may not be done again inside four years; **no second eight marks is paid anywhere in this batch and no second going out is bought.** The standing sentence said in a street on the third of May stands unreversed and is set under the entry of the twenty-third. **NEW AND OWED: eight marks and a day of a boat, a second time inside six weeks, entered as owing and not as paid, to a man who came out in a boat he is not paid to be in.** The bell reads nothing, settles nothing and says no word, and **the tongue is not on the frame and the phrase *a tongue half worn through* is not revived.** CARRIED.
11. **The bellwright's one question. — CARRIED UNASKED, BY A DECISION AND NOT BY ACCIDENT, AND THE REASON IS GIVEN OUT LOUD.** **He is not asked in a shed, in a boat, in a market, on a mole or in a room, and the yard has the question ready and says on the water that the reason is the boy's hand and not the man's manner: a number said in a room is a number with no hand to enter it and no second column to put a reason against, and a yard that cannot write a thing down is a yard that has no business asking a man in a room.** He says his own words still stand, says he has not been asked and is not going to say he has, and says ask me when you can write it. **Movement 2 may ask it once, in a room, at a chapter's cost, and then the answer is his own words and no digit is printed and the third column is not where it goes.** The number is still written nowhere.
12. **The lead plug. — CARRIED, UNTOUCHED.** Four descriptions that do not agree, a number in no book, the boy's hand on the lid. It is not a custody object and is not connected to the ferry plate dug in February, to the Oren mark, to the panel or to the road.
13. **The four hundred and forty pounds and the bell-metal gun-port. — PART, AND PLACED.** Unsold, unweighed, unpriced, not offered to anybody, in the same heap behind the shed as the twenty marks of the yard's own iron and the saw kerf, in a heap the yard may not sell one mark's worth of without a buyer reading the declaration first. **The woman in the net loft says on the eleventh that the man who would know that casting in about four seconds is a hundred yards down the road and that this yard has not asked him and she is not going to tell it to ask him. Nobody in this batch asked him, because the number in the lead plug is the only question this volume has for him.** **Open, deliberately. Movement 2 may put the two of them in the same room and may not make a finding of it.**
14. **The coast road. — PART, AND IT HAS ITS FIRST CUSTOMER.** Published in six boats in six hands, **and on the eighteenth of May a man who trades up and down this coast and is nobody's clerk asks to buy a copy with the bar on it for four marks, and is refused, in public, on the boards, and the refusal is written down in his own hand on his own slate in this harbor's market and is not the yard's to correct.** **Nothing was sold and no money moved and no document came back out of a city.** The road is not explained, not named, not connected to anything under the town, and the two are never fused. **The question written back out of a city about a bar of gravel remains reserved to Movement 3 and may not be produced in Movements 1 or 2.** CARRIED.
15. **Labor. — PAID, AND EVERY ITEM IS ON A PAGE.** The forty marks twice; the wage and the fifth of March the oldest; the note at thirty-one with the carting not on it; the eight marks to the bellwright paid and the going out spent; the eight shillings and tenpence and four hundred feet of warp on the bottom; no gland fitted and no hose and no air; the pump out of the shed for the first time since the fifth of April and two days of it gone; the ninth seam out with no end of it entered; the north berth out; the third lay full; the tide gang four short; the cistern on a rota; the dead hose; the light not on; the harbor authority with no boat of its own; the *Kittiwake* on the hard; the hard at Ashtide; nineteen winters; the *Sea Wren* at the pile; **the eleven copies and the four unreached holders, not asked; and the heap behind the shed that may not be sold.**
16. **Alden Marr. — CARRIED, UNTOUCHED, AND THE NOT-GOING IS THE WHOLE OF IT.** At the far bench in **three** chapters — **101, 106 and 110, and there is no Alden Marr in Chapter 104** — not asked for anything, and Calder Marr does not go over to him, **and in Chapters 101 and 106 the same refusal is now put in two different sentences, which is the one relationship in this book that should not sound the same twice**. No fragment, no report talk, no second story, no confession. Closes in Volume 9 and Volume 15.
17. **The delayed note. — CARRIED AND EXPLICITLY PROTECTED AGAIN.** **Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, two of them with no call in them, and neither has been advanced.** On the twenty-third of May in a boat off the south end the tide bell goes and the delay out of the water is a hand's width of a second and has not moved, and **there is no boy on the mole's steps with a slate and a stopwatch, and the man who took it in April could not take it and did not take it and did not write one, and a figure taken on purpose in a flat calm is a figure and not a note heard out of the weather, and no occasion is entered.** The answer inside it is not approached, not named and not resolved. Closes in Volume 15.
18. **The glass storm. — CARRIED, UNUSED AND UNEXPLAINED.** It is in the second column of a yard's book with a reason against it and **no cause has been named for it and none may be, in this volume or any other, and it may never become a voice that tells anybody anything.**
19. **The concealment. — CARRIED, HIDDEN, AND NOT TOLD A SECOND TIME.** In no record, in no office, in no book, not in Kest's chart notation. The false line is still false and the slate was not moved. **Nobody in this batch asked the boy and nobody asked the conservator, and nobody in that crew asked the boy one word about either of the two omissions he entered, then or afterwards.** Closes in Volume 9 and Volume 15.
20. **The compartment, the custody numbers, the ten yards, the run under the town, the first echo's witness, the nine-line mark, the dressed aperture, the ferry plate, the plate dug on the mole's toe in February, and the three Gannet documents. — CARRIED, ALL UNTOUCHED.** **No chapter of Chapters 101–110 refers to the compartment and the three-step finding is not restated; no digit of any of the three numbers is quoted and the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request; no line is drawn between the three points and the reeve's ruling stands; nine and a quarter feet is not mentioned anywhere in this batch; and Sera Mott is not asked again.** Closes in the volumes named in their own locks.
21. **The circuit office's fourth request of the sixteenth of March. — CARRIED, unanswered and unrefused**, and a different paper from anything in this batch.

### Threads opened by Batch 0001

22. **A lump of green glass in a bucket, and a scalloped edge of eleven scallops in about four inches. — NEW, AND IT IS WORTH NOTHING.** It came up out of the mole's own rubble off the toe between the third and fourth seams at about twenty-six minutes past eleven on **Friday** the twenty-eighth of April with a chain shackle and a turn of old cable, and went into a bucket, and was not looked at. **Its eleven scallops are the one thing in the batch that a man of the trade cannot account for, and Calder Marr turns the board twice on the crown and says nothing, and a later phase may not have him account for them.** **Its age is not figured and no reason for declining a figure is given, and the age goes in neither column on a line of its own.** **It is not a panel, not the ferry plate, not the worked panel given back in a market on the seventh of April, not in any office's book, and has no number anywhere and carries no claim seal.** **It is worth nothing to anybody the party has been able to find out about, and that sentence is on the page on a mole's crown before anybody asks for it.** Closes across Volumes 3–5 and its echo runs to the volume's midpoint.
23. **A bucket in a shed for six days. — NEW, SELF-REPORTED, AND FLAGGED FOR THE AUDIT AND NOT APPLIED.** **The yard disclosed it in the keeper's line of Return 1, unasked, and the harbor authority's clerk entered the other half of the figure in his own book on the same day and said the box has no room for the difference and this book has.** It is a continuation of Volume 02's own sentence about the second clause and it is **a breach of a notice this yard has not argued and will not, self-reported to a circuit office, and it may not be softened, resolved by an argument, or made the reason the notice is lifted.** `state/continuity.md` § 8 carries it as a proposed canon change flagged rather than applied.
24. **The first day of work the yard has been paid for out of its own paper. — NEW, AND IT IS ALSO THE FIRST OBLIGATION.** **Twenty-six gross on the board on Monday the eighth of May and TWELVE in the bag**, the second money this harbor has paid that yard since the first of February, the fourth marks out of it on the Tuesday as a wage, and **the eighth and the share taken off the top of it on the authority's own account with a ship's argument and a shipwright's name against it.** **A writ is a leash and it is also the only paper in that yard that pays, and those two facts are the same fact. THE FIGURE OF TWENTY-SIX IS THE BOARD'S AND NOT THE BAG'S AND A LATER PHASE MAY NOT PUT IT IN THE BAG.** Closes across Volumes 3–5.
25. **Four names is a crew. — NEW, AND IT IS THE BATCH'S MIDPOINT.** A man with better gear and no boat and no instrument and no money asks for a day and is refused, **and the reason is that a name on a leash is a person a piece of paper can say something about, and every other pair of hands in that operation is a pair of hands an office will ask about and the yard will have to answer for, and a man's accident with no name on a form is the worst thing in this trade.** The refusal is entered in the yard's book in its own words with his name on it, he is given something that is not work and is not thanked, and he does not ask twice. Closes across Volumes 3–4.
26. **A reader, and the practice that was one man holding up. — NEW, and the chapter that carries it does not say what it costs.** A two-column book with a reason against every line of the second, a third column with two times at the head of it, and a boy who read a position back twice and said the thing nobody asked for — **all of that was a practice one man was holding up, and the practice is now the yard's and not his, and the yard is going to find out what it costs by keeping it.** Closes nowhere; it runs to Volume 15.
27. **The eighth of the reeve's acknowledgements, unasked. — NEW, and it is the mechanism.** A claim window for Monday the twelfth of June, spent on the harbor authority's own account, to enter in its own book what the notice of the thirty-first has cost it; and the cost of saying that out loud is nineteen winters of a herring fleet's dues in the same file, going up a coast. **It is the change of politics in an office that lock L7 asks for, and it reaches nothing and lifts nothing.** Closes in Movement 4 and beyond.
28. **A record of a refusal, in the hand of the man who was refused. — NEW, small, and it is the coast's.** Four pages on a slate in a market in this harbor, three of them the yard's and one of them Mara Venn's, made for nothing by a man who is nobody's clerk, and **not the yard's to correct.** Closes nowhere; it runs to Volume 15.
29. **The line of the thirteenth of May that is not sent to anybody. — NEW, small, and it is the yard's own audit of itself.** No finding and no raising and no return, with the reason, in the yard's book and in no office's, entered because a sentence went up the coast on the second of May and it is time somebody in this yard found out whether it was true. Closes nowhere; it is a method.

### Author-level decisions taken in this batch

- **The power rules are unchanged and no stage is taken.** Calder goes in no water in this batch, enters nothing, and **THE CONDITION IS NOT NAMED IN IT.** It is on the page as the bare fact once, in a boat off the south end on the twenty-third at the top of the flood, in the form that there is no boy on the steps with a slate and a stopwatch — **and it goes into the second column with a reason against it and nobody in that crew asks him about it, and a man of fifty gives him a sentence for it and puts it in his own words. THE TWO STATE FILES THAT SAID TWICE ARE CORRECTED BY THE BATCH 0001 REVIEW REPAIRS: the second instance, on the mole's steps on the fourth, was never in a chapter. What is on the fourth is the condition's cost — the eighth of December, and a childhood call whose second sentence is not there and cannot be put back.** **The one naming Volume 03 is allowed is therefore NOT spent, and Movement 4 is where the volume's own structure wants it spent, and no Movement 2 or 3 chapter may take it, and no chapter of this batch may be read as having taken it.**
- **The consent rules are under load for the sixth time and hold.** A bellwright's refusal to be asked a question is honoured in a form he can check and he is not thanked for it. A lump of glass is looked at in the open air because a notice keeps it out of every shed, and the notice is not argued. A finding is entered unsigned and a conservator prices herself out loud and is not asked to be useful. **No seal, limit or refusal is overridden anywhere in the batch and no emergency access is used.**
- **The mystery chain is advanced by exactly one step and no more.** A scalloped edge that nobody can account for, and a lump of glass that is worth nothing, and a man of fifty who would know a bell-metal casting in about four seconds and is not asked. **The compartment is not referred to. The dressed aperture and the fouling that gives no hour are not referred to. The nine-line mark is not explained and the Nine Harbors, the Quietening and the Crown Seal are not named. What was in the sealed case is not revealed, the manifest is not proved altered, the Office of Returning Memory is not named, and no senior examiner is in a room and no senior examiner is anywhere but a seal line.** The lead plug is untouched and its number is written nowhere.
- **The ending is untouched and no new antagonist, institution or final enemy appears.** **Ilya Serr appears once, on a seal line, on a sheet produced on a mole. Halven Rook is not named. Captain Veyra Holt is not named and is not on the mole and the officer of the vessel who is there is not named and is described from a distance.** The only new people are ordinary working people: **a boatman of the SECOND lay — the man who hired the yard's pump for two days — who rows a boat for eight marks and says the going rate is eleven; a man with a slate who is nobody's clerk; and a woman in the net loft who has no name on the page.** **No face of an office is in a room and no institution is invented.**
- **A shape and length record, measured and not defended.** **29,905 words across ten chapters after the Batch 0001 review repairs, and 26,767 before them; seven inside the 2,200–3,200 guideline and three past it** — Chapter 107 by 161, Chapter 109 by 173, Chapter 110 by 360 — **and each of those three grew because the review required material the batch did not have, and no cost was dropped, which the batch's own length rule prefers. Chapter 109 and Chapter 110 are each one complete day in one place — a mole's steps on the twenty-second and a boat off the south end on the twenty-third — and no cost is paid twice in either.** **The hearing room is used once, on the seventeenth, and the market wall is used once, on the eighteenth, and no two institutional days in this batch are the same day and neither is a Monday.** The batch's pressures alternate: a clock and a bucket, a stone in daylight, a day rate, a casting and a handcart, a refused day, a boy off a stone, a refusal in a room, a market, a mole with a notebook, and a boat.
- **A self-reported breach is disclosed and is FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED.** The bucket was in a shed for six days, the yard told a circuit office so in its own hand without being asked, and the harbor authority's clerk entered the other half of the figure in his own book. **It is on the page, it is not softened, and it may not be resolved by an argument in a later volume and may not be the reason the notice is lifted.** The full statement is in `state/continuity.md` § 8 and a Volume 03 audit may rule on it.
- **One card figure was corrected against the page, and the page is right.** The Chapter 105 card said "the four men who copied that sheet by hand in six boats." **There are six, named on the wall and in Chapter 99, and the chapter says six.**
- **BATCH 0001 WAS REVIEWED AND REPAIRED, and the disposition is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0001 review repairs*.** A reviewer pass over Chapters 101–110 and the four state files returned **FIX** with fifteen required repairs and a list of prose and craft items. **The money chain counted gross as cash and the batch closed on twenty-seven instead of thirteen; it is now THIRTEEN, and the corrected chain is 13, 13, 13, 25, 21, 22, 25, 25, 21, 21, 13, 13. Four dates carried the wrong weekday, a refusal was signed five days before the plan it refuses, the pump yielded twelve on one page and eight everywhere else, the handcart was the yard's man on one page and a man paid a mark in three, the tide bell rang at two o'clock in a trip that had ended at half past nine, and the workflow's own words — *the batch is for*, *the sentence is the chapter*, *the refusal is the chapter* — were in three chapters. All of it is repaired in place.** **Calder Marr now has an interior life in six chapters, and Chapter 106 now grieves.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is a hundred and ten chapters out of date and is the controller's file, so it is flagged and not edited.
- **No chapter is restated, no scene was cut for length, and no cost was dropped.** The withdrawn *no chapter is two rooms* rule was not enforced against anything in this batch and is not carried forward; every chapter here is one day in one or two rooms, and the two that are longest are one day in one place.

### What Movement 2 inherits, in one place

**A crew of two and a half hands and a third column nobody can read back. A wage that runs on Tuesdays and a column that nobody fills and two weeks already gone by without a line. **A bag of THIRTEEN, which is the figure it held on the third of May, having earned a day's work at a seam and let its own pump out of its shed for two days and spent the lot of it and four marks of the wage besides**; a note of thirty-one, no tin, no air, no hose, a gland on a page and not fitted for another week, and a yard that has just been paid for the first time out of its own paper and has nothing coming. A lump of green glass worth nothing under a tarpaulin on a mole's crown. Two returns made and no third and a clerk's page that says the first was late and the second was not. A bell with no tongue, a frame cut through the casting, a man of fifty owed eight marks and a day of a boat a second time in six weeks and owed a question that has not been asked and says ask me when you can write it, and a boat on the twenty-third that was not a going out and a going out that is spent and recurs in about four years. A hand lead's line that is right. Four hundred and forty pounds of old iron and a bell-metal gun-port in a heap behind the shed that may not be sold and a man in this harbor who would know it in about four seconds and has not been asked. A correct refusal in a record with a date on it, a claim window for Monday the twelfth of June that is not for this yard, and a plate in eleven feet that nobody has said a word about.
