A notice of the thirty-first, six months and sixteen days old, not lifted, with two acknowledgements spent and no ninth. A glass storm in a second column with no cause. A coast road in six boats in six hands with its first customer refused on the boards and a record of the refusal in his own hand in a market. **A boat hired for the day and a man of fifty in it at the top of the flood, with the tide bell going at ten past three and the bellwright on the ladder, and six hours of lying to before it.** A man of fifty-one with a bad wheel who was not in this batch. A woman of thirty with forty marks, a paper of her own going up the coast, and six marks and two days. Eleven logged occasions, two empty answers, and a delay that has not moved.**

**And the standing sentence, unreversed: a bell is going to be out in a boat in about four years, and it will be eight marks and a day of a boat then as it is now, and the four of them were told on the third of May so that in four years there is no argument about it.**

---

## Volume 03, Batch 0002 (Chapters 111–120) — as written, Movement 2, weather and the work that cannot be sold

**Volume 03 Movement 2 is written. This block marks every thread the batch moved, every thread it opened, and every author-level decision it took. No thread below is closed by a chapter unless a chapter says so on the page. The full accounting is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0002 as written (Chapters 111–120), Volume 03*, and the chapters are the authority over both.**

### Threads carried from Batch 0001 and moved here

1. **The reporting clause's four-day clock. — PAID AGAIN, AND THE CLOCK STARTS A THIRD TIME AND IS NOT CLOSED.** The counting rule is spoken aloud in Calder Marr's own mouth and entered in the page on the tenth of June. **The finding is Saturday the tenth of June at about twenty minutes past twelve in the noon, on the second seam's own face landward of the third seam's mark, on the *Anna Ray's* own account; the day of the finding is not counted; the fourth day after it is the day it falls due; and a return in respect of a shard of worked glass of nobody's FALLS DUE ON SATURDAY THE SEVENTEENTH OF JUNE AND IS NOT MADE IN THIS BATCH.** Two returns have been made under the clause and no third. **Movement 3 makes the third return and no chapter of Movement 2 or Movement 3 may move the finding.**
2. **The notice of the thirty-first. — STANDING, AND ITS SECOND CLAUSE IS ON THE PAGE FOUR TIMES AS A LIVE REASON.** The lump is not dried in a shed; the shard is not put in a shed and not put in a boat in this harbor; and both are on the mole's crown above the third seam's mark in the open air. **No acknowledgement is entered in this batch, there is no ninth, and no person, boat or lead of this yard went inside the first clause on any day of it.** The notice is seven months old in the first week of June, is not lifted, is not narrowed and is not argued.
3. **The eighth of the reeve's acknowledgements. — PAID, AND THE DAY IS WORKED.** **Monday the twelfth of June, at the top of the flood, on the mole's toe and inner face between the third and ninth seams, the officer of the harbor authority with its clerk and two men of its tide gang entered in the office's own book what the notice of the thirty-first has cost that office, with the notice not lifted, not narrowed and not altered, and no person of the yard of Calder Marr upon that ground and not to be asked for and would be refused.** The yard was four hundred yards away walking out to a sluice and did not go, did not offer and did not ask. Closes the mechanism; carries into Movement 4.
4. **The boy's June. — PAID AS AN OFFER AND NOT RESOLVED, AND THE BATCH MAY NOT RESOLVE IT.** A herring skipper offered a season's hand in a market on Thursday the eighth of June, in about thirty people's hearing, giving the share figures before he was asked. **On Friday the ninth at about one o'clock the boy shook hands with a man of the third lay in front of about nine people and the holder was thirty feet away with a coil of hemp and did not go over.** **The three nots are entered in the first column with one reason against all three: the yard does not write to him, does not go to see him, and does not ask anybody who has seen him what he said.** The forty marks stand whole and are not reduced. The wage of four marks a week goes on running into an envelope on a bench. **The relationship does not end and is not paid off, and the person who holds the pencil in the last chapter of this volume is not him.**
5. **The wage column. — PAID THREE TIMES, AND THE COST IS NEW.** Five lines and twenty marks at the end of Batch 0001; **eight lines and thirty-two marks at the end of this one; the fifth of March the oldest and staying there.** Three wages of four marks on the thirtieth of May, the sixth and the thirteenth of June, in Calder Marr's own left hand because there is nobody else in that shed who writes, each into an envelope with the week on it, in the bench chest and not among the five rolled in oilcloth, **none of them sent anywhere that week, and the reason entered, and the reason is that a wage is not a reason to go to a door.** The yard enters that it read the column back in the man who wrote it and that a column read back by the man who wrote it is not a column read back. Closes across Volumes 3–5.
6. **The supply problem. — PART AND STILL THE SAME GAP.** **A compressor's gland was fitted to the yard's own pump on Monday the nineteenth of May and is not paid for, and it made no difference whatever because the thing after the gland is the hose and there is not one in this harbor longer than about nine fathoms.** The five marks of the gland are still on the chandler's page at thirty-one and the page is not re-written. **A third pot of air was bought of the harbor authority on Friday the ninth of June at the yard's own two-mark rate and was the whole of the air in this harbor on the morning of the tenth.** The hand lead's line made in the net loft on the second of May is right and was used in this batch. Closes the beat and carries the rest.
7. **The coast road. — PART, AND A SECOND MAN HAS IT IN A BOAT.** One of the six hand-copied sheets went out of this harbor in a boat of the third lay on the sixth of May and is in a boat out of a storm belt, and **the count of the six does not move because a count of hands is not a count of whereabouts.** A pilot of fifty-four who keeps a channel by hand has put a figure of his own beside the yard's and will not be told which is right, and has said that a road kept by three and a half people is a secret and a secret is a thing one person loses. **The road is not resolved, not explained, not named a network, and not connected to anything under this town, and the word is not in the yard's hand anywhere in this batch. NO PAPER OUT OF A CITY IS PRODUCED AND NONE MAY BE.** The question written back out of a city about a bar of gravel remains reserved to Movement 3. CARRIED.
8. **The man of fifty-one with a bad wheel. — CARRIED, USED, AND STILL NOT ASKED.** He is on the mole's crown in a westerly on the twenty-seventh of May because he said he would, and the yard learned on the twenty-second at the top of the steps in the hearing of three men that he had rung the handbell on the twenty-sixth of April and had said he would be the man who rang it. **He is heard twice, flat, saying two words about his wheel, and that is all he says in this batch, and no cause is entered for it.** On Friday the second of June he is told, straight and in time, that he is not on a lead, and **he does not ask why, and the not-asking is the loudest thing in the chapter, and it is entered in the shipwright's own words as the man not asking and the yard not offering.** He says one thing unasked about what a lead is for. **Nobody in that crew asked him one word about what he hears, and the yard did not put him on a lead, in the water, or on a pay day, and did not offer to.** CARRIED.
9. **The bellwright's one question. — STILL UNASKED, AND THE BATCH DID NOT SPEND IT.** He is not asked in a shed, a boat, a market, a room or anywhere, and the plug is still in a chest in a shed with a lock the yard owns, and the number is written nowhere. **His sentence of the twenty-third of May is quoted in Chapter 111 with attribution and not improved on. Eight marks and a day of a boat is owing to him a second time, unpaid and unargued, and the going out of the second of May is not repeated and no second eight marks is paid anywhere in this batch and the standing sentence about four years stands unreversed.** Movement 3 may ask it once, in a room, at a chapter's cost, and no digit may be printed and the third column is not where it goes.
10. **The lead plug, the custody items, the compartment, the ten yards, the run under this town, the first echo's witness, the dressed aperture, the ferry plate, the plate dug on the mole's toe in February, the three Gannet documents, and the nine-line mark. — CARRIED, ALL UNTOUCHED.** **No chapter of Chapters 111–120 refers to the compartment and the three-step finding is not restated; no digit of any of the three numbers is quoted and the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request; no line is drawn between the three points; nine and a quarter feet is not mentioned anywhere in this batch; and the marking on the shard of the tenth of June is entered as not a seal, not a letter and not a figure, and is NOT IDENTIFIED, NOT EXPLAINED AND SETTLES NOTHING.** Closes in the volumes named in their own locks.
11. **Labor. — PAID, AND EVERY ITEM IS ON A PAGE.** The forty marks twice; the wage at four marks a week running into envelopes; the note at thirty-one, not re-written, the carting not on it, the three counts of its weeks unmoved; the eight marks to the bellwright paid and the going out spent; the eight shillings and tenpence and four hundred feet of warp on the bottom and now a name and a figure and a date on a market wall; a gland fitted and unpaid and no hose and no air; the ninth seam out, the north berth out, the third lay full, the tide gang four short; **a cistern charged three times this month by people nobody pays;** the fire party's hose dead; the light not on; **the repair account empty since the middle of February and not going to be filled, and this office with no boat of its own;** the *Kittiwake* on the hard; the hard at Ashtide; nineteen winters; the *Sea Wren* at the pile; the eleven copies and the four unreached holders not asked; **the heap behind the shed that may not be sold and the bell-metal gun-port in it that was not looked at, not weighed, not priced and not offered, and the one man in this harbor who would know it at a glance was not asked and was not had in a room**; and **Neave Tarrant, nine winters, not found, and the yard has not gone and has asked nobody anything.**
12. **Alden Marr. — CARRIED, UNTOUCHED, AND THE NOT-GOING IS THE WHOLE OF IT.** At the far bench in **one** chapter of this batch, 115, not asked for anything, and neither brother went over to him. **No fragment, no report talk, no second story, no confession.** Closes in Volume 9 and Volume 15.
13. **The delayed note. — CARRIED AND EXPLICITLY PROTECTED AGAIN.** **Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October, two of them with no call in them, and neither has been advanced by arithmetic or by a measurement.** A handbell was rung off the mole head on the twenty-seventh and the answer came back off the crown and a man on a boat could not place it and did not write it down; **a bell went at the top of the flood off the south end on the fifteenth of June and nobody in that street could place the sound and it was not taken and it was not written down.** No occasion entered. The answer inside it is not approached, not named and not resolved. Closes in Volume 15.
14. **The concealment. — CARRIED, HIDDEN, AND NOT TOLD A SECOND TIME.** In no record, in no office, in no book, not in Kest's chart notation. The false line is still false. **Nobody in this batch asked the boy and nobody asked the conservator, and nobody in that crew asked the boy one word about either of his two omissions, then or afterwards.**
15. **The self-reported breach. — CARRIED AND EXTENDED, NOT SOFTENED.** The bucket was in a shed of this harbor from the twenty-eighth of April to the fourth of May and the yard disclosed it unasked. **In this batch the yard entered, in the first column, that a lump of green glass was found in water in its bucket in the yard's own boat on a mole's crown to a depth of about a finger, and that the party has not dried it, has not wiped it, has not put a cloth into it and is not going to, and the reason is the second clause.** It is a continuation and not a resolution and it may not be softened, may not be resolved by an argument, and may not be the reason the notice is lifted.

### Threads opened by Batch 0002

16. **A piece of worked glass of about a foot and a half, of nobody's, in a crate under a tarpaulin on the crown of the mole above the third seam's mark. — NEW, AND IT STARTS A CLOCK.** Green through the body and cloudy, two and a half inches at the thick end and a quarter of an inch at the thin, with the second seam's own stone dust in one face and a broken face at the thin end; **a scalloped edge of about nine in about three inches, the same kind of edge as the eleven in about four inches on the lump of the sixth of May, seen for the second time and accounted for neither time, and Calder Marr says out loud that he is not going to make up a name for it on a boat in front of a hired man;** and **a mark on one face which is not a seal and is not a letter and is not a figure, in which a number of the strokes are joined and one is not, which no person in that boat identified and which the party is not going to identify.** It is not a panel, not a plate, not the ferry plate, not the worked panel of the seventh of April, not the lump of the sixth of May, in no office's book, no number anywhere, no seal, no claimant, and **not connected to the lead plug, the Oren mark, the ferry, or anything under this town.** **It came up in a net and a net brings up what is in it, and it does not go into a shed and does not go into a boat in this harbor, and it is about nine feet from the bucket on the same crown under a second tarpaulin.** A third return of the volume falls due on Saturday the seventeenth of June. Closes in Movement 3.
17. **A second glass storm, and a second line in a second column. — NEW, AND UNEXPLAINED AND UNEXPLAINABLE.** About eleven hours out of the west-southwest veering west on the twenty-seventh of May, **not the weather of the twenty-sixth of April and not the same storm.** The yard's second column now carries two lines about these things, in two different hands, **on two pages that are not on top of each other, with the reason that this yard does not know whether they are two things, and no cause and no figure of any kind in either and nothing in either that knows anything.** A cause may never be named in any volume and the storm may never become a voice. Closes nowhere; it runs the whole series.
18. **A cistern charged by people nobody pays, and a wharfinger's column. — PAID AND CLOSED INSIDE VOLUME 03 AS THE VOLUME REQUIRES.** **The cistern behind the engine house was charged on Monday the twenty-ninth of May, on Thursday the fourth of June and on Monday the twelfth of June, by people of this yard, and the rota man walked out on the twenty-second of May because this office cannot pay him to walk out to a sluice.** The roving thread closed: **the man on the rota has not been paid for five pay periods, was last paid at the end of January, and was NOT paid on the twelfth of June and WAS paid on the twelfth of June for the first time in five pay periods by a party that is not the harbor authority and by nothing at all.** **Ewan Tristram put the walk on the office's own page in his own hand at no figure and against the office's own interest, let the mooring's gear out at nothing with a line on the page at nothing, read his own five months out in a market on the thirteenth to nine people, and said that a town is not a thing that is owed things and that it has run out of four of its five coins, and that he would rather have the rota walked than the money and is not sure why and did not resolve it.** **NOTHING WAS FIXED FOR ANYTHING: the light is not on, the repair account is empty and is not going to be filled, the ninth seam's mooring is out of service with no end of it entered, the north berth is out, the third lay is full, and forty feet of this office's own marline is in the sea.** Closes here. Movement 3 inherits a harbor that is materially improved by about one hole with a lid on it.
19. **A person who will go and look at it. — NEW, AND IT IS THE BATCH'S REAL FINDING.** **A pilot of Saltmarch keeps a channel nine miles off the point with two other men, has kept it nineteen years, goes out twice a year for nothing, can move a mark by hand on a bad night, and has been asked three times in nine years to put it in a printed return and has said no three times.** He says a channel is not a set of figures, and that the thing that keeps one is a person who will go and look at it when the figure is wrong. **He has offered to come back in the autumn, late, in his own boat and on his own money, to look at five miles of that coast and to say out loud on the mole's steps which of the two figures for the bar is right; and the yard has said yes.** The cost of saying yes is not money and is that **a yard with two and a half men has agreed to be checked by a stranger, and there is no instrument on this coast that can call him back and none that can stop him.** Closes in Movement 3, and it is the yard's own reason for what Chapter 120 does.
20. **A refusal that is on a public wall. — NEW, SMALL, AND IT IS THE COAST'S.** Sella Brigg's name, a figure, a date and a whole costed column went up on the market wall on Wednesday the seventh of June in daylight in a market under the naval paper of the eighteenth of March, **and a refusal that is written down is a different object from one that is not: it cannot be taken back, it cannot be put in a margin, and the party that wrote it has to go on being the party that wrote it.** **She has made herself, in writing, in a public place, a creditor of this yard's work, which is not the same as a creditor of its money and is worse, because a creditor of money is paid and a creditor of work has to be answered.** The wall is used once in this batch and not again. Closes in Volume 4 at Mara's break, where the public record is the argument.
21. **Three returns named, one funded, two refused. — NEW, AND THE PATTERN IS THE BATCH.** A herring boat's own lost ground tackle on her own account, funded and worked on the tenth of June; **about four hundred feet of a woman's own warp, refused by the woman it belongs to and then priced on a wall;** and about nine feet of the mole's own mooring chain off the toe between the third and fourth seams, **refused as not available at all, because it is on ground the first clause closes and both of this year's acknowledgements are spent and this yard is not going to ask a reeve for a ninth for nine feet of chain, which costs nothing today and everything in a year.** Closes across Volumes 3–5.
22. **A thirty-mark offer refused in nine sentences. — NEW, AND IT IS THE BATCH'S CLIMAX.** A master of two boats with a following, a coaster, an ordinary working man and **not a villain, and not an institution, and not a final enemy**, offered money, a mooring, his own gear at no figure and a line in a book that is not the yard's, in exchange for the right to use the road. **The yard refused in Calder Marr's own left hand on the back of his own paper, gave the paper back, and said without being asked what it would take instead: a person who will go and look at it, twice a year, for nothing, and does not have to be told why.** He said it was a thing a man says when he has nothing, and that he has run two boats for twenty-six years and has never had a favor off a man with eight marks in a bag in his life. **He did not threaten anybody, did not come back, did not write to an office, and said he would not put a mark against the yard's name; the yard entered that no mark is put against his and read the whole of it back to him, and he said he would like to be in this harbor twice.** **The refusal cost the town its third recovery: the thirty marks would have paid for about four hundred feet of a woman's own warp, and the woman has her name and a figure and a date on a market wall and has not been told and is not going to be told this week.** Closes nowhere; he may reappear in this volume as an ordinary man with a grievance and may not be given an office, a ship, a uniform or a title.
23. **A third column filled late, and the lateness entered, twice. — NEW, AND IT IS THE YARD'S OWN PRACTICE WITHOUT THE HAND THAT MADE IT.** Sunday the twenty-eighth of May: read on the steps at about one in the morning, written at about eleven the same morning, about ten hours. Thursday the eighth of June: read at about seven, written at about eight, about an hour, against the two hours the boy used to put at the head of it, **and no reason entered for the change because the party does not have one.** The column is not renamed, not scratched out and not quietly stopped. **Nessa Pike says out loud that the yard is going to have to hire a hand to read these back or stop, and she would rather it hired, and Calder Marr says he is not going to hire a man to read back figures on a Tuesday for four marks a week when he cannot pay four marks a week.** Closes nowhere; it runs to Volume 15.
24. **An envelope on a bench. — NEW, SMALL, AND THE BATCH'S QUIETEST COST.** Three wages of four marks a week, in three envelopes, in the bench chest, not among the five rolled in oilcloth, **none of them sent anywhere, and the reason on the page is that a wage is not a reason to go to a door and that the day this yard uses a wage as an excuse to go and see a man it has owed money to for seven months is the day the column is worth nothing.** Closes across Volumes 3–5.
25. **The yard's own boat on the crown, and two tarpaulins. — NEW, AND IT IS THE SECOND CLAUSE BEING A RULE INSTEAD OF A MEMORY.** The yard's boat is hauled up on the capstan above the third seam's mark; a bucket with a lump of green glass in it and a crate with a shard of worked glass in it are about nine feet apart on that crown; and since Monday the twelfth of June both are weighted with a spare fender and forty feet of the harbor authority's own mooring rope, lent at nothing and entered at nothing. Closes nowhere; it is the volume's standing condition.

### Author-level decisions taken in this batch

- **The power rules are unchanged and no stage is taken.** Calder goes in no water in this batch, enters nothing, and **THE CONDITION IS NOT NAMED IN IT.** It is on the page as the bare fact twice — a sound on a mole's crown in a westerly that he cannot place for a second and a half in either direction, and a handbell's answer off a mole head on a boat — **and each time it goes into the yard's own practice or the yard's own second column with a reason against it, and nobody in that crew asks him about it, and no name is given for it in any of the ten chapters.** **THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 03 IS ALLOWED IS THEREFORE NOT SPENT AND IS OWED TO MOVEMENT 4, AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH MAY BE READ AS HAVING SPENT IT.** No Bellwright, Keelwright, Storm-Reader, Chorus Diver or Openhand stage is taken and none may be.
- **The consent rules are under load for the seventh time and hold.** A bellwright's refusal to be asked a question is honoured a second time and he is not thanked. A man of fifty-one's refusal to be told is honoured, and he is not put on a lead, and a reason is refused entry because the reason is a thing about him. **A lump and then a shard of glass are looked at in the open air on a mole's crown and not indoors, and a wet thing is not dried, and the notice is not argued.** A mark on a shard is entered and is not identified. **The refusal of a wage as a reason to go and see a man is a consent decision and it is on the page with its reason.** No seal, limit or refusal is overridden anywhere in the batch and no emergency access is used.
- **The mystery chain is advanced by exactly one step and no more.** **A marking on a shard that nobody identified; a scalloped edge seen twice and accounted for neither time; a man who keeps a channel by hand and has said what keeps one; and a man of fifty-four with a figure of his own that he will not be told is wrong.** **The compartment is not referred to and the three-step finding is not restated. The dressed aperture and the fouling that gives no hour are not referred to. The nine-line mark is not explained and the Nine Harbors, the Quietening and the Crown Seal are not named. What was in the sealed case is not revealed, the manifest is not proved altered, the Office of Returning Memory is not named, and no senior examiner is anywhere but a seal line. The lead plug is untouched and its number is written nowhere and the bellwright was not asked.**
- **The ending is untouched and no new antagonist, institution or final enemy appears.** **Captain Veyra Holt is not named and is not in a room. Halven Rook is not named. Ilya Serr does not appear.** The only new people are ordinary working people: **a pilot of fifty-four out of a storm belt who keeps a channel by hand; a coaster of fifty-nine with two boats and a following; a hired diver of twenty-nine of the second lay; a man of the second lay of about sixty who keeps the keys to three wharves; a woman of the third lay whose own warp is at the bottom of this harbor; and two men of the third lay who were not of the yard and are not named.** **No face of an office is in a room and no institution is invented.**
- **A shape and length record, measured and not defended.** **30,345 words across ten chapters; six inside the 2,200–3,200 guideline and four past it — 112 by 63, 115 by 59, 111 by 264 and 120 by 273 — and all four ran because a cost could not be dropped. Chapter 111 is a night of weather with a two-column entry written at ten past two in the morning; Chapter 120 is the batch's climax, which is one shed, one man, one paper, and a refusal that has to be nine sentences long and has to say out loud what the yard would take instead.** The hearing room is used **not at all**; its wall comes down bare at eight on the twenty-ninth of May, the fifth of June and the twelfth of June and on no other day, and there is no sitting on any of the three, and the clerk has stopped mentioning it. The market wall is used **once, on Wednesday the seventh of June, in daylight, in a market, on no Monday**. No cost is paid twice and no day is in a chapter twice. The batch's pressures alternate: a storm, a rota, money, method, discovery from outside, a public wall, an emotional consequence, work and a clock, a municipal recovery, and a refusal in a shed.
- **Mechanical checks, run and clean.** **A byte-comparison of every paragraph of twelve words or more across the ten chapters returns zero duplicates, and the same comparison against all one hundred and ten prior chapters returns zero paragraph matches.** **Exact repeated sentences of twelve words or more: zero within the batch and zero against the prior hundred and ten**, after one line — Sella Brigg's standing debt in the Tuesday the thirtieth's read-out — was varied because it was identical to the line in Chapter 106. **No game-style interface, no stat block, no second person in narration, no prompt, no software vocabulary. No chapter states a year. The American spellings are the house forms and four British forms that slipped into Chapters 112, 116 and 120 — *favor* and *honor* — are repaired in place.**
- **One card fact placed rather than repeated.** **The Chapter 111 card says the man of fifty-one said he would be on the mole "in a market on the fifteenth." There is no market on Monday the fifteenth of May in Chapters 101–110 and no line in the yard's book in which that man said he would. It is placed on Monday the twenty-second, at the top of the mole's steps, at ten in the morning, at the production of the writ, in the hearing of three men, and entered as a fact the yard learned on a day the yard names.** No chapter writes "in a market on the fifteenth" as though a page said it.
- **One shape rule applied as written.** **The rule "no chapter is two rooms" was not enforced against anything in this batch and is not carried forward.** Chapter 111 is a crown and a shed on two consecutive days; Chapter 116 is a market, a wall and a shed across two days; Chapter 119 is a mole head, a sluice, a market and a shed across two days. **Every chapter in this batch is one day in one or two rooms, and the two longest are one day in one place.**
- **`state/phase-ledger.json` is one hundred and twenty chapters out of date and it is the controller's file, so it is flagged and not edited.**

### What Movement 3 inherits, in one place

**A crew of two and a half hands and a third column that gets filled late and is not renamed and is not scratched out and is not going to be quietly stopped, with nobody in the shed who will read a position back and Nessa Pike saying out loud that the yard is going to have to hire a hand to read these back or stop, and Calder Marr saying he is not going to hire a man to read back figures on a Tuesday for four marks a week when he cannot pay four marks a week.** A wage that runs on Tuesdays into an envelope on a bench — **eight lines and thirty-two marks in the column and the fifth of March the oldest** — and three envelopes that are not going anywhere.

**EIGHT marks in a bag, which is five less than the yard had on the twenty-fourth of May, and thirty-one on the chandler's note with three static counts unmoved since the fourth of January, and nothing in a tin, and no money received from any office in three weeks and no money paid to any person in three weeks except three wages.** A compressor's gland fitted to the yard's own pump and not paid for and no delivery hose in this harbor, and a third pot of air spent in a day on the tenth of June.

**A THIRD RETURN FALLING DUE ON SATURDAY THE SEVENTEENTH OF JUNE**, out of a piece of worked glass of about a foot and a half, green and cloudy, nine scallops in three inches accounted for neither time, a mark on one face that nobody identified, in a crate under a tarpaulin on a mole's crown above the third seam's mark in the open air because the second clause of a notice neither of us wrote will not let it be in a building, about nine feet from a bucket holding a lump of green glass that is wetter than it was on the sixth of May. **The yard has three days to write it and no boy to read it back and no boy in this harbor who reads anything back.**

**Three recoveries named, one funded and worked, two refused and still refused** — a warp at the bottom with a woman's name and a figure and a date on a market wall since the seventh of June, and a chain on ground the first clause closes, with no ninth acknowledgement and a refusal that costs nothing today and everything in a year.

**A pilot out of a storm belt who keeps a channel by hand with two other men, who has a figure of his own for the bar that he will not be told is wrong, who has said that a road kept by three and a half people is a secret and a secret is a thing one person loses, who will not name the place he has watched lose one and asked not to be asked, and who is coming back in the autumn for nothing to look at five miles of that coast and say out loud on the mole's steps which figure is right.** And the yard has agreed to be checked, and has no instrument on this coast that can call him back.

**A man of fifty-one with a bad wheel, in a boat of another man's, who came on a mole because he said he would, who is better on a lead than anybody in this yard, who has not been put on one, who was told so straight and in time and did not ask why, and who has asked not to be told what he hears and has not been asked.**

**A boy of twenty-two who took a season's hand in a market on the ninth of June, forty marks of claim standing whole, a wage going into an envelope, a third column with a head on it, and three nots entered in the first column with one reason against all three, and nobody who went after him.**

**A harbor authority that went out on Monday the twelfth of June and wrote down what a notice has cost it, and did not fix one thing, and a wharfinger who has read his own five months out in a market and asked for nothing, and a cistern with a lid on it that is charged three times a month by people nobody pays, and a man on that rota who has not been paid for five pay periods and was paid on the twelfth of June by nothing at all.**

**Eight marks and a day of a boat still owing to a bellwright who came for nothing and has still not been asked his one question and whose plug is still in a chest in a shed with a number in it that is written nowhere. Four hundred and forty pounds of old iron and a bell-metal gun-port in a heap behind the shed that may not be sold, and the one man who would know it in about four seconds has not been asked. A notice six and a half months old, not lifted, with eight acknowledgements in a ledger and no ninth. A glass storm twice and no cause named in any volume, and two lines in a second column in two hands on two pages that are not on top of each other, and nothing in either that knows anything. Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October with two of them empty, and neither moved. A coast road in six boats and six hands, one of the six in a boat out of a storm belt, and no paper out of a city and no question out of a city. A boy who read a position back twice and is not on the stone. A bell with no tongue and a going out that recurs in about four years at eight marks and a day of a boat then as it is now, and it is seven weeks and a day from the first of July, a Saturday, and this is a yard of two and a half.**

**And the standing sentence, unreversed: a bell is going to be out in a boat in about four years, and it will be eight marks and a day of a boat then as it is now, and the four of them were told on the third of May so that in four years there is no argument about it.**

---

## Volume 03, Batch 0003 (Chapters 121–130) — as written

**Movement 3 is written and its turn has landed, and the batch has been REVIEWED AND REPAIRED: thirty-three thousand seven hundred and thirty-one words after the repairs and thirty-three thousand six hundred and twelve before them, Chapters 121–130, Saturday the seventeenth of June to Thursday the sixth of July. The disposition of every finding is `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0003 review repairs* and the reviewer's log is `logs/batch-0003.review.log`.** A third return made on the day; a chart bought out of a reeve's hand for nineteen winters of a herring skipper's dues; a rival crew's printed sheet that is better than the yard's because it can be bought, printed off a slip of eleven names the yard nailed up itself; a coast office that answers at last and files three of this harbor's own lines in a list; a question written back out of a city about a bar of gravel the yard cannot answer and has made a rule it will not break; a bellwright asked in a room and answered once and not written down; and a yard that declines to publish. **Every thread below is marked PAID, PART, CARRIED, RULED or OPEN BY DESIGN. Movement 4 is the volume's midpoint and what it inherits is set out at the end of this block in plain terms.**

### Paid

- **PAID — The third return. (Ch 121, Saturday the seventeenth of June, on the day.)** Made out of a finding of Saturday the tenth, the day of the finding not counted, the fourth day after it, spoken out loud in the entry. One sheet, unshortened, with the place, the hour, the keeper and the limits; a copy for the harbor authority's ledger in the clerk's own hand; the clerk's own page carrying the hour it was written and not the hour it was done. **The finding of the tenth is not moved. The counting rule is not moved. There is no fourth return and none may be produced out of a day that produced nothing.** It went up the coast in a sealed jacket and the clerk said, on the steps, that it is the only thing this yard makes that anybody in a city is obliged to look at.
- **PAID — The scalloped edge and the mark, as a fact and not a conclusion, twice seen and accounted for neither time.** Both on the return and on the yard's own wall, and **not identified, not explained, and not allowed to settle anything.** The counting of them has been expressly refused: a third time or not, and the count is going on no wall and in no column.
- **PAID — The coast office's answer. (Ch 126, Wednesday the twenty-eighth of June, said in a man in this harbor's mouth and not read out of a file.)** **RESERVED TO MOVEMENT 3 AND SPENT HERE, ONCE.** Two sentences: the office does not moor a fleet, has no power over a harbor authority, has had the fisher's document since the first of March and has not answered it and is not going to; and it has read that document and taken one line out of it — the north berth out of service, the ninth seam's mooring out of service since the first of May with no end entered, the third lay full — and entered that line in a list of what this coast can take next winter, being made up in the first week of July, with Elias Voke's nineteen winters on the same page and not on the same line. **THE COAST OFFICE IS NOT A VILLAN ON THE PAGE AND IS NOT WRITTEN AS ONE: it asked for a capacity figure, the harbor sent one, and the figure is going to be right in November because the harbor wrote it down itself and gave it away for nothing.**
- **PAID — A man in a city reads a writ with a reporting clause on it and writes back a question about a bar of gravel. (Ch 127, Thursday the twenty-ninth and Friday the thirtieth.)** **RESERVED TO MOVEMENT 3 AND SPENT HERE, ONCE.** J. Hesketh, forty-four, a printer of Veyr who prints a yearly coasting return at his own cost and marks what he has seen differently from what he has been given, holds no seal, is of no office and has not asked for one, and has a hand-copied version of the yard's tenth thing from a master of the second lay who copied it at the foot of the yard's own dock in June for nothing. Four questions, three answered out of the yard's own book in the yard's own hand, one not answered. **It is not a complaint, it is not a seizure and it is not an answer to anything. The yard's answer went in the ordinary post at no cost and the reason is in its FIRST LINE and not its last.** **The effect the outline asked for is on the page: a figure the yard published in May has become a question that has to be answered by somebody, and the somebody is the yard, and the yard cannot answer the fourth question, and the reason it cannot is a rule it has held since the third of May and has now paid for in public.**
- **PAID — The cutter asks for the harbor authority's chart a second time. (Ch 122, Monday the nineteenth, on the mole's steps; traded in Ch 123, Wednesday the twenty-first, in the hearing room; delivered Thursday the twenty-second.)** **The chart is not on a wall in this harbor and is not in the yard's keeping, the yard may not produce it and may not refuse it on its own account because it is not the yard's, and it said so in nine sentences on the steps in about nine people's hearing before the officer went down them.** Captain Veyra Holt is named on the paper, described from a distance, and is not in this harbor, did not come up the hill, and is not going to. **No face-to-face meeting occurred.**
- **PAID — The reeve is offered something, and the price is named in front of the room and paid in somebody else's winter. (Ch 123, Wednesday the twenty-first, the hearing room, with the clerk. THE BATCH'S THIRD RESERVED BEAT, PAID ONCE, IN KEST'S CHAPTER AND NOT THE YARD'S.)** Offered: a paper signed by the master of a cutter withdrawing the vessel's objection to the exercise of claim windows by this harbor authority on any ground inside the harbor line for the remainder of the term. Price: **the chart, which carries in Kest's own hand of the twenty-fifth of December a notation that any person proposing to work the bell at the south end is to be given the account of the herring fleet's dues first — and there is no second copy of that notation, because the notation is on the chart and the chart is the paper.** **The price is Elias Voke's nineteen winters and the man it is paid out of was one day at sea and did not know, and the reeve wrote it to him in four lines in his own hand and sent it up the coast by a boy who was going anyway and was paid nothing.** **The chapter says in terms that the offer is not a claim window, not an acknowledgement and not a ninth — both of this year's are spent, no ninth exists, and nothing has put a person, a boat or a lead inside the closure since the eighth of June and nothing is going to — and is not a work permit, a charter or a renewal, and that the second season is not that office's to give.** The reeve also named what he bought: **an uncontested instrument, and a reeve who can no longer find out whether his own instruments work.**
- **PAID — The turn. (Ch 124, Saturday the twenty-fourth, and Ch 125, Sunday the twenty-fifth.)** **A public route is a thing other people can now use, and one of the things they can do with it is take it, and the yard's own eleven copies and six hand-copies become the reason a rival crew's map looks better than the yard's.** Perry Ollett, thirty-nine, of the *Sallow Reach*, a crew of Ashtide, six men, a compressor of her own, forty fathoms of her own hose, has a printed, scaled, dated, priced coasting sheet with the ground, the hour, the number of times and the hand against every figure the yard published in May — **and at the head of it, in eleven lines, the eleven places the copies of the public sheet of the fourteenth of October are held, printed off the yard's own slip, which has been nailed to the market wall since the third of May for a shilling.** **The yard refused to sell a copy, refused to ask for the slip to be taken down, said out loud that the slip is the only reason a stranger could print a sheet anybody could write to, and did not ask which four of the eleven he had found.** Ollett is not a villain, has no office, no ship, no uniform and no title, does not threaten anybody, does not use the word the yard refused in public, does not say what a road is and explains nothing.
- **PAID — The bellwright's one question. (Ch 128, asked in a doorway on Saturday the first of July and asked in a ROOM on Sunday the second, in the cabin of the *Anna Ray*, with the door shut, the hatch open, four men present, no office and no seal and no ledger, and the chest not brought down the hill and not opened.)** **THE OFFER STANDS AND IS SPENT. He answered once. What he said is on the page in his own words and NO DIGIT IS PRINTED IN ANY CHAPTER OF THIS BATCH AND NO FIGURE GOES INTO ANY COLUMN.** His three conditions were agreed in the room: the number is not written down, nobody comes about it again, and the chest stays shut. **THE YARD HAS UNDERTAKEN IN ITS OWN BOOK NOT TO ASK HIM AGAIN, AND THAT UNDERTAKING BINDS THIS PARTY AND NOT THE OFFICE AND NOT THE MARKET AND NOT THE BOY, WHO WAS NOT ASKED AND IS NOT TO BE. NO LATER CHAPTER IN THIS VOLUME MAY ASK HIM AGAIN.** He did not ask for money and said so, and that he did not ask is entered.
- **PAID — The cistern thread, materially improved and nothing fixed for nothing, and CLOSED INSIDE VOLUME 03. (Ch 129, Tuesday the fourth; Ch 130, Thursday the sixth.)** **Eight walks on the office's own page at no figure and against the office's own interest, the cistern holding since the nineteenth of June, and a wharfinger saying the end of it out loud in a market: a cistern that holds is a hole with a lid on it and not a town, said in that order, in a market, and not taken back, and not asking anybody for anything.** The thread is not reopened. The harbor's poverty is not fixed for anything: the light is not on, the north berth is out, the third lay is full, forty feet of the office's own marline is in the sea, the hose is dead until the autumn, the *Kittiwake* is on the hard, the hard at Ashtide is unanswered and nineteen winters are in a naval file and in four lines of a letter.
- **PAID — The hand lead's line, checked against itself. (Ch 128, Saturday the first of July.)** Four casts of the hand lead on the second seam at the top of the flood, three on the mark at eleven and a half and one forty feet south at eleven and three-eighths, **the same as the second of June five weeks earlier in the same hour, and entered as a fact and not as a proof, because eight casts is not four times a year and a yard that begins to treat eight casts as a figure is a yard about to put a number on a wall.**
- **PAID — The yard's own boat, off the haul-off and back into the water. (Ch 125, Monday the twenty-sixth, at no figure, a day gone and no money either way.)** Both objects of nobody's were left on the mole's crown under two tarpaulins, because a boat in this harbor with a thing like that in her is a boat in a shed with a lid on.

### Part

- **PART — The condition. (Ch 121, Sunday the eighteenth; Ch 129, Monday the third.)** **NOT NAMED. ON THE PAGE AS THE BARE FACT TWICE IN THIS BATCH, IN THE SECOND COLUMN, WITH A REASON AGAINST EACH LINE, NO CAUSE AGAINST ANY OF THEM, NO NAME IN ANY OF THEM, AND NOBODY ASKED — AND WITH A COUNT OF FOUR entered against the fourth, in the shipwright's hand and not his.** **For the first time in five months the holder said it out loud to a person**, and the shipwright wrote it in her own hand and refused to put a name against the line, and her reason is on the page: the day a man of this yard writes that he could not tell which of two sounds it was with his name on it, in about two years there is a page in that book that says what a man of this yard can and cannot hear, and that page will be read by somebody in Veyr who is looking for exactly that. **THE ONE NAMING VOLUME 03 IS ALLOWED IS THEREFORE NOT SPENT AND IS OWED TO MOVEMENT 4.**
- **PART — Bevin Rook. (Named only; not in the batch.)** Not in the batch. Not written to, not visited, nobody asked what he said to them. The three nots of the eighth of June stand and were recited on the fourth of July. **His sentence on the lid of the chest is quoted on the second of July as the reason the yard will not write the number down, and that is the only use made of him, and he was not asked and is not to be.** Forty marks standing whole; a wage running into an envelope; six envelopes on a bench and not one of them anywhere. **NOT PAID, NOT REPAIRED, NOT REDUCED. MOVEMENT 4 MAY NOT PAY IT OFF EITHER.**
- **PART — Sella Brigg's warp. (Ch 125, Sunday the twenty-fifth, in a market.)** **She said in about forty people's hearing that she is going to ask a man of Ashtide about about four hundred feet of her own warp, and the yard said she has a right to and will not stand in the way and has no standing with her and did not try.** The yard also said out loud what that costs: the refusal of the seventh of June is the reason a man with a compressor can print a better sheet than the yard can, and in about three weeks he will come up this hill and be able to do a job the yard could have done on the tenth of June for four marks, **and the only reason he can is that the yard could not.** And she said out loud that she worked out about the thirty marks herself, in eleven days, from a coat. **Nothing is raised, nothing is sold, and the eight shillings and tenpence to each of two women stand undiminished and unpaid.**
- **PART — The herald of the turn, and the lead. (Ch 122, Monday the nineteenth.)** A paper's rider asking for the place of every copy of a public record was answered on the mole, in full, in the open air, in front of nine people, **and the yard said it is not going to ask the four unreached holders and entered that it did not believe the officer's promise not to pass the list on.** A day on the mole's crown at the top of the flood put a fact nobody can check into the second column with a reason, and did not let the yard skip the reading of it.
- **PART — The ninth seam's mooring. (Ch 129, Monday the third.)** **AN END OF IT IS ENTERED, THE FIRST OF SEPTEMBER, in the harbor authority's own book by the reeve's own hand, and it is the first thing that office has entered in five months a person in this harbor can use, and it is entered because that office's instruments are not being contested.** **THE NOTICE OF THE THIRTY-FIRST OF OCTOBER IS NOT LIFTED, NOT NARROWED AND NOT ALTERED, AND NO ACKNOWLEDGEMENT WAS ENTERED, AND A MUNICIPAL MOORING IS NOT A PERSON, A BOAT OR A LEAD.**
- **PART — The harbor's reader. (Ch 125, Monday the twenty-sixth.)** **Nessa Pike refused to say a third time that a reader must be hired, and said that a thing said three times in a shed is a complaint and she does not make complaints, she makes entries.** The third column has been filled five times since the second of May and read back by the man who wrote it, and on the fourth of July it went in a day and a half late, which is the longest since the column was created.
- **PART — The third column. (Three entries in this batch: the eighteenth and the twenty-sixth of June and the fourth of July, with the two earlier ones standing — the twenty-eighth of May and the eighth of June. Five in all since the column was created, and the head of it was written on the twenty-first of May and the boy went off the stone on the fifteenth, so every entry is after him.)** Each carries two times at the head and a lateness against it — about ten hours, about an hour, about eight hours, about two hours, about a day and a half. **A line was written at the head of the column on Sunday the second of July with nothing under it, saying that the plug is not where this goes and is not going to go in a second column either.** The count of four sound entries goes into the first column in the shipwright's hand on **Tuesday the eleventh of July, which is a wage day and a market day**, and has not gone in yet. **IT IS THE ONE FORWARD BEAT IN BATCH 0003 THAT CHAPTER 133 OWES AND MUST PAY.**

### Carried

- **CARRIED — The coast road.** Not resolved, not explained, not named a network by anybody in this batch including the rival, not connected to anything under this town, and the question of who edits the charts was not asked by the man in the city and was not answered by the yard. Its figures are now printed in a market in this harbor and are going to press in a city in July. **The pilot out of a storm belt is still coming in the autumn for nothing, with no instrument in this harbor that can call him and none that can stop him, and the yard's closing page says out loud that it is waiting for him and that it did not go and look at five miles of that coast this month.**
- **CARRIED — The glass storm.** Not used. Two written occurrences, two second-column lines in two hands on two pages that are not on top of each other, no cause in any volume, nothing in either that knows anything, and the delay on the call in the water still not given.
- **CARRIED — The delayed note.** Eleven logged occasions since the twenty-sixth of October and two of them with no call in them, **neither moved by anything in this batch and neither moved by a measurement.** The mole head's handbell went on Monday the third of July at about eleven and a person of this party could not say in which of two directions the answer came. **NO OCCASION WAS ENTERED, THE DELAY WAS NOT TAKEN, AND THE DELAY IS A HAND'S WIDTH OF A SECOND AND HAS NOT MOVED.** The answer inside the note is reserved to the Volume 15 midpoint.
- **CARRIED — The bell.** Fouled, no tongue on the frame, two parts of a chain in a lead plug where the tongue was, a frame cut through the casting on one side. **THE GOING OUT OF THE SECOND OF MAY IS DONE AND SPENT AND IS NOT REPEATED, NO SECOND EIGHT MARKS IS PAID, NO BOAT GOES OFF THE SOUTH END IN THIS BATCH, AND THE STANDING SENTENCE SAID IN A STREET ON THE THIRD OF MAY STANDS UNREVERSED.** **ITS NOTATION IS NOW IN A NAVAL FILE AND IS NOT ON A WALL IN THIS HARBOR AND CANNOT BE TAKEN DOWN.** Eight marks and a day of a boat owing since the twenty-third of May, unpaid, unargued, not paid in this batch.
- **CARRIED — The lead plug.** In a chest in a shed with a lock the yard owns, with the boy's writing on the lid. **ITS NUMBER WAS SAID ONCE, IN A ROOM, AND IS NOW WRITTEN NOWHERE AT ALL, AND THAT IS THE FIRST TIME SINCE THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF FEBRUARY THAT ANY PERSON HAS SAID IT TO ANY PERSON.** It is not a custody object, not one of the three items, and not connected to the plate, the Oren mark, the coast road, the worked panel or anything under this town.
- **CARRIED — Custody.** Three items, three numbers, digits not quoted, none returns, none is asked for, the municipal claim on the third stands and the yard will not make a request. **No paper states what a municipal claim does to a presumed Crown material and no clerk answered it in this batch.**
- **CARRIED — The concealment.** Hidden. The Oren mark is in no record, in no office, in no book and not in Kest's chart notation. **Nobody asked the boy and nobody asked the conservator.** The yard's refusal to make itself able to answer a question about its own bar figure, on the page on the twenty-ninth of June, is a refusal about a lead and a slate and is not a reference to anything else.
- **CARRIED — Neave Tarrant.** Nine winters, out since the twenty-eighth of February, not found, one line in the yard's book, and **this yard has not gone and has asked nobody anything**, and the line was recited on the fourth of July.
- **CARRIED — The fifth-seam plate.** Still in eleven feet, seen and not measured and not touched. **It is referred to once in this batch, in nine words, on the twenty-first, by a man who has just been told what a thing costs: *I am not going to say one word about the plate at the fifth seam.* The plan is not reopened and no chapter of this batch reached for it.** The mechanism remains **a change of politics in an office** and it has now produced one thing in another office's book: an end of the first of September on a mooring.
- **CARRIED — Labor, whole.** Four marks in a bag and thirty-one on the note and nothing in a tin. Eleven lines and forty-four marks in the wage column with the fifth of March the oldest, six envelopes on a bench and not one of them anywhere. Ten marks in and fourteen out in twenty-one days, and **no office in this harbor has paid this yard a mark since the eighth of May and none is going to.** Forty marks of claim standing whole. Forty marks to a conservator with no office, first after food, not reachable by the writ, not one mark of it paid. Eight shillings and tenpence to each of two women. Nineteen winters, in a naval file and in a letter and in a list. Eight marks and a day of a boat to a bellwright. A compressor's gland on the note at five marks, unpaid, useless, because the thing after the gland is the hose. No delivery hose, and a crew of Ashtide at the outer end of the second lay with forty fathoms of her own. The ninth seam out with an end entered at last. The north berth out. The third lay full. The tide gang four men short. A cistern charged five times in twenty-one days by people nobody pays, and eight times at no figure on the office's own page since the end of May. The fire party's hose dead. The light not on. The repair account empty since the middle of February. No boat of this office's own since the evening of the twenty-eighth of April. The *Kittiwake* on the hard. The hard at Ashtide. The *Sea Wren* at the pile. Eleven copies and a list of the eleven on a wall and a printed sheet over it. Two objects of nobody's in the open air on a mole's crown. Twenty marks of unsold iron and four hundred and forty pounds of old iron in a heap that may not be sold, with a bell-metal gun-port in it that was not looked at in this batch.

### Ruled

- **RULED — Captain Holt's posting after her defeat is NOT decided and is explicitly still open, and no chapter of this volume may name a posting before the defeat that follows it.** The defeat is in Movement 5. **What Chapter 130 forecloses on the page, in the yard's own hand, is what a posting may not be: no posting, appointment, commission, transport or instruction out of any city has put anybody past this harbor line, the outer marks and the bar of four hundred yards off them stand where the writ says they are and are not nearer, and this party is not going past them **this season** and has not tried and is not going to.** A Movement 5 writer must decide the posting on the page with a date, and may not make it a command in this harbor, an inspecting commission over a harbor authority, or anything that puts a person, a boat or a lead inside the closure of the notice.
- **RULED — The channel by which a disgraced officer reaches a Morrow Quay reeve is THE WRIT, and it is decided on the page, with a date, in Chapter 130.** **A man in a coat, of any office, disgraced or not, arrives as a stranger with a paper about the second season of the writ of the first of May, and the yard will read it, will answer it on the day it comes, and will not treat him as a friend or an enemy — because the writ's own last line says the second season is not this office's to give and will arrive out of a city from a man who has never been inside this harbor and the holder will be told when it arrives and not before.** The channel had been in a sheet on a bench since the first of May. **No new person, office or institution was created to carry it, and no later volume may invent a second channel.**
- **RULED — the first request for the harbor authority's chart is referred to with no date, on purpose.** Chapter 122 says the yard has been able to establish no date for it and has not invented one. A later chapter may supply a date **only if it can be derived from a chapter and not out of a file.**

### Open by design

- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the marked face.** Unidentified, unexplained, and not allowed to settle anything, and expressly not to be counted. A third time or not, the count goes on no wall and in no column.
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the figure on the bar of gravel.** A stranger's number against a stranger's number, printed in a city in July, and a woman in a shed said out loud that the printed sheet is wrong about one thing and refused to say which and would not let either of them write it down. **A yard that wrote it down would be asking a man with a slate about himself, and that man is Harl Dace, forty-eight, of the *Grey Widow*, and he has no wheel of his own in this harbor. THE MAN OF FIFTY-ONE WITH THE BAD WHEEL IS A DIFFERENT MAN AND THE TWO MAY NEVER BE MERGED.**
- **OPEN BY DESIGN — the first echo's witness.** Unnamed, unlocated, uninterviewed, and not in this batch.

### What Movement 4 inherits, in plain terms

**Movement 4 is the volume's midpoint and it is owed four things, and the fourth is the largest debt in the volume.**

1. **THE ONE NAMING OF THE CONDITION IS OWED TO MOVEMENT 4 AND IS NOT SPENT.** It is on the page four times as a bare fact with a reason against it and a count against the fourth, and for the first time in another person's hand, and nobody may read any chapter of Batch 0003 as having spent it. **Movement 4 is where Calder Marr gets a seam wrong in front of people who trusted him, and the naming belongs there and nowhere else.**
2. **THE FAULTREADER STAGE IS OWED TO MOVEMENT 4, late, badly, at a price, and a seam identifies difference and not truth.** No stage of any kind is taken in this batch and none may be taken until Movement 4. **No Faultreader, Bellwright, Keelwright, Storm-Reader, Chorus Diver or Openhand.**
3. **THE PHYSICAL JOIN IS OWED TO MOVEMENT 4, and it must be found by labor and not by an argument** — a place where a keel-line ran between two things that were never made to be joined, a material, a repair, a fouling, a figure, a witness who was not coerced and a joiner who was, and **it is not the compartment and the compartment must not be re-used, and it is not the coast road and the coast road must not be resolved.** **And it is not the shard of the tenth of June: that shard is on a mole's crown under a tarpaulin, worth nothing, with nine scallops accounted for neither time, and it has not moved and is not going to be moved for the sake of a chapter.**
4. **MARA VENN IS OWED A SCENE IN MOVEMENT 4 AND HER SENTENCE, and it ends her time on the crew.** **She is in no room in this batch, has been owed forty marks since the fifteenth of October, has not been paid one mark of it out of anything that has come into this yard since the eighth of May, has not been asked to be useful, is not an assistant, and her break with the Registry is Volume 4 and is a different event and the two may not be fused.** **A public record is the subject of Movement 4 and this batch has set it up and has not settled it — and the setup is that this yard has learned, in public, that the only thing that makes a wall worth reading is somebody else being able to be asked a question about it, and that there is nothing in this harbor that could answer.**
5. **ALSO CARRIED FORWARD AND NOT SPENT:** the autumn visit of a man of fifty-four with a different figure for the bar and no instrument to call him; Sella Brigg's about four hundred feet of warp and a crew of Ashtide who can raise it; a girl of twenty-two's June and forty marks of claim; the third column that this yard intends to keep and cannot yet fill and has now promised to hire for; the ninth seam's end of the first of September and what an office with uncontested instruments does next; the first paper out of a city, and the answer to it, and the second one that will come.

---

## Volume 03, Batch 0004 (Chapters 131–140) — as written

**Review artifact. Batch 0004 HAS BEEN REVIEWED AND REPAIRED.** A reviewer pass over Chapters 131–140, the four state files and this batch's own queued prompt returned **FIX** with **fourteen required repairs** and a list of prose and craft items. **No chapter was restarted, no scene was cut, no cost was dropped and no planned plot was altered**, and the batch is **30,890 words where it was 30,635**. The repairs are disposed of in full in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0004 review repairs*, which is the authority for them. **What moved, and every thread below is marked against the corrected text: the bag of Monday the tenth of July is six and not four and the day closes at nine out of thirteen; the *N Mondays* counter is the tenth, eleventh and twelfth and Chapter 129 is retitled *Nine Mondays, and a Wage*; the six conditions are read aloud on the eighth of July; the top of the flood moves forward across the batch; the string is three hundred and fifty-one feet; there are three thicknesses of fouling; the plate's second referral is thirteen words; the market wall's own two Monday references are Saturday; and the conservator has been in this harbor about a fortnight and not nine days.**

**Movement 4 is written and its turn has landed, and the volume's midpoint is paid: a physical join found by labor and in weather, with a hand lead's line and a slate and a hired man's flat iron, and not by an argument in a shed. Thirty thousand six hundred and eight words across ten chapters, Saturday the seventh to Wednesday the twenty-sixth of July.** A crevice four fathoms down on the second seam's own face, landward of the third seam's mark, with a scarph in it between two ends cut to each other, nine fastenings in about seven feet at a hand's breadth apart, three chisel blows on every head, a packing that is not pitch and not lime, and a chip of worked glass put into the packing on purpose. **A conservator of no office, in this harbor about a fortnight since about the thirtieth of June and not known to anybody in this yard, proves six things with her own calipers and cannot give a seventh, and the seventh is who.** A seam read for the first time by a man in the water, and a statement made in a market in eleven people's hearing that is wrong in three parts out of four, and fouling that takes it apart in two days. **The one naming Volume 03 is allowed is SPENT, IN CHAPTER 137, ON THE TWENTIETH OF JULY.** Nine places read out of four books in a press in the market office and laid out on a shed floor with a string: three hundred and fifty-one feet, a builder's day-book with a stone cut into the mole head, a fisher's own storm, a village of twenty-two children, a lay of this coast that is not on any chart. **The yard's own fourth return nailed up on a market wall with no scale and no price on it**, and a woman of no office disagreeing with the holder in the open air in forty people's hearing and her unsigned objection going onto the sheet. **A sentence said before anybody asked it that ends a conservator's time on the crew, and a boat's name said out loud off the outer marks that this party writes down and does not go and look at.** Every thread below is marked PAID, PART, CARRIED, RULED or OPEN BY DESIGN. Movement 5 is the volume's climax and what it inherits is set out at the end of this block in plain terms.

### Paid

- **PAID — THE PHYSICAL JOIN, found by labor, in weather, with a lead and a slate. (Ch 131, Saturday the eighth; Ch 132, Monday the tenth; Ch 136, Ch 137.)** A hand lead's line made in this yard's own net loft snags at about four fathoms with no weight in it; eleven minutes of hand work; a piece of metal about the length of a hand comes up with three chisel blows on its head. Four days later a hired man on a hirer's own account brings up a lump about the size of two fists out of a crevice where the mole's own stones come out and a hull's side comes in, and in the tallow of the lead, at about ten past four in the afternoon, a chip of green glass about the length of a thumbnail. **THE RETURN FALLS DUE ON THE FOURTEENTH AND IS MADE ON THE DAY. The join is checkable by any man who can get off a mole: a scarph of two ends cut to each other, a packing that is not stone, oakum, lime or pitch, nine fastenings in about seven feet at a hand's breadth apart, three blows a head with the third a different depth from the first two, the same three blows on all nine, a runnel of packing thinning seaward with the hard side seaward, and three thicknesses of fouling on the seaward face.** A boatwright of the second lay comes down for nothing because a stranger asked him at the top of the mole's steps, confirms the count and the spacing with his own rule, and says the spacing is not a spacing anybody on this coast works to and that he has not said a word about where it came from and does not know who. **IT IS NOT THE COMPARTMENT, IT IS NOT THE COAST ROAD, IT IS NOT THE SHARD OF THE TENTH OF JUNE — WHICH HAS NOT MOVED AND IS NOT GOING TO BE MOVED FOR THE SAKE OF A CHAPTER — IT IS NOT CONNECTED TO ANYTHING UNDER THIS TOWN, AND THE RETURN SAYS ALL OF THAT IN ONE LINE AND INSTRUCTS THAT NOBODY IS TO WRITE ANY TWO OF THOSE THINGS TOGETHER.**
- **PAID — A WITNESS WHO WAS NOT COERCED AND A JOINER WHO WAS, AS SHAPE AND NOT AS A NAME. (Ch 135, Ch 136.)** **The witness is the glass: a chip of worked green glass in the packing, the same material as the chip out of the tallow, put there on purpose at the moment the packing went in, with a conservator's own reason for saying so and not a fisherman's.** The coercion is in what the fouling says and not in what anybody says: the joint was made over three inches of fouling, from the seaward side, from a boat that was lying there in a working harbor at its leisure, and a man packing a joint does not pick up a piece of glass and put it in the seam. **AND THE RECORD PROVES THE SHAPE OF THE SYSTEM AND NOT THE NAME OF ANYBODY WHO BUILT IT: the finding, the fouling, the direction, the spacing and the three blows are all checkable, and WHO IS NOT, and the reason is in a trade's own mouth — a joint is made by a body and two hands and a decision, a decision is not a mark, and a body does not leave anything on a thing that a trade can read.**
- **PAID — THE VESSELS ARE NOT A SCATTER AND NOT A SINGLE THING: THEY STAND IN A CHAIN THAT NINE COMMUNITIES KEPT, AND THE MEMORIES IN THEM ARE CIVIC RECORDS. (Ch 138, Friday the twenty-first.)** **A string with two pins in it laid along the foot of that face comes to three hundred and fifty-one feet with nothing in its way, and the six joints sit on it at the same place.** The six places are the six places in six entries of a builder's day-book of the port of Ashtide that has been in the market office press since a year nobody names, and the builder was paid by the foot and wrote down where he worked, and his name is cut into a stone at the mole head. **A weir-book, with nine pages in a fisher's own hand and a night in a westerly and eleven hundred and forty fish and a lost boat and a repair four years later, and the third joint a foot and four inches under that weir's own sill stone. A poor-book, with twenty-two children put out of a village and nine of them named, and the village not on this coast and not on any chart anybody in this harbor has seen. A tide-gang's step book, whose column for the foot of this mole's face stops, and after it the name of a sand and shell lay of this coast that is not on any chart in this harbor.** **THE YARD DECLINES THE WORD. Nine places is nine places, a line is a line, and the word *chain*, said in a market on the eighteenth by a man who was wrong in three parts out of the same sentence, is disowned in the yard's own book. THE SIX FASTENINGS ARE NOT A MARK OF NINE JOINED LINES, THE NINE PLACES ARE NOT THE NINE HARBORS, AND THE TWO HALVES OF THE FINDING ARE NOT JOINED: the account is about the sea, the finding is about the glass, and neither of them is a reason to believe the other.**
- **PAID — THE FAULTREADER STAGE, TAKEN LATE, BADLY, AND AT A PRICE. (Ch 136, Ch 137.)** **TAKEN WITH NO CEREMONY, BY A MAN IN THE WATER, AND NOT NAMED AS A STAGE BY ANYBODY.** What it gains is stated on the page and is exactly one thing: **the perception of incompatible joins — that the two cuts in a joint can be of different ages, and that the difference between a cut made once and a cut made on top of a cut that was there first comes into a hand without a picture and without a sound.** **IT IDENTIFIES DIFFERENCE AND NOT TRUTH: it told him there were mendings under mendings and it told him nothing whatever about when, in what order, or by whose hand, AND HE SUPPLIED ALL THREE.** What it costs: **concentration a paying job needs — a day of a woman's own account bought at seventeen and half of it done because the man in the water could not say whether the basket had gone up four times or three, and the boat off the face two hours and a quarter before the flood; conflicting aftertaste — a bad day on all three days he was in the water in company, and ordinary talk in a shed going on as more than one talk at once, and an answer given a quarter of a second late; and the fact that a seam does not tell a man what happened.** **NO BELLWRIGHT, KEELWRIGHT, STORM-READER, CHORUS DIVER OR OPENHAND STAGE IS TAKEN AND NONE MAY BE.**
- **PAID — THE WRONG, IN FRONT OF PEOPLE WHO TRUSTED HIM, IN A WAY THE PROSE DOES NOT EXPLAIN AWAY. (Ch 136, said; Ch 137, disproved and paid for.)** **It is said at the head of this dock at about half past three on Tuesday the eighteenth, in a market, in about eleven people's hearing, and it is not cured, not forgiven, not explained by anything, and not paid off quietly.** It is disproved **by labor and not by a seam and not by an argument**: fouling goes on in order, and the two thinnest readings are the two at the root end, which is the north end, and he had told eleven people the newest was the first. **And it is not one wrong thing but three: the order, the single time, and the single set of hands.** It lands on **Tam Slee, a hired diver of another boat on the writ, in the water on his own account and not paid, who said he went down because he was told to and is not doing it because of Tuesday; on Edric Pringle, who paid twelve marks at the mole for half a day and came up the hill to say the tide was there at seven and at two and the boat came off the face because the man in the water lost the count, and who is owed eight shillings and tenpence and did not ask; and on Nessa Pike, who did not stop him and wrote it down and gave the reason she did not stop him, which is that a man saying his own trade in the open air is not doing wrong and that a shipwright who contradicts a man in a market is a person who has decided that a man's mouth is a thing to be managed.**
- **PAID — THE ONE NAMING OF THE CONDITION. (Ch 137, Thursday the twentieth, at about a quarter past nine, in the shed.)** **SPENT, ONCE, IN THIS CHAPTER, IN THE YARD'S OWN BOOK, IN THE HOLDER'S OWN LEFT HAND, IN THE SECOND COLUMN, WITH THE WORD *A TIDE-SICKNESS* AND WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE.** It breaks his own line of the second of June. The reason against it is on the page: a man who can lose a quarter of a second out of an ordinary conversation in a shed is a man whose account of a seam under four fathoms is a thing that can be asked about, and the party has decided it can be asked about in this book, on this page, on this date, by anybody who comes up this hill, and the only difference between that and a man with a seal is that it can be argued with. **NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 03 AFTER CHAPTER 137 MAY PUT THE WORD IN AGAINST HIM. The four lines above it are still nameless, are not struck out, and a request to strike one out is refused.**
- **PAID — THE COUNT OF FOUR, owed to this batch by the last one. (Ch 133, Tuesday the eleventh, in the first column, in the shipwright's own hand.)** **Four dates, four weathers, no name against it, and her reason for the absence of a name.** A count is a thing this yard can check; the second column is a page of things it cannot.
- **PAID — THE FOURTH RETURN, MADE ON THE DAY, AND THE TURN. (Ch 134, Friday the fourteenth; Ch 138, Saturday the twenty-second.)** One sheet, unshortened, with place, hour, keeper and limits, the disavowal line in full, four lines of second column, a copy into the harbor authority's ledger in the clerk's own hand, up the coast at about eight in a sealed jacket. **AND THE TURN IS THAT THE YARD NAILS IT UP ON A MARKET WALL ITSELF, over a rival's better printed sheet, with no scale on it and no price on it and nowhere to write a letter to, and a shilling changes hands at the foot of the dock for the rival's and not for the yard's.** The fleet's formation is legible and it is legible as a public fact and the yard cannot unread it and cannot make it private again, and a printer in a city has been told three lines in advance in the ordinary post at no cost that it is coming and that he is to print what it says and not what it is about, and he cannot be stopped. **Chapter 130's sentence — the only thing that makes a wall worth reading is somebody else being able to be asked a question about it, and there is nothing in this harbor that could answer — is answered by an event and not by an argument, and what answers it is a Crown attachment on a party's own writ and a market office press that is open to anybody on a Tuesday and a Friday morning.**
- **PAID — MARA VENN'S PROVING, AND HER SENTENCE, AND HER TIME ON THE CREW. (Ch 135; Ch 138, the public disagreement; Ch 139, the sentence.)** **SIX THINGS SHE CAN PROVE AND ONE SHE CANNOT, and the one is who.** She prices herself out loud before she does anything, sets the conditions of her finding in words she has chosen, enters it unsigned with the reason on the page, and says that a person of no office is six marks and two days on every coast and does not come down. **THE DISAGREEMENT IS IN PUBLIC AND IN THE OPEN AIR AND IN FORTY PEOPLE'S HEARING ON A MARKET WALL, and it is about whether to publish a dangerous witness, and it is NOT settled by him giving her the evidence: he gives her the whole of it — the day-book, the weir, the poor-book, the step book, nine places, three hundred and fifty-one feet — and she calls it the best thing anybody has found out in this harbor in five months and then says it is worth nothing to her, because she is not being paid for it, and a thing a person is given because she is owed money is not a gift and not a trade and not a finding.** Her objection goes onto the sheet unsigned with the reason, and his answer goes on beside it in fifteen words: both, and I will not be able to tell you which was right in October. **HER SENTENCE IS SAID ON THE TWENTY-THIRD, AT THE TOP OF THE MOLE'S STEPS, IN HER OWN WORDS, BEFORE ANYBODY ASKS HER, AND SHE HAS WRITTEN IT OUT HERSELF THE NIGHT BEFORE AND HANDS IT OVER: a yard that cannot pay me asks me whether I can be useful; I have been useful four times since the twenty-seventh of January, not asked once, not paid once, and the fifth time I did the work anyway and I am not going to do that again; six marks and two days is my price and it does not come down, and I would rather be owed at the rate I am owed than be had at the rate I am had; and a trade that says yes to nothing is not a trade. IT IS IN THE FIRST COLUMN AND NOBODY DISPUTES A WORD OF IT.** She takes her own calipers off the bench and does not leave the harbor and is in Chapter 140.
- **PAID — CALDER'S LESSON, IN HIS OWN MOUTH, ON THE PAGE. (Ch 139.)** **A conservator I cannot pay is a conservator I have to keep asking, and asking her to be useful is asking her to be cheap, and I have been doing the second thing since the twenty-seventh of January and calling it the first, and she has let me do it, and the reason she has let me do it is that she would rather be useful than be forty marks in front of a man who cannot pay her, and that was a kindness and I have taken it for eight months and called it a trade.** And the standing arrangement that replaces it: the party will keep asking and will not ask her to be useful, and a conservator of no office in Morrow Quay is to be found on a Friday morning at about ten in a room open to anybody, **and the party did not arrange that and would not dare to.**
- **PAID — THE REEVE SPENDS AGAIN AND THE PRICE LANDS ON SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT THE YARD, AND NEITHER MAN GETS WHAT HE WANTS.
